\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}(T),\tilde{x}^{i,i}(T))}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\sqrt{\mathbb{P}{({\sup_{k\in\left\{0..2^{i}\right\}}\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},x_{0})}>\frac{2^{i/2}L_{1}}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}})}}\cdot{(\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}(T),x_{0})}^{2}}\right]}+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(\tilde{x}^{i,i}(T),x_{0})}^{2}}\right]})}

(A.10)

From Lemma
27
, and our assumed bound on
T
,

\displaystyle\sqrt{\mathbb{P}{({\sup_{k\in\left\{0...2^{i}\right\}}\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},x_{0})}\geq\frac{2^{i/2}L_{1}}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}})}}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}}{L_{1}^{2}2^{i}}\exp{(1+8TL_{\beta}^{\prime}+2TL_{R}d+2T\delta^{i}L_{R}L_{0}^{2})}{(3Td+8T^{2}L_{0}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}{(Td+T^{2}L_{0}^{2})}}{L_{1}^{2}}\cdot{2^{4-i}}

From Lemma
26
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}(T),x_{0})}^{2}}\right]\leq

\displaystyle\exp{(1+8TL_{\beta}^{\prime}+TL_{R}d+T^{2}L_{R}L_{0}^{2})}\cdot{(Td+8T^{2}L_{0}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4{(Td+8T^{2}L_{0}^{2})}

The same upper bound also applies to
\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(\tilde{x}^{i,i}(T),x_{0})}^{2}}\right]
. Plugging into (
A.10
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}(T),\tilde{x}^{i,i}(T))}}\right]\leq 2^{6-i}\frac{{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}{(Td+T^{2}L_{0}^{2})}^{3/2}}{L_{1}^{2}}

By identical steps, we can also upper bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x^{i+1}(T),\tilde{x}^{i+1,i}(T))}}\right]\leq 2^{6-i}\frac{{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}{(Td+T^{2}L_{0}^{2})}^{3/2}}{L_{1}^{2}}

Plugging into (
A.9
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}(T),x^{i+1}(T))}}\right]\leq

\displaystyle 2^{7-i/2}T^{3/2}{(T^{3}L_{R}^{2}L_{1}^{6}+L_{R}^{2}d^{3}+T{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}L_{1}^{2}+{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}d)}^{1/2}+2^{7-i}\frac{{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}{(Td+T^{2}L_{0}^{2})}^{3/2}}{L_{1}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2^{8-i/2}{(T^{2}L_{1}^{2}+T^{2}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}+T^{3/2}{(d^{3/2}{(L_{R}+{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}/{L_{1}^{2}})}+L_{\beta}^{\prime}\sqrt{d})})}

Summing over
i
, we can bound, for any integers
0\leq\ell
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x^{0}(T),x^{\ell^{\prime}}(T))}}\right]\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{\ell}2^{8-i/2}{(T^{2}L_{1}^{2}+T^{2}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}+T^{3/2}{(d^{3/2}{(L_{R}+{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}/{L_{1}^{2}})}+L_{\beta}^{\prime}\sqrt{d})})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2^{9}{(T^{2}L_{1}^{2}+T^{2}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}+T^{3/2}{(d^{3/2}{(L_{R}+{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}/{L_{1}^{2}})}+L_{\beta}^{\prime}\sqrt{d})})}

Since the above holds for all
\ell
, by dominated convergence together with Lemma
1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x^{0}(T),x(T))}}\right]\leq 2^{9}{(T^{2}L_{1}^{2}+T^{2}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}+T^{3/2}{(d^{3/2}{(L_{R}+{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}/{L_{1}^{2}})}+L_{\beta}^{\prime}\sqrt{d})})}

∎

A.4
Proof of Theorem
1

Proof of Theorem
1
.

Let us define

\displaystyle r_{0}:=64{(\mathcal{R}+\frac{\sqrt{L_{R}}d}{m}+\sqrt{\frac{d}{m}}{(\log{({\frac{L_{R}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{4}d^{2}}{m^{5}}+L_{\beta}^{\prime}\mathcal{R}^{2}})})})}

Let
\delta\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
be some fixed stepsize satisfying

\displaystyle\delta\leq

\displaystyle\min\Bigg\{\frac{m}{32{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}},\frac{1}{16L_{R}d},\frac{d}{4m}

\displaystyle\frac{m}{32\sqrt{L_{R}}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}r_{0}},\sqrt{\frac{m^{3}}{2^{22}dL_{R}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{4}\log^{2}K}},\sqrt{\frac{m^{3}}{2^{22}dL_{R}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{4}\log^{2}{(dL_{R}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{4}/m^{3})}}}

\displaystyle\frac{d}{16m\sqrt{L_{R}}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}r_{0}},\sqrt{\frac{d}{2^{22}mL_{R}\log^{2}K}},\sqrt{\frac{d}{2^{22}mL_{R}\log^{2}{(mL_{R}/d)}}}\Bigg\}

(A.11)

and let
r\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
be a radius given by

\displaystyle r:=r_{0}+64\sqrt{\frac{d}{m}}\log{(\frac{K}{\delta})}

Let
A_{k}
denote the event
\left\{\max_{i\leq k}\mathrm{d}{(x_{i},y_{i})}\right\}\leq r
.

Step 1: Tail Bound:

By Lemma
34
and Lemma
29
and Lemma
28
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{A_{k}^{c}\right\}}}\right]=\mathbb{P}{({A_{k}^{c}})}\leq 32k\delta m\exp{(\frac{m\mathcal{R}^{2}}{d}+\frac{2L_{R}d}{m}-\frac{mr^{2}}{32d})}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}}\right]\leq\frac{2^{12}L_{R}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{4}d^{2}}{m^{6}}+\frac{9{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}\mathcal{R}^{2}}{m}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(y_{k},x^{*})}^{2}}\right]\leq\frac{2^{12}L_{R}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{4}d^{2}}{m^{6}}+\frac{9{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}\mathcal{R}^{2}}{m}

By our definition of
r
and applying Cauchy Schwarz, we verify that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{A_{K}^{c}\right\}}\mathrm{d}{(y_{K},x_{K})}}\right]\leq\sqrt{\delta}

(A.12)

Note that Lemma
34
requires
\delta\leq\min\left\{\frac{m}{16{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}{\sqrt{L_{R}}r}},\frac{d}{4m{\sqrt{L_{R}}r}}\right\}
. This bound follows from (
A.11
), using Lemma
17
, together with some algebra.

Step 1: One-step analysis:

We will bound
\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{A_{k+1}\right\}}\mathrm{d}{(y_{k+1},{x}_{k+1})}}\right]
in terms of
\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{A_{k}\right\}}\mathrm{d}{(y_{k},{x}_{k})}}\right]
. Consider a fixed but arbitrary
k
. We define a useful intermediate variable

\displaystyle\bar{x}_{k+1}:=\Phi{(\delta,x_{k},{E^{k}}^{x},\beta,\mathbf{B}_{k}^{x})}

where
{E^{k}}^{x}
is an orthonormal basis at
x_{k}
. In words,
\bar{x}_{k+1}
is the result of a
\delta
-time exact diffusion, starting at
x_{k}
. From Lemma
3
, there exists a coupling such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{A_{k}\right\}}f{(\mathrm{d}{(y_{k+1},\bar{x}_{k+1})})}}\right]\leq e^{-\alpha\delta}f{(\mathrm{d}{(y_{k},x_{k})})}

(A.13)

where
f
is a Lyapunov function satisfying
f(r)\geq\frac{1}{2}\exp{(-{(q+L_{\Ric})}\mathcal{R}^{2}/2)}r
and
{\left|f^{\prime}(r)\right|}\leq 1
and
\alpha:=\min\left\{\frac{m}{16},\frac{1}{2\mathcal{R}^{2}}\right\}\cdot\exp{(-\frac{1}{2}{(q+L_{\Ric})}\mathcal{R}^{2})}
are as defined in Lemma
3
.

Next, we will bound
\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{A_{k+1}\right\}}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k+1},\bar{x}_{k+1})}}\right]
. We would like to apply Lemma
4
with
L_{1}=L_{\beta}^{\prime}r
. We verify that under the event
A_{k}
,
\beta(x_{k})
is indeed bounded by
L_{1}
. Lemma
4
requires that
\delta
be upper bounded by a few quantities, most of these are immediately satisfied by our definition of
\delta
in (
A.11
), but the assumption that
\delta\leq\frac{1}{16\sqrt{L_{R}}L_{1}}=\frac{1}{16\sqrt{L_{R}}L_{\beta}^{\prime}r}
is slightly tricky, because our definition of
r
itself depends on
\delta
; we verify that this bound indeed holds by using Lemma
17
.

Lemma
4
thus guarantees that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{A_{k}\right\}}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k+1},\bar{x}_{k+1})}}\right]\leq

\displaystyle 2^{9}{(\delta^{2}L_{1}^{2}+\delta^{2}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}+\delta^{3/2}{(d^{3/2}{(L_{R}+{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}/{L_{1}^{2}})}+L_{\beta}^{\prime}\sqrt{d})})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2^{10}{(\delta^{2}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}{(1+r^{2})}+\delta^{3/2}{(d^{3/2}{(L_{R}+1/{r^{2}})}+L_{\beta}^{\prime}\sqrt{d})})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tilde{O}{(\delta^{3/2})}

(A.14)

where
\tilde{O}
hides polynomial dependency on
L_{\beta}^{\prime},d,L_{R},\mathcal{R},\frac{1}{m},\log K
.. Combining, (
A.13
) and (
A.14
), and using triangle inequality, together with the fact that
A_{k+1}\subset A_{k}
, and the fact that
{\left|f^{\prime}\right|}\leq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{A_{k+1}\right\}}f{(\mathrm{d}{(y_{k+1},x_{k+1})})}}\right]\leq e^{-\alpha\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{A_{k}\right\}}f{(\mathrm{d}{(y_{k},x_{k})})}}\right]+\tilde{O}{(\delta^{3/2})}

Applying the above recursively, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{A_{K}\right\}}f{(\mathrm{d}{(y_{K},x_{K})})}}\right]\leq e^{-\alpha K\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[{f{(\mathrm{d}{(y_{0},x_{0})})}}\right]+\frac{1}{\alpha}\cdot\tilde{O}{(\delta^{1/2})}

(A.15)

Combining (
A.12
) and (
A.15
), we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{f{(\mathrm{d}{(y_{K},x_{K})})}}\right]\leq e^{-\alpha K\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[{f{(\mathrm{d}{(y_{0},x_{0})})}}\right]+\frac{1}{\alpha}\cdot\tilde{O}{(\delta^{1/2})}

Using the fact that
\frac{1}{2}\exp{(-{(q+L_{\Ric})}\mathcal{R}^{2}/2)}r\leq f(r)\leq r
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(y_{K},x_{K})}}\right]\leq

\displaystyle e^{-\alpha K\delta+{(q+L_{Ric})}\mathcal{R}^{2}/2}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(y_{0},x_{0})}}\right]+\frac{\exp{({(q+L_{Ric})}\mathcal{R}^{2}/2)}}{\alpha}\cdot\tilde{O}{(\delta^{1/2})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{-\alpha K\delta+{(q+L_{Ric})}\mathcal{R}^{2}/2}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(y_{0},x_{0})}}\right]+\exp{({(q+L_{Ric})}\mathcal{R}^{2})}\cdot\tilde{O}{(\delta^{1/2})}

where
\tilde{O}
hides polynomial dependency on
L_{\beta}^{\prime},d,L_{R},\mathcal{R},\frac{1}{m},\log K
.

Finally, the constant
{\mathcal{C}}_{0}
in the theorem statement is simply 1 over the right side of (
A.11
).
∎

Appendix B
Distance Contraction under Kendall Cranston Coupling

In this section, we prove Lemma
3
, which is the main tool for proving mixing of manifold diffusion processes under the distant dissipativity assumption. We note again that the proof is entirely based on existing results, and is only included for completeness.

B.1
The Kendall Cranston Coupling

Lemma 9
.

Let
T\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
be some fixed time. Assume that there is are constants
L_{\beta},L_{\beta}^{\prime}
such that for all
x,y\in M
,
{\left\|\beta(x)\right\|}\leq L_{\beta}
and
{\left\|\beta(x)-\Gamma_{y}^{x}\beta(y)\right\|}\leq L_{\beta}^{\prime}{\left\|x-y\right\|}
. Let
i
be some integer satisfying

i\geq\max\left\{\log_{2}{(32T\sqrt{L_{R}}L_{\beta})},\log_{2}{(32Td)},\log_{2}{(32L_{\beta}T)}\right\}
.

Let
\kappa(r):=\frac{1}{r^{2}}\sup_{\mathrm{d}{(x,y)}=r}\bm{\left\langle}\Gamma_{y}^{x}\beta(y)-\beta(x),\Exp_{x}^{-1}(y)\bm{}
.

Let
x,y\in M
and let
E^{x}
be an arbitrary orthonormal basis of
T_{x}M
and let
E^{y}
be an arbitrary orthonormal basis of
T_{y}
. let
x^{i}(t):=\overline{\Phi}(t;x,E^{x},\beta,\mathbf{B}^{x},i)
and
y^{i}(t):=\overline{\Phi}(t;y,E^{y},\beta,\mathbf{B}^{y},i)
where
\mathbf{B}^{x}
and
\mathbf{B}^{y}
are standard Brownian motion in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and where
\overline{\Phi}
is as defined in (
3.3
).

For any
\varepsilon
, there exists a coupling between
\mathbf{B}^{x}
and
\mathbf{B}^{y}
, and Brownian motion
\mathbf{W}^{i}
over
\mathbb{R}
, such that for all
k\in\left\{0...2^{i}\right\}
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k+1},y^{i}_{k+1})}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle{(1+\delta^{i}{(2\kappa{(\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})})}+L_{Ric})})}\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}>\varepsilon\right\}}{(4\delta^{i}-4\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}{(\mathbf{W}^{i}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{W}^{i}{(k\delta^{i})})})}

\displaystyle\quad+\tau^{i}_{k}

where
\tau^{i}_{k}
satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{{\mathcal{F}}_{k}}}\left[{{\left|\tau^{i}_{k}\right|}}\right]\leq{\mathcal{C}}_{1}{\delta^{i}}^{3/2}{(1+L_{\beta}^{4})}{(1+\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}^{2})}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{{\mathcal{F}}_{k}}}\left[{{{\tau}^{i}_{k}}^{2}}\right]\leq{\mathcal{C}}_{1}{{(1+\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}^{4})}{\delta^{i}}^{2}}

where
{\mathcal{C}}_{1}
is a constant depending on
L_{R},L_{R}^{\prime},d,T
.

Proof.

We set up some notation: throughout this proof, consider a fixed
i
. Recall that
\delta^{i}:=T/2^{i}
, and assume
i
is large enough such that
\delta^{i}\leq\frac{1}{32\sqrt{L_{R}}L_{\beta}}
. Let
x^{i}_{k}
be as defined in (
3.1
) so that
x^{i}_{k}=x^{i}(k\delta^{i})
. Let us also define
K:=2^{i}
, so that
T=K\delta^{i}
.

Step 1: defining the coupling

By definition, for any
k\in\left\{0...K\right\}
,

\displaystyle x^{i}_{k+1}:=\Exp_{x^{i}_{k}}{(\delta^{i}\beta{(x^{i}_{k})}+{{(\mathbf{B}^{x}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}^{x}{(k\delta^{i})})}}\circ E^{i}_{k})}

\displaystyle y^{i}_{k+1}:=\Exp_{y^{i}_{k}}{(\delta^{i}\beta{(y^{i}_{k})}+{{(\mathbf{B}^{y}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}^{y}{(k\delta^{i})})}}\circ\tilde{E}^{i}_{k})}

Let
\gamma^{i}_{k}:[0,1]\to M
denote a minimizing geodesic from
x^{i}_{k}
to
y^{i}_{k}
.

Let
F^{i}_{k}
be an orthonormal basis at
T_{y^{i}_{k}}M
, obtained from the parallel transport of
E^{i}_{k}
along
\gamma^{i}_{k}
, i.e. for all
j=1...d
,

\displaystyle F^{i,j}_{k}=\Gamma_{\gamma^{i}_{k}}E^{i,j}_{k}

Let us define
\mathbf{M}^{i}_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
as matrix whose
a,b
entry is

\displaystyle{\left[\mathbf{M}^{i}_{k}\right]}_{a,b}=\bm{\left\langle}F^{i,a}_{k},\tilde{E}^{i,b}_{k}\bm{}

one can verify that
\mathbf{M}^{i}_{k}
is an orthogonal matrix, and that for all
\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\mathbf{v}\circ F^{i}_{k}=\mathbf{M}\mathbf{v}\circ\tilde{E}^{i}_{k}
.

Let us define
\bar{\bm{\nu}}^{i}_{k}
denote the unique coordinates of
\frac{{\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(1)}{{\left\|{\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(1)\right\|}}
wrt
F^{i}_{k}
(equivalently the coordinates of
\frac{{\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(0)}{{\left\|{\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(0)\right\|}}
wrt
E^{i}_{k}
). We define
\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k}:={\mathbbm{1}\left\{\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}>\varepsilon\right\}}\bar{\bm{\nu}}^{i}_{k}
.

We now define a coupling between
\mathbf{B}^{x}(t)
and
\mathbf{B}^{y}(t)
as follows:

\displaystyle\mathbf{B}^{y}(t):=\int_{0}^{T}{\mathbbm{1}\left\{t\in[k\delta^{i},(k+1)\delta^{i})\right\}}\mathbf{M}^{i}_{k}{(I-2\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k}{\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k}}^{T})}d\mathbf{B}^{x}(t)

For this to be a valid coupling, it suffices to verify that

\int_{0}^{T}{\mathbbm{1}\left\{t\in[k\delta^{i},(k+1)\delta^{i})\right\}}\mathbf{M}^{i}_{k}{(I-2\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k}{\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k}}^{T})}d\mathbf{B}^{x}(t)
is indeed a standard Brownian motion. This can be done by verifying that the definition satisfies Levy’s characterization of Brownian motion. We omit the proof, but highlight two important facts: 1.
\int_{0}^{T}{\mathbbm{1}\left\{t\in[k\delta^{i},(k+1)\delta^{i})\right\}}\mathbf{M}^{i}_{k}{(I-2\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k}{\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k}}^{T})}
is adapted to the natural filtration of
\mathbf{B}^{x}(t)
, and 2.
\mathbf{M}^{i}_{k}{(I-2\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k}{\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k}}^{T})}
is an orthogonal matrix. We have thus defined a coupling between
\mathbf{B}^{x}
and
\mathbf{B}^{y}
, and consequently, a coupling between
x^{i}(t)
and
y^{i}(t)
for all
t
.

Step 2: Applying Lemma
64

Having defined a coupling between
x^{i}_{k}
and
y^{i}_{k}
, we bound
\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{K},y^{i}_{K})}^{2}}\right]
for
K:=T/\delta^{i}=2^{i}
by applying Lemma
64
, with
x=x^{i}_{k}
,
y=y^{i}_{k}
,
u=\delta^{i}\beta{(x^{i}_{k})}+{{(\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})})}}\circ E^{i}_{k}
,
v=\delta^{i}\beta{(y^{i}_{k})}+{{(\tilde{\mathbf{B}}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\tilde{\mathbf{B}}{(k\delta^{i})})}}\circ\tilde{E}^{i}_{k}
and
\gamma:=\gamma^{i}_{k}
.

Following the notation in Lemma
64
, let
u(t)
and
v(t)
be the parallel transport of
u
and
v
along
\gamma(t)
. We verify that
u(s)=\delta^{i}\Gamma_{x^{i}_{k}}^{\gamma^{i}_{k}(s)}\beta(x^{i}_{k})+{(\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})})}\circ\Gamma_{x^{i}_{k}}^{\gamma^{i}_{k}(s)}E^{i}_{k}
and that

\displaystyle v(s)=

\displaystyle\delta^{i}\Gamma_{y^{i}_{k}}^{\gamma^{i}_{k}(s)}\beta(y^{i}_{k})+\mathbf{M}^{i}_{k}{(I-2\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k}{\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k}}^{T})}{(\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})})}\circ\Gamma_{x^{i}_{k}}^{\gamma^{i}_{k}(s)}\tilde{E}^{i}_{k}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta^{i}\Gamma_{y^{i}_{k}}^{\gamma^{i}_{k}(s)}\beta(y^{i}_{k})+{(I-2\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k}{\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k}}^{T})}{(\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})})}\circ\Gamma_{y^{i}_{k}}^{\gamma^{i}_{k}(s)}F^{i}_{k}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta^{i}\Gamma_{y^{i}_{k}}^{\gamma^{i}_{k}(s)}\beta(y^{i}_{k})+{(I-2\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k}{\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k}}^{T})}{(\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})})}\circ\Gamma_{x^{i}_{k}}^{\gamma^{i}_{k}(s)}E^{i}_{k}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta^{i}\Gamma_{y^{i}_{k}}^{\gamma^{i}_{k}(s)}\beta(y^{i}_{k})+{(\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})})}\circ\Gamma_{x^{i}_{k}}^{\gamma^{i}_{k}(s)}E^{i}_{k}

\displaystyle\quad-2\bm{\left\langle}\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k},\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})}\bm{}\frac{{\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(s)}{{\left\|{\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(s)\right\|}}

where the second equality is by definition of
\mathbf{M}^{i}_{k}
, the third equality is by definition of
F^{i}_{k}
, the fourth equality is by definition of
\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k}
and the fact that
\gamma^{i}_{k}
is a geodesic. It is convenient subsequently to note the following:

\displaystyle v(s)-u(s)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta^{i}{(\Gamma_{y^{i}_{k}}^{\gamma^{i}_{k}(s)}\beta(y^{i}_{k})-\Gamma_{x^{i}_{k}}^{\gamma^{i}_{k}(s)}\beta(x^{i}_{k}))}-2\bm{\left\langle}\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k},\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})}\bm{}\frac{{\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(s)}{{\left\|{\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(s)\right\|}}

and

\displaystyle(1-s)u(s)+sv(s)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1-s)\delta^{i}\Gamma_{x^{i}_{k}}^{\gamma^{i}_{k}(s)}\beta(x^{i}_{k})+s\delta^{i}\Gamma_{y^{i}_{k}}^{\gamma^{i}_{k}(s)}\beta(y^{i}_{k})

\displaystyle\quad+{(\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})})}\circ\Gamma_{x^{i}_{k}}^{\gamma^{i}_{k}(s)}E^{i}_{k}

\displaystyle\quad-2s\bm{\left\langle}\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k},\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})}\bm{}\frac{{\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(s)}{{\left\|{\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(s)\right\|}}

Step 3: Reorganizing Lemma
64

With
u,v
as defined above, Lemma
64
implies that

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k+1},y^{i}_{k+1})}^{2}-\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\bm{\left\langle}{\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(0),v(0)-u(0)\bm{}+{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad-2\int_{0}^{1}\bm{\left\langle}R{({\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(s),(1-s)u(s)+sv(s))}(1-s)u(s)+sv(s),{\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(s)\bm{}ds

\displaystyle\quad+{(2{\mathcal{C}}^{2}e^{{\mathcal{C}}}+18{\mathcal{C}}^{4}e^{2{\mathcal{C}}})}{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|}^{2}+{(18{\mathcal{C}}^{4}e^{2{\mathcal{C}}}+4{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+4{\mathcal{C}}^{2}e^{2{\mathcal{C}}}\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|}

(B.1)

where
{\mathcal{C}}:=\sqrt{L_{R}}{({\left\|u\right\|}+{\left\|v\right\|})}
and
{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}:=L_{R}^{\prime}{({\left\|u\right\|}+{\left\|v\right\|})}^{3}
.

Below, we bound each of the terms above

\displaystyle 2\bm{\left\langle}{\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(0),v(0)-u(0)\bm{}

\displaystyle=2\delta^{i}\bm{\left\langle}{\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(0),\Gamma_{y^{i}_{k}}^{x^{i}_{k}}\beta(y^{i}_{k})-\beta(x^{i}_{k})\bm{}-4{\left\|{\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(0)\right\|}\bm{\left\langle}\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k},\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})}\bm{}

\displaystyle{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 4\bm{\left\langle}\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k},\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})}\bm{}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{{\delta^{i}}^{2}L_{\beta}^{2}+4\delta^{i}L_{\beta}\bm{\left\langle}\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k},\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})}\bm{}}_{\tau^{i}_{k,1}}

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\bm{\left\langle}R{({\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(s),(1-s)u(s)+sv(s))}(1-s)u(s)+sv(s),{\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(s)\bm{}ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\bm{\left\langle}R{({\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(s),{(\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})})}\circ\Gamma_{x^{i}_{k}}^{\gamma^{i}_{k}(s)}E^{i}_{k})}{(\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})})}\circ\Gamma_{x^{i}_{k}}^{\gamma^{i}_{k}(s)}E^{i}_{k},{\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(s)\bm{}ds

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{4\bm{\left\langle}\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k},\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})}\bm{}^{2}}{{\left\|{\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(0)\right\|}^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}s^{2}\underbrace{\bm{\left\langle}R{({\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(s),{\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(s))}{\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(s),{\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(s)\bm{}}_{=0\text{ as $R(u,u)=0$ for all $u$}}ds

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{{\delta^{i}}^{2}L_{R}\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}^{2}L_{\beta}^{2}+4\delta^{i}L_{R}\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}^{2}L_{\beta}{\left\|\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})}\right\|}_{2}}_{\tau^{i}_{k,2}}

Finally, we will take the remaining terms, and denote them by

\displaystyle\tau^{i}_{k,3}:=

\displaystyle{(2{\mathcal{C}}^{2}e^{{\mathcal{C}}}+18{\mathcal{C}}^{4}e^{2{\mathcal{C}}})}{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+{(18{\mathcal{C}}^{4}e^{2{\mathcal{C}}}+4{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}^{2}+4{\mathcal{C}}^{2}e^{2{\mathcal{C}}}\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|}

We claim that under our assumption on
i
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{{\mathcal{F}}_{k}}}\left[{{\left|\tau^{i}_{k,1}+\tau^{i}_{k,2}+\tau^{i}_{k,3}\right|}}\right]=O{({\delta^{i}}^{3/2}{(1+L_{\beta}^{4})}{(1+\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}^{2})})}

where
O()
hides dependencies on
L_{R},L_{R}^{\prime},d,T
.

We omit the proof for the above claim, which involves some tedious but straightforward algebra, but we note that the proof uses
\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})}\right\|}_{2}^{j}}\right]=O{({\delta^{i}}^{j/2})}
(for all integer
j
) and that
\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(a{\left\|\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})}\right\|}_{2})}}\right]\leq 4\exp{(2a^{2}\delta^{i}d)}\leq 8
(for all positive
a
). It is also important to use our assumed upper bound on
\delta^{i}
in the Lemma statement.

We simplify (
B.1
) to

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k+1},y^{i}_{k+1})}^{2}-\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\delta^{i}\kappa{(\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})})}\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}^{2}-4\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}\bm{\left\langle}\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k},\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})}\bm{}

\displaystyle\quad+4\bm{\left\langle}\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k},\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})}\bm{}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+2\int_{0}^{1}\bm{\left\langle}R{({\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(s),{(\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})})}\circ\Gamma_{x^{i}_{k}}^{\gamma^{i}_{k}(s)}E^{i}_{k})}{(\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})})}\circ\Gamma_{x^{i}_{k}}^{\gamma^{i}_{k}(s)}E^{i}_{k},{\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(s)\bm{}ds

\displaystyle\quad+\tau^{i}_{k,1}+\tau^{i}_{k,2}+\tau^{i}_{k,3}

(B.2)

Step 4: Pulling out the expectation

We will further simplify (
B.2
) by replacing a few terms by their expectations. Define

\displaystyle{\tau}^{i}_{k,4}:=\int_{0}^{1}\bm{\left\langle}R{({\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(s),{(\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})})}\circ\Gamma_{x^{i}_{k}}^{\gamma^{i}_{k}(s)}E^{i}_{k})},{(\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})})}\circ\Gamma_{x^{i}_{k}}^{\gamma^{i}_{k}(s)}E^{i}_{k},{\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(s)\bm{}

\displaystyle\qquad-\delta^{i}Ric{({\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(s))}ds

\displaystyle{\tau}^{i}_{k,5}:=\delta^{i}-\bm{\left\langle}\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k},\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})}\bm{}^{2}

By definition of Ricci Curvature,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{{\mathcal{F}}_{k}}}\left[{\int_{0}^{1}\bm{\left\langle}R{({\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(s),{(\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})})}\circ\Gamma_{x^{i}_{k}}^{\gamma^{i}_{k}(s)}E^{i}_{k})},{(\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})})}\circ\Gamma_{x^{i}_{k}}^{\gamma^{i}_{k}(s)}E^{i}_{k},{\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(s)\bm{}ds}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\delta^{i}Ric{({\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(s))}ds

By definition of
\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k}
,
\mathbb{E}\left[{\bm{\left\langle}\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k},\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})}\bm{}^{2}}\right]=\delta^{i}{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}>\varepsilon\right\}}
.

Let
\tau^{i}_{k}:=\tau^{i}_{k,1}+\tau^{i}_{k,2}+\tau^{i}_{k,3}
.
We can thus further simplify (
B.2
) to

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k+1},y^{i}_{k+1})}^{2}-\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\delta^{i}\kappa{(\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})})}\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}^{2}-4\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}\bm{\left\langle}\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k},\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})}\bm{}

\displaystyle\quad+4\delta^{i}{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}>\varepsilon\right\}}+2\delta^{i}\int_{0}^{1}Ric{({\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(s))}ds

\displaystyle\quad+\tau^{i}_{k}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\delta^{i}{(2\kappa{(\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})})}+2L_{Ric})}\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad-4\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}\bm{\left\langle}\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k},\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})}\bm{}+4\delta^{i}{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}>\varepsilon\right\}}

\displaystyle\quad+\tau^{i}_{k}

(B.3)

the conclusion follows by defining
\mathbf{W}^{i}(t):=\int_{0}^{t}{\mathbbm{1}\left\{t\in[k\delta^{i},(k+1)\delta^{i}]\right\}}\bm{\left\langle}\bar{\bm{\nu}}^{i}_{k},\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})}\bm{}
and verifying that it is a Brownian motion. (Recall our definition that
\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k}:={\mathbbm{1}\left\{\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}>\varepsilon\right\}}\bar{\bm{\nu}}^{i}_{k}
)
∎

B.2
Lyapunov function and its smooth approximation

In this section, we consider a Lyapunov function
f
taken from
Eberle 2016
. By analyzing how
f(\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})})
evolves under the dynamic in Lemma
9
, one can demonstrate that the distance function contracts.

Let
\mathcal{L},\mathcal{R}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
. We will see later that
\mathcal{L}
and
\mathcal{R}
will correspond to distant-dissipativity parameters in (
1
).

Let
\varepsilon\in[0,\infty)
. One should think of
\varepsilon
as being arbitrarily small, as eventually we are only interested in the limit as
\varepsilon\to 0
.

Define functions
\psi_{\varepsilon}(r)
,
\Psi_{\varepsilon}(r)
and
\nu(r)
, all from
\mathbb{R}^{+}
to
\mathbb{R}
:

\displaystyle\mu_{\varepsilon}(r)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1,&\text{for }r\leq\mathcal{R}\\
1-(r-\mathcal{R})/{(\varepsilon)},&\text{for }r\in{\mathcal{R},\mathcal{R}+\varepsilon}\\
0,&\text{for }r\geq\mathcal{R}+\varepsilon\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle\nu_{\varepsilon}(r):=1-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\int_{0}^{r}\frac{\mu_{\varepsilon}(s)\Psi_{\varepsilon}(s)}{\psi_{\varepsilon}(s)}ds}{\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\mu_{\varepsilon}(s)\Psi_{\varepsilon}(s)}{\psi_{\varepsilon}(s)}ds}

\displaystyle\psi_{\varepsilon}(r):=e^{-\frac{\mathcal{L}\int_{0}^{r}r\mu_{\varepsilon}(r)dr}{2}}

\displaystyle\Psi_{\varepsilon}(r):=\int_{0}^{r}\psi_{\varepsilon}(s)ds,

We defined an
\varepsilon
-smoothed Lyapunov function as

Definition 1
.

\displaystyle f_{\varepsilon}(r):=\int_{0}^{r}\psi_{\varepsilon}(s)\nu_{\varepsilon}(s)ds

\displaystyle g_{\varepsilon}(s)=f_{\varepsilon}{(\sqrt{s+\varepsilon})}

The case when
\varepsilon=0
(when there is no smoothing) will be of particular interest to us:

Definition 2
.

\displaystyle f(r):=f_{0}(r)=g_{0}(r)

Remark 4
.

The Lyapunov function from
Eberle 2016
is more general, but for the specific case of
\mathcal{L},\mathcal{R}
distant dissipative functions, it is equal to
f
as defined in (
2
).

Lemma 10
.

Assume
\varepsilon\in(0,\min\left\{1/4,1/(4\sqrt{\mathcal{L}}),1/{(4\mathcal{L}\mathcal{R})}\right\}]

\displaystyle 1.\ f_{\varepsilon}(r)\in[\frac{1}{2}\exp{(-(1+\varepsilon)\mathcal{L}\mathcal{R}^{2}/2)}r,r]\qquad

for all
r

\displaystyle 2.\ f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(r)\in[\frac{1}{2}\exp{(-(1+\varepsilon)\mathcal{L}\mathcal{R}^{2}/2)},1]\qquad

for all
r

\displaystyle 3.\ f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}(r)\in[-4{\mathcal{L}}^{3/2},0]\qquad

for all
r

\displaystyle 4.\ f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}(r)+\mathcal{L}rf_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(r)\leq-\frac{\exp{(-(1+\varepsilon)\mathcal{L}\mathcal{R}^{2}/2)}}{{(1+\varepsilon)}^{2}\mathcal{R}^{2}}f_{\varepsilon}(r)\qquad

for
r\in[0,\mathcal{R}]

\displaystyle 5.\ {\left|f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime\prime}(r)\right|}\leq\frac{256\sqrt{\mathcal{L}}}{\varepsilon}\qquad

for all
r

Proof.

We can verify that

\displaystyle f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(r)

\displaystyle=\psi_{\varepsilon}(r)\nu_{\varepsilon}(r)

\displaystyle f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}(r)

\displaystyle=\psi_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(r)\nu_{\varepsilon}(r)+\psi_{\varepsilon}(r)\nu_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(r)

\displaystyle=-\mathcal{L}\mu_{\varepsilon}(r)r\psi_{\varepsilon}(r)\nu_{\varepsilon}(r)+\psi_{\varepsilon}(r)\nu_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(r)

\displaystyle f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime\prime}(r)

\displaystyle=-\mathcal{L}\psi_{\varepsilon}(r)\nu_{\varepsilon}(r)+\mathcal{L}r\psi_{\varepsilon}(r)\mu_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(r)+\mathcal{L}^{2}r^{2}\psi_{\varepsilon}(r)\nu_{\varepsilon}(r)-2\mathcal{L}r\psi_{\varepsilon}(r)\nu_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(r)+\psi_{\varepsilon}(r)\nu_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}(r)

1. follows from integrating 2.

2. follows from
\nu_{\varepsilon}(r)\in[1/2,1]
and
\psi_{\varepsilon}\in[\exp{(-(1+\varepsilon)\mathcal{L}\mathcal{R}^{2}/2)},1]
and the expression for
f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(r)
above.

3. follows from
\mu_{\varepsilon},\psi_{\varepsilon},\nu_{\varepsilon}\geq 0
and
\nu_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\leq 0
, and the fact that
r\psi_{\varepsilon}(r)\leq 2\sqrt{\mathcal{L}}
and (
B.4
).

4. is a little more involved. First note that over
r\in[0,\mathcal{R}]
,
\mu_{\varepsilon}(r)=1
. This will simplify some calculations. From the expression for
f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}
above, we verify

\displaystyle f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}(r)+\mathcal{L}rf_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(r)=\psi_{\varepsilon}(r)\nu_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(r)=-\frac{\Psi_{\varepsilon}(r)}{2\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\mu_{\varepsilon}(s)\Psi_{\varepsilon}(s)}{\psi_{\varepsilon}(s)}ds}

We can bound the denominator as

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\mu_{\varepsilon}(s)\Psi_{\varepsilon}(s)}{\psi_{\varepsilon}(s)}ds\leq\int_{0}^{\mathcal{R}+\varepsilon}\frac{\Psi_{\varepsilon}(s)}{\psi_{\varepsilon}(s)}ds\leq\frac{\int_{0}^{\mathcal{R}+\varepsilon}\Psi_{\varepsilon}(s)ds}{\psi{(\mathcal{R}+\varepsilon)}}\leq\frac{(1+\varepsilon)^{2}\mathcal{R}^{2}}{2\exp{(-\mathcal{L}(1+\varepsilon)\mathcal{R}^{2}/2)}}

where the first inequality is by
\mu_{\varepsilon}(s)\leq 1
, and
\mu_{\varepsilon}(r)=0
for
r\geq\mathcal{R}+\varepsilon
the second inequality is by
\psi_{\varepsilon}(r)
being monotonically decreasing, and the third inequality is by
\Psi_{\varepsilon}(r)\leq r
.Finally, note that
\Psi_{\varepsilon}(r)\geq f_{\varepsilon}(r)
. Put together,

\displaystyle f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}(r)+\mathcal{L}rf_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(r)\leq-\frac{\exp{(-(1+\varepsilon)\mathcal{L}\mathcal{R}^{2}/2)}}{{(1+\varepsilon)}^{2}\mathcal{R}^{2}}f_{\varepsilon}(r)

We now prove the bound for 5. It is useful to recall that
\psi_{\varepsilon}(r)\leq 1
and
\nu_{\varepsilon}(r)\leq 1
.

\displaystyle\Psi_{\varepsilon}(r)=\int_{0}^{r}\exp{(-\mathcal{L}s^{2})}ds\leq\frac{4}{\sqrt{\mathcal{L}}}

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\mu_{\varepsilon}(s)\Psi_{\varepsilon}(s)}{\psi_{\varepsilon}(s)}ds\geq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\mathcal{R}}\frac{\Psi_{\varepsilon}(s)}{\psi_{\varepsilon}(s)}ds\geq\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{1/\sqrt{2\mathcal{L}}}\Psi_{\varepsilon}(s)ds\geq\frac{1}{16\mathcal{L}}

(B.4)

\displaystyle{\left|\psi_{\varepsilon}(r)\nu^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}(r)\right|}\leq\frac{\Psi{(\mathcal{R}+\varepsilon)}}{2\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\mu_{\varepsilon}(s)\Psi_{\varepsilon}(s)}{\psi_{\varepsilon}(s)}ds}\leq 8\sqrt{\mathcal{L}}

For
r\in[0,\mathcal{R}+\varepsilon]
(
\nu_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}=0
outside this range),

\displaystyle{\left|\psi_{\varepsilon}(r)\nu_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}(r)\right|}\leq\frac{\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\Psi{(r)}+r\psi_{\varepsilon}(r)/\varepsilon+\psi_{\varepsilon}(r)+2r\Psi_{\varepsilon}(r)/\psi_{\varepsilon}(r)}{2\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\mu_{\varepsilon}(s)\Psi_{\varepsilon}(s)}{\psi_{\varepsilon}(s)}ds}\leq 32\mathcal{L}\cdot\Psi_{\varepsilon}(r)\cdot{(\frac{2}{\varepsilon}+2\mathcal{L}\mathcal{R})}\leq\frac{128\sqrt{\mathcal{L}}}{\varepsilon}

We can thus bound
{\left|f^{\prime\prime\prime}(r)\right|}
as

\displaystyle{\left|f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime\prime}(r)\right|}\leq 2\mathcal{L}+16\mathcal{L}^{3/2}\mathcal{R}+\frac{128\sqrt{\mathcal{L}}}{\varepsilon}\leq\frac{256\sqrt{\mathcal{L}}}{\varepsilon}

∎

Lemma 11
.

Assume
\varepsilon\in(0,\min\left\{1/4,1/(4\sqrt{\mathcal{L}}),1/{(4\mathcal{L}\mathcal{R})}\right\}]

\displaystyle 1.\ {g_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(s)}=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{s+\varepsilon}}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(\sqrt{s+\varepsilon})

\displaystyle 2.\ {g_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}(s)}=\frac{1}{4{(s+\varepsilon)}}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}(\sqrt{s+\varepsilon})-\frac{1}{4{(s+\varepsilon)}^{3/2}}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(\sqrt{s+\varepsilon})

\displaystyle 3.\ {g_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime\prime}(s)}=\frac{1}{8{(s+\varepsilon)}^{3/2}}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime\prime}(\sqrt{s+\varepsilon})-\frac{1}{8{(s+\varepsilon)}^{2}}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}(\sqrt{s+\varepsilon})+\frac{1}{6{(s+\varepsilon)}^{5/2}}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(\sqrt{s+\varepsilon})

\displaystyle 4.\ {\left|g_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime\prime}(s)\right|}\leq O{(\varepsilon^{-5/2})}\qquad\text{for all $s$}

where
O()
notation hides dependency on
\mathcal{L}
and
\mathcal{R}
.

Proof.

The first 3 points follow from chain rule.

The last point follows from point 5 from Lemma
10
.

\displaystyle{\left|g_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime\prime}(s)\right|}\leq\frac{64\sqrt{\mathcal{L}}}{\varepsilon^{5/2}\mathcal{R}}+\frac{\sqrt{\mathcal{L}}}{\varepsilon^{2}}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{5/2}}

∎

B.3
Contraction of Lyapunov Function under Kendall Cranston Coupling

Lemma 12
.

Consider the same setup as Lemma
9
. Let
\kappa(r):=\frac{1}{r^{2}}\sup_{\mathrm{d}{(x,y)}=r}\bm{\left\langle}\Gamma_{y}^{x}\beta(y)-\beta(x),\Exp_{x}^{-1}(y)\bm{}
. Assume there exists
\mathcal{R}\geq 0,q\leq 0
such that
\kappa(r)\leq q
for all
r\leq\mathcal{R}
. Let
\mathcal{L}=q+L_{Ric}
. Let
\varepsilon\in(0,\min\left\{1/4,1/(4\sqrt{\mathcal{L}}),1/{(4\mathcal{L}\mathcal{R})}\right\}]
. Let
g_{\varepsilon}
be as defined in
1
with parameters
\mathcal{L}
and
\mathcal{R}
. Let
{\mathcal{F}}_{k}
denote the natural filtration generated by
x^{i}_{k}
and
y^{i}_{k}
.

There exists a constant
c_{1}
, depending on
L_{\beta},L_{\beta}^{\prime},L_{R},T,d
, and some constant
c_{2}
, depending on
L_{\beta}^{\prime},L_{Ric},\mathcal{R}
such that for any
i>c_{1}
and
\varepsilon>c_{2}
, there exists a coupling between
x^{i}_{k}
and
y^{i}_{k}
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{g_{\varepsilon}{(\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k+1},y^{i}_{k+1})}^{2})}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{r>\mathcal{R}\right\}}\delta^{i}{({(\kappa(r_{k})+L_{Ric})}\exp{(-(1+\varepsilon)\mathcal{L}\mathcal{R}^{2}/2)}/8)}g_{\varepsilon}{(\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k+1},y^{i}_{k+1})}^{2})}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{\exp{(-(1+\varepsilon)\mathcal{L}\mathcal{R}^{2}/2)}}{2{(1+\varepsilon)}^{2}\mathcal{R}^{2}}\delta^{i}\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{r\leq\mathcal{R}\right\}}g_{\varepsilon}{(\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k+1},y^{i}_{k+1})}^{2})}}\right]+O{(\delta^{i}{\varepsilon^{1/2}}+\varepsilon^{-5/2}{\delta^{i}}^{3/2})}

where
O{()}
hides dependency on
L_{R},L_{\beta}^{\prime},T,d
.

Proof.

Let us define, for convenience,
r_{k}:=\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}
. By Lemma
9
, for any
i
and any
\varepsilon
, there exists a coupling satisfying

\displaystyle r_{k+1}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle{(1+\delta^{i}{(2\kappa{(r_{k})}+2L_{Ric})})}r_{k}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+{\mathbbm{1}\left\{r_{k}>\varepsilon^{1/3}\right\}}{(4\delta^{i}-4r_{k}\mathbf{W}^{i}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{W}^{i}{(k\delta^{i})})}+\tau^{i}_{k}

where
\tau^{i}_{k}
satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left|\tau^{i}_{k}\right|}}\right]\leq O{({\delta^{i}}^{3/2}{(1+L_{\beta}^{4})}{(1+r_{k}^{2})})}\qquad\mathbb{E}\left[{{{\tau}^{i}_{k}}^{2}}\right]\leq O{({{(1+r_{k}^{4})}{\delta^{i}}^{2}})}

where
O{()}
hides dependencies on
L_{R},L_{R}^{\prime},d,T
.

By third order Taylor expansion,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{g_{\varepsilon}{(r_{k+1}^{2})}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{g_{\varepsilon}{(r_{k}^{2})}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\left[{g_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}{(r_{k}^{2})}\cdot{(\delta^{i}{(2\kappa{(r_{k})}+2L_{Ric})})}r_{k}^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\left[{g_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}{(r_{k}^{2})}\cdot 4\delta^{i}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\left[{\frac{1}{2}g_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}{(r_{k}^{2})}\cdot{(4r_{k}{\mathbbm{1}\left\{r_{k}>\varepsilon^{1/3}\right\}}{(\mathbf{W}^{i}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{W}^{i}{(k\delta^{i})})})}^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+O{(\varepsilon^{-5/2}{\delta^{i}}^{3/2})}

(B.5)

Note that the last line uses two facts:

1.

From Lemma
24
, for any
j
, there exists a constant
{\mathcal{C}}
, depending on
T,d,L_{R},L_{\beta}^{\prime},L_{\xi}
, but independent of
L_{\beta}
, such that for all
i,k
,
\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},x_{0})}^{2j}}\right]<{\mathcal{C}}
and
\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(y^{i}_{k},y_{0})}^{2j}}\right]<{\mathcal{C}}
.

2.

Roughly speaking,
\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k+1},y^{i}_{k+1})}^{2}-\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}^{2}}\right]=O{({\delta^{i}}^{3/2})}
. More specifically:

\displaystyle{\left|\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k+1},y^{i}_{k+1})}-\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}\right|}

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k}x^{i}_{k+1})}+2\mathrm{d}{(y^{i}_{k}y^{i}_{k+1})}

\displaystyle\leq 2\delta^{i}{({\left\|\beta(x_{0})\right\|}+{\left\|\beta(y_{0})\right\|}+L_{\beta}^{\prime}\mathrm{d}{(\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},x_{0})})}+L_{\beta}^{\prime}\mathrm{d}{(\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},x_{0})})})}

\displaystyle\quad+4{\left\|\mathbf{B}((k+1)\delta^{i})-\mathbf{B}(k\delta^{i})\right\|}_{2}

Plugging in the definition of
g_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}
and
g_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}
,

\displaystyle g_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}{(r_{k}^{2})}\cdot{(\delta^{i}{({(2\kappa{(r_{k})}+2L_{Ric})})}r_{k}^{2}+4{\mathbbm{1}\left\{r_{k}>\varepsilon^{1/3}\right\}}\delta^{i})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\delta^{i}}{2\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon}}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}{(\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon})}{({(2\kappa{(r_{k})}+2L_{Ric})}r_{k}^{2}+4{\mathbbm{1}\left\{r_{k}>\varepsilon^{1/3}\right\}})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\delta^{i}}{2\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon}}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}{(\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon})}{({(2\kappa{(r_{k})}+2L_{Ric})}r_{k}^{2})}+2{\mathbbm{1}\left\{r_{k}>\varepsilon^{1/3}\right\}}\frac{\delta^{i}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}{(\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon})}}{\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon}}

where we use the assumption that
\varepsilon\leq\frac{1}{4\mathcal{R}^{2}}
and
m\geq 2L_{Ric}
and
\varepsilon<1
.

On the other hand,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{{\mathcal{F}}_{k}}}\left[{\frac{1}{2}g_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}{(r_{k}^{2})}\cdot{(4r_{k}{\mathbbm{1}\left\{r_{k}>\varepsilon^{1/3}\right\}}{(\mathbf{W}^{i}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{W}^{i}{(k\delta^{i})})})}^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 8\delta^{i}r_{k}^{2}g_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}{(r_{k}^{2})}\cdot{\mathbbm{1}\left\{r_{k}>\varepsilon^{1/3}\right\}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathbbm{1}\left\{r_{k}>\varepsilon^{1/3}\right\}}\frac{2\delta^{i}r_{k}^{2}}{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}{(\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon})}-{\mathbbm{1}\left\{r_{k}>\varepsilon^{1/3}\right\}}\frac{2\delta^{i}r_{k}^{2}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}{(\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon})}}{{(r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon)}^{3/2}}

Note that
r_{k}>\varepsilon^{1/3}
implies that
\frac{r_{k}^{2}}{{(r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon)}^{3/2}}\geq\frac{1}{1+\varepsilon^{1/3}}
. Thus

\displaystyle 2{\mathbbm{1}\left\{r_{k}>\varepsilon^{1/3}\right\}}\frac{\delta^{i}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}{(\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon})}}{\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon}}-{\mathbbm{1}\left\{r_{k}>\varepsilon^{1/3}\right\}}\frac{2\delta^{i}r_{k}^{2}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}{(\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon})}}{{(r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon)}^{3/2}}\leq 4\delta^{i}\varepsilon^{1/3}

(B.6)

where we use the fact that
{\left|f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right|}\leq 1
.

We now bound
\frac{\delta^{i}}{2\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon}}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}{(\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon})}{({(2\kappa{(r_{k})}+2L_{Ric})}r_{k}^{2})}+{\mathbbm{1}\left\{r_{k}>\varepsilon^{1/3}\right\}}\frac{2\delta^{i}r_{k}^{2}}{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}{(\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon})}
. Consider three cases:

1.

r_{k}\leq\varepsilon^{1/3}
:

\displaystyle\frac{\delta^{i}}{2\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon}}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}{(\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon})}{({(2\kappa{(r_{k})}+2L_{Ric})}r_{k}^{2})}\leq{\delta^{i}{(q+L_{Ric})}}{\varepsilon^{1/2}}

2.

r_{k}\in(\varepsilon^{1/3},\mathcal{R}]
:

\displaystyle\frac{\delta^{i}}{2\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon}}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}{(\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon})}{({(2\kappa{(r_{k})}+2L_{Ric})}r_{k}^{2})}+\frac{2\delta^{i}r_{k}^{2}}{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}{(\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\delta^{i}r_{k}^{2}}{{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon}}{(\mathcal{L}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}{(\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon})}\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon}+2f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}{(\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon})})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\frac{\exp{(-(1+\varepsilon)\mathcal{L}\mathcal{R}^{2}/2)}}{2{(1+\varepsilon)}^{2}\mathcal{R}^{2}}\delta^{i}f_{\varepsilon}{(\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon})}

where we use Lemma
10
and the definition of
\mathcal{L}
.

3.

r_{k}>\mathcal{R}
: We use the fact that
f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}(r)\leq 0
for all
r\geq\mathcal{R}\geq\varepsilon
. Thus

\displaystyle\frac{\delta^{i}}{2\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon}}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}{(\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon})}{({(2\kappa{(r_{k})}+2L_{Ric})}r_{k}^{2})}+\frac{2\delta^{i}r_{k}^{2}}{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}{(\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\delta^{i}}{2\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon}}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}{(\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon})}{({(2\kappa{(r_{k})}+2L_{Ric})}r_{k}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\frac{\delta^{i}{({(\kappa(r_{k})+L_{Ric})})}r_{k}^{2}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}{(\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon})}}{8\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{8}\delta^{i}{({(\kappa(r_{k})+L_{Ric})}\exp{(-(1+\varepsilon)\mathcal{L}\mathcal{R}^{2}/2)})}f_{\varepsilon}{(\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon})}

∎

Proof of Lemma
3
.

Lemma
12
almost gives us what we need. However, because we assumed that
\beta
satisfies Assumption
1
, the assumption that
{\left\|\beta(x)\right\|}\leq L_{\beta}
cannot possibly hold. We thus need to approximate
\beta
by a sequence of increasingly non-Lipschitz functions.

Consider a fixed
i
.

Let
s^{j}
be a sequence of increasing radius, such that
s^{j}\to\infty
as
j\to\infty
. Let
\beta^{j}
denote the truncation of
\beta
to norm
s^{j}
, i.e.

\displaystyle\beta^{j}(x):=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\beta(x)&\text{for }{\left\|\beta(x)\right\|}\leq s^{j}\\
\beta(x)\cdot\frac{s^{j}}{{\left\|\beta(x)\right\|}}&\text{for }{\left\|\beta(x)\right\|}>s^{j}\end{array}\right.

We verify that
\beta^{j}
also satisfies Assumption
2
with the same
L_{\beta}^{\prime}
as
\beta
.

Recall that we defined

\displaystyle x^{i}(t):=\overline{\Phi}(t;x_{0},E^{x},\beta,\mathbf{B}^{x},i)

\displaystyle y^{i}(t):=\overline{\Phi}(t;y_{0},E^{y},\beta,\mathbf{B}^{y},i)

\displaystyle x(t):={\Phi}(t;x_{0},E^{x},\beta,\mathbf{B}^{x})

\displaystyle y(t):={\Phi}(t;y_{0},E^{y},\beta,\mathbf{B}^{y})

where
\Phi
and
\overline{\Phi}
are as defined in (
3.3
) and (
3.4
), and where
\mathbf{B}^{x}
and
\mathbf{B}^{y}
are two Brownian motions which may be coupled in a non-trivial way. Furthermore, define, for all
i
,

\displaystyle\tilde{x}^{i,j}(t):=\overline{\Phi}(t;x_{0},E^{x},\beta^{j},\mathbf{B}^{x},i)

\displaystyle\tilde{y}^{i,j}(t):=\overline{\Phi}(t;y_{0},E^{y},\beta^{j},\mathbf{B}^{y},i)

\displaystyle\tilde{x}^{\cdot,j}(t):={\Phi}(t;x_{0},E^{x},\beta^{j},\mathbf{B}^{x})

\displaystyle\tilde{y}^{\cdot,j}(t):={\Phi}(t;y_{0},E^{y},\beta^{j},\mathbf{B}^{y})

note that the above definition implies a non-trivial coupling between
\tilde{x}^{i,j}(t)
and
x^{i}(t)
for all
j
, via the shared Brownian motion
\mathbf{B}^{x}
.

Let
L_{0}:=\max\left\{{\left\|\beta(x_{0})\right\|},{\left\|\beta(y_{0})\right\|}\right\}
. Let us define
{\tilde{r}^{i,j}_{k}}:=\mathrm{d}{(\tilde{x}^{i,j}_{k},\tilde{y}^{i,j}_{k})}
. Using Assumption
1
and the assumption that
m\geq 2L_{Ric}
, we verify that

\displaystyle{\mathbbm{1}\left\{{\tilde{r}^{i,j}_{k}}>\mathcal{R}\right\}}{(\kappa({\tilde{r}^{i,j}_{k}})+L_{Ric})}

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\mathcal{R}<{\tilde{r}^{i,j}_{k}}\leq\frac{s^{j}-L_{0}}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}\right\}}{(-m/2)}+{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\mathcal{R}<{\tilde{r}^{i,j}_{k}},\frac{s^{j}-L_{0}}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}\leq{\tilde{r}^{i,j}_{k}}\right\}}{(\frac{s^{j}}{{\tilde{r}^{i,j}_{k}}}+L_{Ric})}

Let
q,\mathcal{R}
be the parameters in Assumption
1
. This implies that
\kappa(r)\leq q
for all
r\leq\mathcal{R}
. Let
\mathcal{L}:=q+L_{Ric}
and
\varepsilon
be as defined in Lemma
12
. Then by Lemma
12
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{g_{\varepsilon}{(r_{k+1}^{2})}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{r>\mathcal{R}\right\}}\delta^{i}{({(\kappa({\tilde{r}^{i,j}_{k}})+L_{Ric})}\exp{(-(1+\varepsilon)\mathcal{L}\mathcal{R}^{2}/2)}/8)}g_{\varepsilon}{(r_{k+1}^{2})}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{\exp{(-(1+\varepsilon)\mathcal{L}\mathcal{R}^{2}/2)}}{2{(1+\varepsilon)}^{2}\mathcal{R}^{2}}\delta^{i}\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{r\leq\mathcal{R}\right\}}g_{\varepsilon}{(r_{k+1}^{2})}}\right]+O{(\delta^{i}{\varepsilon^{1/2}}+\varepsilon^{-5/2}{\delta^{i}}^{3/2})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\frac{\delta^{i}m\exp{(-(1+\varepsilon)\mathcal{L}\mathcal{R}^{2}/2)}}{16}\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\mathcal{R}<{\tilde{r}^{i,j}_{k}}\leq\frac{s^{j}-L_{0}}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}\right\}}g_{\varepsilon}{(r_{k+1}^{2})}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{\delta^{i}\exp{(-(1+\varepsilon)\mathcal{L}\mathcal{R}^{2}/2)}}{2{(1+\varepsilon)}^{2}\mathcal{R}^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{r\leq\mathcal{R}\right\}}g_{\varepsilon}{(r_{k+1}^{2})}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\delta^{i}\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\frac{s^{j}-L_{0}}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}\leq{\tilde{r}^{i,j}_{k}}\right\}}{({s^{j}}+L_{Ric}{\tilde{r}^{i,j}_{k}})}}\right]+O{(\delta^{i}{\varepsilon^{1/2}}+\varepsilon^{-5/2}{\delta^{i}}^{3/2})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\alpha_{\varepsilon}\delta^{i}\mathbb{E}\left[{g_{\varepsilon}{(r_{k+1}^{2})}}\right]+\delta^{i}\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\frac{s^{j}-L_{0}}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}\leq{\tilde{r}^{i,j}_{k}}\right\}}{({s^{j}}+{(m+L_{Ric})}{\tilde{r}^{i,j}_{k}})}}\right]+O{(\delta^{i}{\varepsilon^{1/2}}+\varepsilon^{-5/2}{\delta^{i}}^{3/2})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\alpha_{\varepsilon}\delta^{i}\mathbb{E}\left[{g_{\varepsilon}{(r_{k+1}^{2})}}\right]+\delta^{i}\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\frac{s^{j}-L_{0}}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}\leq{\tilde{r}^{i,j}_{k}}\right\}}{({s^{j}}+{(m+L_{Ric})}{\tilde{r}^{i,j}_{k}})}}\right]+O{(\delta^{i}{\varepsilon^{1/2}}+\varepsilon^{-5/2}{\delta^{i}}^{3/2})}

where we define
\alpha_{\varepsilon}:=\min\left\{\frac{m}{16},\frac{1}{2{(1+\varepsilon)}^{2}\mathcal{R}^{2}}\right\}\exp{(-\frac{1}{2}(1+\varepsilon)\mathcal{L}\mathcal{R}^{2})}
.

Applying the above recursively for
k=0...K
, where
K=T/2^{i}
, we get that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{g_{\varepsilon}{({(\tilde{r}^{i,j}_{K})}^{2})}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp{(-\alpha_{\varepsilon}K\delta^{i})}\mathbb{E}\left[{g_{\varepsilon}{(r_{0}^{2})}}\right]+O{(T\varepsilon^{1/2}+T\varepsilon^{-5/2}{\delta^{i}}^{1/2})}

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\frac{s^{j}-L_{0}}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}\leq\sup_{k\leq K}{\tilde{r}^{i,j}_{k}}\right\}}\sum_{k=0}^{K}\delta^{i}{\tilde{r}^{i,j}_{k}}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp{(-\alpha_{\varepsilon}K\delta^{i})}\mathbb{E}\left[{g_{\varepsilon}{(r_{0}^{2})}}\right]+O{(T\varepsilon^{1/2}+T\varepsilon^{-5/2}{\delta^{i}}^{1/2})}

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{P}{({\frac{s^{j}-L_{0}}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}\leq\sup_{k\leq K}{\tilde{r}^{i,j}_{k}}})}^{1/2}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[{{(\sum_{k=0}^{K}\delta^{i}{\tilde{r}^{i,j}_{k}})}^{2}}\right]}

From Lemma
26
,
\mathbb{P}{({\frac{s^{j}-L_{0}}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}\leq\sup_{k\leq K}{\tilde{r}^{i,j}_{k}}})}^{1/2}=O{(\frac{1}{s^{j}})}
and
\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[{{(\sum_{k=0}^{K}\delta^{i}{\tilde{r}^{i,j}_{k}})}^{2}}\right]}=O{(T)}
. Recalling the definition of
\tilde{r}
, and the fact that
\mathrm{d}{(\tilde{x}^{i}_{K},\tilde{y}^{i}_{K})}:=\mathrm{d}{(\tilde{x}^{i}(T),\tilde{y}^{i}(T))}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{g_{\varepsilon}{(\mathrm{d}{(\tilde{x}^{i,j}(T),\tilde{y}^{i,j}(T)}^{2})}}\right]\leq

\displaystyle\exp{(-\alpha_{\varepsilon}K\delta^{i})}g_{\varepsilon}{(\mathrm{d}{(x_{0},y_{0})}^{2})}+O{(\varepsilon^{1/2}+\varepsilon^{-5/2}{\delta^{i}}^{1/2}+\frac{1}{s^{j}})}

First, by taking the limit of
i
to infinity (e.g. for each
i
, we see that for any
j
and any
\varepsilon
,

\displaystyle\lim_{i\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[{g_{\varepsilon}{(\mathrm{d}{(\tilde{x}^{i,j}(T),\tilde{y}^{i,j}(T))}^{2})}}\right]\leq

\displaystyle\exp{(-\alpha_{\varepsilon}K\delta^{i})}g_{\varepsilon}{(\mathrm{d}{(x_{0},y_{0})}^{2})}+O{(\varepsilon^{1/2}+\frac{1}{s^{j}})}

Let us define
\tilde{x}^{\cdot,j}(t)
as the almost sure limit of
\tilde{x}^{i,j}(t)
, as
i\to\infty
, whose existence is shown in Lemma
1
(similarly for
\tilde{y}^{\cdot,j}(t)
). It follows that
g_{\varepsilon}{(\mathrm{d}{(\tilde{x}^{i,j}(T),\tilde{y}^{i,j}(T)}^{2})}
converges almost surely to
g_{\varepsilon}{(\mathrm{d}{(\tilde{x}^{\cdot,j}(T),\tilde{y}^{\cdot,j}(T))}^{2})}
as
i\to\infty
. By dominated convergence (Lemma
26
implies a single constant upper bounds
\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(\tilde{x}^{i,j}(T),\tilde{y}^{i,j}(T)}^{2}}\right]
for all
i
),
\mathbb{E}\left[{g_{\varepsilon}{(\mathrm{d}{(\tilde{x}^{i,j}(T),\tilde{y}^{i,j}(T))}^{2})}}\right]
converges to
\mathbb{E}\left[{g_{\varepsilon}{(\mathrm{d}{(\tilde{x}^{\cdot,j}(T),\tilde{y}^{\cdot,j}(T))}^{2})}}\right]
as
i\to\infty
. Let
\Omega
denote the set of all couplings between the
\mathbf{B}(t)
and
\tilde{\mathbf{B}}(t)
, which induces a coupling between
\tilde{x}^{\cdot,j}(t)
and
\tilde{y}^{\cdot,j}(t)
. Then

\displaystyle\inf_{\Omega}\mathbb{E}\left[{g_{\varepsilon}{(\mathrm{d}{(\tilde{x}^{\cdot,j}(T),\tilde{y}^{\cdot,j}(T))}^{2})}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lim_{i\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[{g_{\varepsilon}{(\mathrm{d}{(\tilde{x}^{i,j}(T),\tilde{y}^{i,j}(T))}^{2})}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp{(-\alpha_{\varepsilon}K\delta^{i})}g_{\varepsilon}{(\mathrm{d}{(x_{0},y_{0})}^{2})}+O{(\varepsilon^{1/2}+\frac{1}{s^{j}})}

(B.7)

From Lemma
26
, we know there exists a constant
{\mathcal{C}}
(depending on
T,L_{R},L_{\beta}^{\prime},{\left\|\beta(x_{0})\right\|}
), such that for all
i
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\sup_{t}\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}(t),x_{0})}^{2}\geq s})}\leq\frac{{\mathcal{C}}}{s}

(by definition in (
3.3
),
x^{i}(t)
are linear interpolations of
x^{i}(k)
). Thus
\mathbb{P}{({\sup_{t}\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}(t),x_{0})}=\infty})}=0
. Next, notice that when
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathrm{d}{(x(t),x_{0})}\leq\frac{s^{j}-L_{0}}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}
,
x(t)=\tilde{x}^{\cdot,j}(t)
for all
t\in[0,T]
. It thus follows that as
s^{j}\to\infty
,
\mathrm{d}{(x(t),\tilde{x}^{\cdot,j}(t))}
converges to
0
almost surely (similarly for
y
). Thus
g_{\varepsilon}{(\mathrm{d}{(\tilde{x}^{\cdot,j}(T),\tilde{y}^{\cdot,j}(T))})}
converges to
g_{\varepsilon}{(\mathrm{d}{(x(T),y(T))})}
almost surely, and by dominated convergence, in
L_{1}
as well. Thus taking limit of (
B.7
) as
j\to\infty
, aka
s^{j}\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\inf_{\Omega}\mathbb{E}\left[{g_{\varepsilon}{(\mathrm{d}{(x(T),y(T))}^{2})}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp{(-\alpha_{\varepsilon}K\delta^{i})}g_{\varepsilon}{(\mathrm{d}{(x_{0},y_{0})}^{2})}+O{(\varepsilon^{1/2})}

Finally, take the limit of
\varepsilon\to 0
. Note that
g_{\varepsilon}(r^{2})\to g_{0}(r^{2})=f(r)
. Note also that
\alpha_{\varepsilon}\to\alpha
as defined in the lemma statement. Finally, the properties of
f
follows from Lemma
10
.

∎

B.4
Useful Miscellaneous Results

The following Lemma is taken from
Sun et al. 2019
:

Lemma 13
.

For any
x\in M
,
a,y\in T_{x}M

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(\Exp_{x}(y+a),\Exp_{\Exp_{x}(a)}{(\Gamma_{x}^{\Exp_{x}(a)}y)})}\leq

\displaystyle L_{R}{\left\|a\right\|}{\left\|y\right\|}{({\left\|a\right\|}+{\left\|y\right\|})}e^{\sqrt{L_{R}}{({\left\|a\right\|}+{\left\|y\right\|})}}

Proof.

From the proof of Lemma 3 from
Sun et al. 2019
(which is in turn a refinement of the proof from
Karcher 1977
)

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(\Exp_{x}(y+a),\Exp_{\Exp_{x}(a)}{(\Gamma_{x}^{\Exp_{x}(a)}y)})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\cosh(\sqrt{L_{R}}{\left\|y+(1-t)a\right\|})-\frac{\sinh(\sqrt{L_{R}}{\left\|y+(1-t)a\right\|})}{\sqrt{L_{R}}{\left\|y+(1-t)a\right\|}}}{{\left\|y+(1-t)a\right\|}}dt\cdot{\left\|a\right\|}{\left\|y\right\|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{L_{R}}\int_{0}^{1}\sqrt{L_{R}}{\left\|y+(1-t)a\right\|}e^{\sqrt{L_{R}}{\left\|y+(1-t)a\right\|}}dt\cdot{\left\|a\right\|}{\left\|y\right\|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle L_{R}{\left\|a\right\|}{\left\|y\right\|}{({\left\|a\right\|}+{\left\|y\right\|})}e^{\sqrt{L_{R}}{({\left\|a\right\|}+{\left\|y\right\|})}}

where we use the fact from Lemma
14
that for all
r\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\frac{\cosh(r)}{r}-\frac{\sinh(r)}{r^{2}}\leq re^{r}

∎

Lemma 14
.

For all
r\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\sinh(r)\leq re^{r}

\displaystyle\cosh(r)-1\leq\frac{r^{2}}{2}e^{r}

\displaystyle\frac{\cosh(r)}{r}-\frac{\sinh(r)}{r^{2}}\leq re^{r}

\displaystyle\cosh(r)\leq e^{r}

\displaystyle\frac{\sinh(r)}{r}-1\leq r^{2}e^{3}

Proof.

Wolfram alpha says that
\sinh(r)-re^{r}
has global maximum at
r=0
with value
\sinh(0)=0
.

Wolfram alpha also says that
\cosh(r)-1=2\sinh^{2}(r/2)\leq r^{2}e^{r}/2
by the first bound.

The last two inequalities are all from Wolfram alpha.

∎

Appendix C
Non-Gaussian Approximation

In this section, we prove Theorem
2
, which bounds the distance between a manifold diffusion, and a random walk on the manifold with non-Gaussian noise term.

C.1
Proof of Theorem
2
and Lemma
5

Proof of Theorem
2
.

Step 1: Tangent Space Non-Gaussian Walk

For
k\leq K-1
, define

\displaystyle y_{k+1}=\Psi(\delta;y_{0},\beta,\xi_{k})

\displaystyle\tilde{z}_{k+1}=\tilde{\Psi}{(\delta;y_{0},\tilde{z}_{k},\beta,\xi_{k})}

\displaystyle\tilde{y}_{k+1}=\Exp_{y_{0}}{(\tilde{z}_{k+1})}

where
\Psi
and
\tilde{\Psi}
are as definde in (
4.1
) and (
C.13
) respectively. Assume
T
satisfies

\displaystyle T\leq\min\left\{\frac{1}{64L_{\beta}^{\prime}},\frac{1}{64{L_{\xi}^{\prime}}^{2}},\frac{1}{16\sqrt{L_{R}}L_{\beta}},\frac{1}{256L_{R}L_{\xi}^{2}},\frac{{\mathcal{C}}_{r}}{16L_{\beta}},\frac{{\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{2}}{1024L_{R}L_{\xi}^{2}\log{(L_{R}/{\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{2})}},\frac{{\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{2/3}}{2L_{\xi}^{2/3}}\right\}

(C.1)

where
{\mathcal{C}}_{r}
is defined in (
C.12
). We can apply Corollary
15
to bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(\tilde{y}_{K},y_{K})}}\right]\leq\tilde{O}{({(K\delta)}^{3/2}{(1+L_{\beta}^{2})})}

where
\tilde{O}
hides polynomial dependency on
L_{R},L_{R}^{\prime},L_{\beta}^{\prime},L_{\xi},\log\frac{1}{K\delta}
.

Step 2: Tangent Space Central Limit Theorem

In the previous step, we have shown that,
y_{K}
can be well approximated by
\tilde{y}_{K}=\Exp_{y_{0}}{(\tilde{z}_{K})}
. In this step, we show that
\tilde{z}_{K}
is close to a Gaussian distribution. Recall that
E
is an orthonormal basis of
y_{0}
(the exact choice does not matter). For any
v\in T_{y_{0}}M
, we let
\mathbf{v}
denote its coordinates wrt
E
. Let us define the random function
H:T_{y_{0}}M\to T_{y_{0}}M
as

\displaystyle H_{k}(z)=\Gamma_{\Exp_{y_{0}}(z)}^{y_{0}}{(\xi_{k}{(\Exp_{y_{0}}(z))})}

where the parallel transport is along the geodesic
\gamma(t)=\Exp_{y_{0}}(tz)

We can verify, from definition of (
C.13
), that the following are equivalent:

\displaystyle\tilde{z}_{k+1}=\tilde{\Psi}{(\delta;y_{0},\tilde{z}_{k},\beta,\xi_{k})}

\displaystyle\tilde{z}_{k+1}=\tilde{z}_{k}+\delta\beta(y_{0})+\sqrt{\delta}H_{k}(\tilde{z}_{k})

(C.2)

Let us define
{\mathbf{H}}_{k}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
as the vector-valued function, whose
j^{th}
coordinate is given by

\displaystyle{\mathbf{H}}_{k,j}(\mathbf{z})=\bm{\left\langle}H_{k}{(\mathbf{z}\circ E)},E_{j}\bm{}

(recall that
\mathbf{z}\circ E=\sum_{j=1}^{d}\mathbf{z}_{j}E_{j}
).

Finally, let us define
{\bm{\beta}}
denote the coordinates of
\beta(y_{0})
wrt
E
.

Let us define, for
k\leq K-1
,

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k+1}=\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}+\delta{\bm{\beta}}+\sqrt{\delta}{\mathbf{H}}_{k}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k})

(C.3)

We can verify the processes in (
C.2
) and (
C.3
) are equivalent in the following sense:
\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}\circ E=\tilde{z}_{k}
.

The main goal of this section is to bound the Euclidean distance between
\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}
in (
C.3
) and
\mathbf{z}_{k}
defined below:

\displaystyle{\mathbf{z}}_{k+1}={\mathbf{z}}_{k}+\delta{\bm{\beta}}+\sqrt{\delta}{\bm{\zeta}}_{k+1}

(C.4)

where
{\bm{\zeta}}_{k}
are iid samples from
\mathcal{N}(0,I)
.

Before going into the main proof, we state two important assumptions, and their consequences.

Step 2.1: Regularity of
{\mathbf{H}}

Under Assumption
6
, we can use Lemma
18
to bound the derivatives of
{\mathbf{H}}_{k}
, for all
{\left\|\mathbf{z}\right\|}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{L_{R}}}
. Let
L_{{\mathbf{H}}}:=L_{\xi}
. We claim that there exists constants
L_{\mathbf{H}},L_{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime},L_{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime\prime}=poly{(L_{R},L_{R}^{\prime},L_{R}^{\prime\prime},L_{\xi},L_{\xi}^{\prime},L_{\xi}^{\prime\prime})}
such that

For all
{\left\|\mathbf{z}\right\|}_{2}
:

\displaystyle\qquad{\left\|{\mathbf{H}}(\mathbf{z})\right\|}_{2}\leq L_{\mathbf{H}}

For all
{\left\|\mathbf{z}\right\|}_{2}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{L_{R}}}
:

\displaystyle\qquad{\left\|\nabla{\mathbf{H}}(\mathbf{z})\right\|}_{2}\leq L_{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime}\qquad{\left\|\nabla^{2}{\mathbf{H}}(\mathbf{z})\right\|}_{2}\leq L_{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime\prime}

(C.5)

The first line of (
C.5
) is immediate, since
H_{k}
is simply the parallel transport of
\xi_{k}
. The second line of (
C.5
) follows from Lemma
18
. The exact expression of
L_{\mathbf{H}},L_{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime},L_{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime\prime}
can be found in the statement of Lemma
18
.

Step 2.2: Moments of
{\mathbf{H}}

Again using the fact that
H_{k}
is the parallel transport of
\xi_{k}
, and using Assumption
7
and Lemma
67
, we verify that for all
\mathbf{u}
,
\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbf{H}}(\mathbf{u})}\right]=0
and
\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbf{H}}(\mathbf{u}){\mathbf{H}}(\mathbf{u})^{T}}\right]=I
.

Step 2.3: Assumptions on
\delta
and
K\delta

Let us assume that

\displaystyle T\leq\min\left\{\frac{1}{16L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}},\frac{1}{16{L_{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime}}^{2}},\frac{1}{32\sqrt{L_{R}}L_{\beta}},\frac{1}{128L_{R}L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}},\frac{1}{256L_{R}L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}\log{(2^{10}L_{R})}}\right\}

We will apply Lemma
42
, which shows that there exists a coupling between
\mathbf{z}_{k}
and
\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}
such that

\displaystyle\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{K}-\mathbf{z}_{K}\right\|}_{2}^{2}}\right]}\leq

\displaystyle\lambda_{6}\cdot\log{(\frac{1}{T})}^{4}\cdot T^{3/2}

(C.7)

where
\lambda_{6}=poly{(L_{\mathbf{H}},L_{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime},L_{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime\prime},L_{R})}=poly{(L_{R},L_{R}^{\prime},L_{R}^{\prime\prime},L_{\xi},L_{\xi}^{\prime},L_{\xi}^{\prime\prime})}
is some positive constant. We will apply Lemma
63
to convert this bound on
{\left\|\mathbf{z}_{K}-\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{K}\right\|}
to a bound on
\mathrm{d}{(\Exp_{y_{0}}(z_{K}),\Exp_{y_{0}}(\tilde{z}_{K}))}
: let
{\mathcal{C}}:=\sqrt{L_{R}}{({\left\|\mathbf{z}_{K}\right\|}_{2}+{\left\|\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{K}\right\|}_{2})}
, then

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(\Exp_{y_{0}}(z_{K}),\Exp_{y_{0}}(\tilde{z}_{K}))}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle 32e^{{\mathcal{C}}}{\left\|\mathbf{z}_{k}-\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}\right\|}_{2}^{2}

Taking square-root, then taking expectation and applying Cauchy Schwarz,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(\Exp_{y_{0}}(z_{K}),\Exp_{y_{0}}(\tilde{z}_{K}))}}\right]\leq 8\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[{e^{\sqrt{L_{R}}{({\left\|\mathbf{z}_{K}\right\|}_{2}+{\left\|\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{K}\right\|}_{2})}}}\right]}\cdot\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|\mathbf{z}_{k}-\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}\right\|}_{2}^{2}}\right]}

The
\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|\mathbf{z}_{k}-\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}\right\|}_{2}^{2}}\right]}
has been bound in (
C.7
). The
\mathbb{E}\left[{e^{\sqrt{L_{R}}{({\left\|\mathbf{z}_{K}\right\|}_{2}+{\left\|\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{K}\right\|}_{2})}}}\right]
can be bound using Lemma
56
: Using our assumption that
K\delta\leq\frac{1}{L_{R}L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{e^{\sqrt{L_{R}}{\left\|\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{K}\right\|}_{2}}}\right]\leq

\displaystyle 4\mathbb{E}\left[{e^{L_{R}{\left\|\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{K}\right\|}_{2}^{2}}}\right]\leq 4\mathbb{E}\left[{e^{\frac{1}{K\delta L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}}{\left\|\mathbf{z}_{K}\right\|}_{2}^{2}}}\right]\leq 8

Similarly, recalling that
L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}\geq d
since
Cov({\mathbf{H}})=I
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{e^{\sqrt{L_{R}}{\left\|\mathbf{z}_{K}\right\|}_{2}}}\right]\leq

\displaystyle 4\mathbb{E}\left[{e^{\frac{1}{K\delta d}{\left\|\mathbf{z}_{K}\right\|}_{2}^{2}}}\right]\leq 4{(\mathbb{E}\left[{e^{\frac{1}{K\delta}{\left\|\mathbf{z}_{K}\right\|}_{2}^{2}}}\right])}^{1/d}\leq 8

Putting everything together,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(\Exp_{y_{0}}(z_{K}),\Exp_{y_{0}}(\tilde{z}_{K}))}}\right]\leq 512\lambda_{6}\cdot\log{(\frac{1}{T})}^{4}\cdot T^{3/2}

Recall the definition of
\bar{y}_{k}=\overline{\Phi}(k\delta;y_{0},E,\beta,\mathbf{B},0)
, and notice from the definition of
\overline{\Phi}
in (
3.3
) that
\bar{y}_{K}
has the same distribution as
\Exp_{y_{0}}{({\mathbf{z}}_{K})}
. This implies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(\bar{y}_{K},\tilde{y}_{K})}}\right]\leq\tilde{O}{({(K\delta)}^{3/2})}

By triangle inequality, we can verify that
\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(\bar{y}_{K},y_{K})}}\right]\leq\tilde{O}{({(K\delta)}^{3/2}{(1+L_{\beta}^{2})})}
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
5
.

Let us define a radius
r
and the event
A_{k}
(for
k\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}
) as

\displaystyle r:=64{(\mathcal{R}+\frac{\sqrt{L_{R}}L_{\xi}^{2}}{m})}+64\sqrt{\frac{L_{\xi}^{2}}{m}}\log{(\frac{NK}{\delta})}

\displaystyle A_{k}:=\max_{j\leq k}\mathrm{d}{({y}_{j},x^{*})}\leq r

(C.8)

Recall that
K=\delta^{-2}
so that
r
depends polynomially on
N
and
\log(1/K\delta)
.

Step 1:
K
-step Approximation to Euler Murayama Step

Let us define
L_{\beta}:=L_{\beta}^{\prime}r
, and
\beta^{r}(x):=\Pi_{L_{\beta}}{(\beta(x))}
. Note that
\beta^{r}
also satisfies Assumption
2
with parameter
L_{\beta}^{\prime}
.

Consider a fixed
i
. Let
\tilde{y}_{iK}:=y_{iK}
, and for
k=0...K-1
,

\displaystyle\tilde{y}_{iK+k+1}=\Psi(\delta;\tilde{y}_{iK+k},\beta^{r},\xi_{k})

where
\Psi
is as defined in (
4.1
). Let us also define

\displaystyle\bar{y}_{(i+1)K}:=\overline{\Phi}(K\delta;y_{iK},E^{iK},\beta,\bar{\mathbf{B}}^{iK})

where
\Phi
is as defined in (
3.4
),
E^{iK}
is an orthonormal basis at
T_{y_{iK}}M
,
\bar{\mathbf{B}}^{iK}
is a standard Euclidean Brownian motion. From the definition of
\beta^{r}
,
\overline{\Phi}(K\delta;y_{iK},E^{iK},\beta,\bar{\mathbf{B}}^{iK})=\overline{\Phi}(K\delta;y_{iK},E^{iK},\beta^{r},\bar{\mathbf{B}}^{iK})
under the event
A_{iK}
, because
\mathrm{d}{(y_{iK},x^{*})}\leq r\Rightarrow{\left\|\beta(y_{iK})\right\|}\leq L_{\beta}\Rightarrow\beta(y_{iK})=\beta^{r}(y_{iK})
. By Theorem
2
(with
\beta^{r}
as the drift vector field),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{A_{iK}\right\}}{(\mathrm{d}{(\bar{y}_{(i+1)K},\tilde{y}_{(i+1)K})})}}\right]\leq{O}{({(K\delta)}^{3/2}{(1+{s}^{3})})}=\tilde{O}{({(K\delta)}^{3/2})}

where
\tilde{O}
hides polynomial dependence on
L_{R},L_{R}^{\prime},L_{\beta}^{\prime},L_{\xi},L_{\xi}^{\prime},L_{\xi}^{\prime\prime},\log(N),\log{(\frac{1}{K\delta})}
. Note that Theorem
2
requires that
K\delta\leq 1/{\mathcal{C}}_{1}
for some
{\mathcal{C}}_{1}=poly(L_{\beta},L_{\beta}^{\prime},L_{\xi},L_{\xi}^{\prime},L_{\xi}^{\prime\prime},L_{R},L_{R}^{\prime})

=poly(r,L_{\beta}^{\prime},L_{\xi},L_{\xi}^{\prime},L_{\xi}^{\prime\prime},L_{R},L_{R}^{\prime})
. By Lemma
17
and some algebra, we verify that there exists
{\mathcal{C}}_{2}
depending polynomially on
\log N
(and polynomially on the various Lipschitz parameters) such that
T=K\delta\leq{\mathcal{C}}_{2}
satisfies the requirement for Theorem
2
. Finally, let

\displaystyle\hat{y}_{(i+1)K}:={\Phi}(K\delta;y_{iK},E^{iK},\beta,\bar{\mathbf{B}}^{iK})

By Lemma
4

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{A_{iK}\right\}}\mathrm{d}{(\hat{y}_{(i+1)K},\bar{y}_{(i+1)K})}}\right]\leq

\displaystyle 2^{9}{(T^{2}(L_{\beta}^{\prime}r)^{2}+T^{2}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}+T^{3/2}{(d^{3/2}{(L_{R}+{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}/{L_{\beta}^{2}})}+L_{\beta}^{\prime}\sqrt{d})})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O{({(K\delta)}^{3/2})}

. Applying triangle inequality,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{A_{iK}\right\}}\mathrm{d}{(y_{(i+1)K},\hat{y}_{(i+1)K})}}\right]=\tilde{O}{({(K\delta)}^{3/2})}

Step 2: Contraction under Exact Diffusion

Let
f
be as defined in (
2
). By Lemma
3
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{A_{iK}\right\}}f{(\mathrm{d}{(x_{(i+1)K},\mathrm{d}{(\hat{y}_{(i+1)K})})})}}\right]\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{A_{iK}\right\}}\exp{(-\alpha K\delta)}f{(\mathrm{d}{(x_{iK},y_{iK})})}}\right]

Combined with our previous bound on
\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{A_{iK}\mathrm{d}{(y_{(i+1)K},\hat{y}_{(i+1)K})}\right\}}}\right]
, as well as the fact that
{\left|f^{\prime}\right|}\leq 1
from Lemma
10
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{A_{iK}\right\}}f{(\mathrm{d}{(x_{(i+1)K},{y}_{(i+1)K})})}}\right]\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{A_{iK}\right\}}\exp{(-\alpha K\delta)}f{(\mathrm{d}{(x_{iK},y_{iK})})}}\right]+\tilde{O}{({(K\delta)}^{3/2})}

Applying the above recursively,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{A_{NK}\right\}}f{(\mathrm{d}{(x_{NK},{y}_{NK})})}}\right]\leq

\displaystyle\exp{(-\alpha NK\delta)}f{(\mathrm{d}{(x_{0},y_{0})})}+\frac{1}{\alpha}\tilde{O}{({(K\delta)}^{1/2})}

(C.9)

Step 3: Tail Bound

We now consider the low probability event that
A_{NK}
fails to hold (i.e. the iterate
y_{k}
was at some point further than distance
r
from
x^{*}
). Specifically, our goal is to verify the following bound:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{A^{c}_{NK}\right\}}\mathrm{d}{({y}_{NK},x_{NK})}}\right]\leq O{((K\delta)^{2})}

(C.10)

By Lemma
36
and Lemma
29
and Lemma
30
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{A_{k}^{c}\right\}}}\right]=\mathbb{P}{({A_{k}^{c}})}\leq 32k\delta m\exp{(\frac{m\mathcal{R}^{2}}{L_{\xi}^{2}}+\frac{2L_{R}L_{\xi}^{2}}{m}-\frac{mt^{2}}{32L_{\xi}^{2}})}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}}\right]\leq\frac{2^{12}L_{R}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{4}d^{2}}{m^{6}}+\frac{9{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}\mathcal{R}^{2}}{m}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(y_{k},x^{*})}^{2}}\right]\leq\frac{2^{12}L_{R}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{4}L_{\xi}^{2}}{m^{6}}+\frac{9{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}\mathcal{R}^{2}}{m}

By our definition of
r
and applying Cauchy Schwarz, we verify that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{A_{K}^{c}\right\}}\mathrm{d}{(y_{K},x_{K})}}\right]\leq O{(\delta)}\leq O(K\delta)^{2})

(C.11)

Note that Lemma
36
requires
\delta\leq\min\left\{\frac{m}{16{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}{\sqrt{L_{R}}r}}\right\}
. Using Lemma
17
, there exists
{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}=poly{(\mathcal{R},L_{R},L_{\xi},d,\frac{1}{m},L_{\beta}^{\prime},\log N)}
such that
\delta\leq{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}
implies the desired bound.

Combining (
C.9
) and (
C.11
), and using the fact that
\frac{1}{2}\exp{(-{(q+L_{\Ric})}\mathcal{R}^{2}/2)}r\leq f(r)\leq r
and the fact that
\delta=(K\delta)^{3}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x_{NK},{y}_{NK})}}\right]\leq

\displaystyle\exp{(-\alpha NK\delta+{(q+L_{Ric})}\mathcal{R}^{2})}\mathrm{d}{(x_{0},y_{0})}+\exp{({(q+L_{Ric})}\mathcal{R}^{2})}\cdot\tilde{O}{(\delta^{1/6})}

where
\tilde{O}
hides polynomial dependency on
L_{R},L_{R}^{\prime},L_{\beta}^{\prime},L_{\xi},L_{\xi}^{\prime},L_{\xi}^{\prime\prime},\log N,\log{(\frac{1}{\delta})}
.
∎

C.2
Tangent Space Approximation

We define an approximation to (
4.1
), where each step is a "straight line" in the tangent space
T_{y_{0}}M
. We will define a constant, which will be useful throughout the proof.

\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}_{r}:=\frac{1}{16}\min\left\{{L_{R}^{\prime}}^{-1/3},\frac{1}{8\sqrt{L_{R}}}\right\}

(C.12)

Definition 3
.

Given a deterministic vector field
\beta
, a random vector field
\xi
, an initial point
y\in M
, we define

\displaystyle\tilde{\Psi}(\delta;y,z,\beta,\xi):=z+\delta\beta(y)+\Gamma_{\Exp_{y}(z)}^{y}{(\xi{(\Exp_{y}(z))})}

(C.13)

where the parallel transport is along the geodesic
\gamma(t)=\Exp_{y}{(tz)}
.

We will be interested in the sequence

\displaystyle\tilde{z}_{0}=0

\displaystyle\tilde{z}_{k+1}=\tilde{\Psi}{(\delta;y_{0},\tilde{z}_{k},\beta,\xi_{k})}

(C.14)

Define
\tilde{y}_{k}:=\Exp_{y_{0}}{(z_{k})}
. We will show that
\tilde{y}_{k}
is a good approximation to

\displaystyle y_{k+1}=\Psi(\delta;y_{k},\beta,\xi_{k})

(C.15)

for
\Psi
as defined in (
4.1
). It will help to write (
C.13
) in the following equivalent form:

\displaystyle\tilde{y}_{0}=y_{0}\qquad\tilde{z}_{0}=0

\displaystyle\gamma_{k}(t)=\Exp_{\tilde{y}_{0}}{((1-t)\tilde{z}_{k})}

\displaystyle\tilde{z}_{k+1}=\tilde{z}_{k}+\delta\beta(y_{0})+\sqrt{\delta}\Gamma_{\gamma_{k}(t)}{(\xi_{k}(\tilde{y}_{k}))}

\displaystyle\tilde{y}_{k+1}=\Exp_{y_{0}}{(\tilde{z}_{k+1})}

(C.16)

Note that by definition, for all
k
,
\gamma_{k}(t):[0,1]\to M
is a geodesic, with
\gamma_{k}(0)=\tilde{y}_{k}
and
\gamma_{k}(1)=\tilde{y}_{0}
.

The main goal of this subsection is to prove the following approximation bound between (
C.15
) and (
C.16
).

Corollary 15
.

Let
y_{0}
be some initial point. For
i\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}
, let
\xi_{i}
be independent samples of some random function
\xi
. Let
\beta
be a vector field satisfying Assumption
2
, in addition, assume that there exists a constant
L_{\beta}
, such that for all
x\in M
,
{\left\|\beta(x)\right\|}\leq L_{\beta}
. Assume
\xi
satisfies (
7
) and assume that there exists
L_{\xi}
such that
{\left\|\xi(x)\right\|}\leq L_{\xi}
for all
x
. Let
\tilde{z}_{0}:=0
. Define

\displaystyle y_{k+1}=\Psi(\delta;y_{k},\beta,\xi_{k})

\displaystyle\tilde{z}_{k+1}=\tilde{\Psi}{(\delta;y_{0},\tilde{z}_{k},\beta,\xi_{k})}

\displaystyle\tilde{y}_{k+1}=\Exp_{y_{0}}{(\tilde{z}_{k+1})}

with
\tilde{y}_{0}=y_{0}
, where
\Psi
and
\tilde{\Psi}
are as defined in (
4.1
) and (
C.13
) respectively. Let
K,\delta
satisfy

\displaystyle\delta\leq\min\left\{\frac{{\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{2}}{4L_{\xi}^{2}}\right\}

\displaystyle K\delta\leq\min\left\{\frac{1}{64L_{\beta}^{\prime}},\frac{1}{64{L_{\xi}^{\prime}}^{2}},\frac{1}{64L_{\xi}^{2}},\frac{1}{16\sqrt{L_{R}}L_{\beta}},\frac{1}{256L_{R}L_{\xi}^{2}},\frac{{\mathcal{C}}_{r}}{16L_{\beta}},\frac{{\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{2}}{1024L_{R}L_{\xi}^{2}\log{(L_{R}/{\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{2})}}\right\}

(C.17)

where
{\mathcal{C}}_{r}
is as defined in (
C.12
).
Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(\tilde{y}_{K},y_{K})}}\right]\leq\tilde{O}{({(K\delta)}^{3/2}{(1+L_{\beta}^{2})})}

where
\tilde{O}
hides polynomial dependency on
L_{R},L_{R}^{\prime},L_{\beta}^{\prime},L_{\xi},\log\frac{1}{K\delta}
.

Proof of Corollary
15
.

Let us first define a radius

\displaystyle r_{1}:=\sqrt{64K^{2}\delta^{2}L_{\beta}^{2}+256K\delta L_{\xi}^{2}\log{(\frac{1}{32K\delta L_{R}L_{\xi}^{2}})}}

(C.18)

We verify that assumptions on
K,\delta
and the definition of
r_{1}
satisfy the conditions for Lemma
16
, which we need shortly. In particular, we wish to verify that
r_{1}\leq{\mathcal{C}}_{r}
. By (
C.17
),
64K^{2}\delta^{2}L_{\beta}^{2}\leq{\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{2}/2
, so that it suffices to verify
256K\delta L_{\xi}^{2}\log{(\frac{1}{32K\delta L_{R}L_{\xi}^{2}})}\leq\frac{{\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{2}}{2}
, moving terms around, this is equivalent to
\frac{1}{32K\delta L_{R}L_{\xi}^{2}\log{(\frac{1}{32K\delta L_{R}L_{\xi}^{2}})}}\geq\frac{4L_{R}}{{\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{2}}
. Notice from definition that
L_{R}/{\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{2}\leq 1
, so that we can apply Lemma
17
, which guarantees that this holds as along as
\frac{1}{32K\delta L_{R}L_{\xi}^{2}}\geq\frac{12L_{R}}{{\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{2}}\log{(\frac{4L_{R}}{{\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{2}})}
, this is guaranteed by our assumption that
K\delta\leq\frac{{\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{2}}{1024L_{R}L_{\xi}^{2}\log{(L_{R}/{\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{2})}}

We can decompose
\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(\tilde{y}_{K},y_{K})}^{2}}\right]
as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(\tilde{y}_{K},y_{K})}^{2}}\right]\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{A_{K}\right\}}\mathrm{d}{(\tilde{y}_{K},y_{K})}^{2}}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{A_{K}^{c}\right\}}\mathrm{d}{({\tilde{y}}_{K},y_{k})}^{2}}\right]

To bound the first term, we simply apply the result of Lemma
16
recursively over
k=0...K-1
.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{A_{K}\right\}}\mathrm{d}{(\tilde{y}_{K},y_{K})}^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{A_{K-1}\right\}}\mathrm{d}{(\tilde{y}_{K},y_{K})}^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{(1+\frac{1}{K})}\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{A_{K-2}\right\}}\mathrm{d}{(y_{K-1},\tilde{y}_{K-1})}^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+{(9K\delta^{2}L_{R}^{2}r_{1}^{4}L_{\beta}^{2}+9K\delta^{2}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}r_{1}^{2}+8\delta L_{R}^{2}r_{1}^{4}L_{\xi}^{2}+2^{27}K{({L_{R}^{\prime}}^{2}r_{1}^{4}+L_{R}^{2}r_{1}^{2})}{(\delta^{4}L_{\beta}^{4}+\delta^{2}L_{\xi}^{4})})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{(1+\frac{1}{K})}\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{A_{K-2}\right\}}\mathrm{d}{(y_{K-1},\tilde{y}_{K-1})}^{2}}\right]+\tilde{O}{(K^{2}\delta^{3}{(1+L_{\beta}^{2})})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle...

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tilde{O}{({(K\delta)}^{3}{(1+L_{\beta}^{2})})}

the first inequality is because
{\mathbbm{1}\left\{A_{K}\right\}}\leq{\mathbbm{1}\left\{A_{k-1}\right\}}
for all
k
, the third inequality is because
r_{1}=\tilde{O}{(K\delta)}
. The
\tilde{O}()
hides polynomial dependency on
L_{R},L_{R}^{\prime},L_{\beta}^{\prime},L_{\xi},\log\frac{1}{K\delta}
.

To bound the second term, we first bound the probability of
A_{K}^{c}
. We can apply Lemma
32
: let
E
denote any orthonormal basis of
T_{y_{0}}M
, let
\mathbf{v}
denote the coordinates of
\beta(y_{0})
wrt
E
, and
{\mathbf{H}}_{k}
denote the coordinates of
\Gamma_{\tilde{y}_{k}}^{y_{0}}\xi_{k}(\tilde{y}_{k})
. We verify that
\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbf{H}}_{k}}\right]=0
,
\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbf{H}}_{k}{\mathbf{H}}_{k}^{T}}\right]=I
(using Assumption
7
together with Lemma
67
), and that
{\left\|{\mathbf{H}}_{k}(x)\right\|}\leq L_{{\mathbf{H}}}:=L_{\xi}
for all
x
. Let
\mathbf{z}_{k}
denote the coordinates of
\tilde{z}_{k}
wrt
E
. Finally, we verify that
{\left\|\mathbf{v}\right\|}_{2}\leq\delta{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}\mathrm{d}{(y_{0},x^{*})}
. Thus by Lemma
32
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{k\leq K}{\left\|{\tilde{z}}_{k}\right\|}\geq t})}=\mathbb{P}{({\max_{k\leq K}{\left\|\mathbf{z}_{k}\right\|}_{2}\geq t})}\leq\exp{(\frac{{64K^{2}\delta^{2}L_{\beta}^{2}+8K\delta L_{\xi}^{2}}-t^{2}}{128K\delta L_{\xi}^{2}})}

Note that Lemma
32
explicitly deals with
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, but it also works for
\tilde{z}
which lies in
T_{y_{0}}M
which is isometric to
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Similarly, by Lemma
31
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{k\leq K}\mathrm{d}{(y_{k},y_{0})}\geq t})}\leq\exp{(\frac{32K^{2}\delta^{2}{L_{\beta}}^{2}+8K\delta L_{\xi}^{2}-t^{2}}{128K\delta L_{\xi}^{2}})}

By union bound,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({A_{k}})}\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{k\leq K}{\left\|{\tilde{z}}_{k}\right\|}\geq{r}_{1}})}+\mathbb{P}{({\max_{k\leq K}\mathrm{d}{(y_{k},y_{0})}\geq{r}_{1}})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\exp{(\frac{64K^{2}\delta^{2}L_{\beta}^{2}+8K\delta L_{\xi}^{2}-{r}_{1}^{2}}{128K\delta L_{\xi}^{2}})}

By Lemma
26
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(y_{k},y_{0})}^{2}}\right]\leq

\displaystyle\exp{(1+8K\delta L_{\beta}^{\prime}+K\delta L_{R}L_{\xi}^{2}+K\delta^{2}L_{R}L_{\beta}^{2})}\cdot{(2K\delta L_{\xi}^{2}+8K^{2}\delta^{2}L_{\beta}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 16{(K\delta L_{\xi}^{2}+K^{2}\delta^{2}L_{\beta}^{2})}

From the definition of
{\left\|\tilde{z}_{k}\right\|}
, we can also bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|\tilde{z}_{k}\right\|}^{2}}\right]\leq

\displaystyle 2{(K^{2}\delta^{2}L_{\beta}^{2}+K\delta L_{\xi}^{2})}

We can therefore bound using Cauchy Schwarz and triangle inequality

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{A_{K}^{c}\right\}}\mathrm{d}{(\tilde{y}_{K},y_{K})}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 32\exp{(\frac{64K^{2}\delta^{2}L_{\beta}^{2}+8K\delta L_{\xi}^{2}-{r}_{1}^{2}}{256K\delta L_{\xi}^{2}})}\sqrt{K^{2}\delta^{2}L_{\beta}^{2}+K\delta L_{\xi}^{2}}

From our choice of
r_{1}
, we verify that

\displaystyle r_{1}\geq\sqrt{64K^{2}\delta^{2}L_{\beta}^{2}+8K\delta L_{\xi}^{2}+128K\delta L_{\xi}^{2}\log{(\frac{1}{32K\delta L_{R}L_{\xi}^{2}})}}

Note that we use the fact that
\log{(\frac{1}{32K\delta L_{R}L_{\xi}^{2}})}\geq\log{(8)}\geq 1
, using the upper bound on
K\delta
in the assumption in (
C.17
). It follows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{A_{K}^{c}\right\}}\mathrm{d}{(\tilde{y}_{K},y_{K})}}\right]\leq{(K\delta)}^{3/2}{(L_{\beta}\sqrt{L_{R}}L_{\xi}+L_{R}L_{\xi}^{3})}={O}{({(K\delta)}^{3}{(1+L_{\beta}^{2})})}

Combining the above with our earlier bound that
\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{A_{K}\right\}}\mathrm{d}{(\tilde{y}_{K},y_{K})}^{2}}\right]\leq\tilde{O}{({(K\delta)}^{3}{(1+L_{\beta}^{2})})}
gives the desired bound in the Lemma.

∎

Lemma 16
.

Let
y_{0}
be some initial point. For
i\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}
, let
\xi_{i}
be independent samples of some random function
\xi
satisfying
{\left\|\xi(x)\right\|}\leq L_{\xi}
for all
x
. Let
\beta
be a vector field satisfying Assumption
2
, in addition, assume that there exists a constant
L_{\beta}
, such that for all
x\in M
,
{\left\|\beta(x)\right\|}\leq L_{\beta}
. Let
\tilde{z}_{0}:=0
. Define the two stochastic processes

\displaystyle y_{k+1}=\Psi{(\delta,y_{k},\beta,\xi_{k})}

\displaystyle\tilde{z}_{k+1}=\tilde{\Psi}{(\delta;y_{0},\tilde{z}_{k},\beta,\xi_{k})}

\displaystyle\tilde{y}_{k+1}=\Exp_{y_{0}}{(\tilde{z}_{k+1})}

where
\Psi
and
\tilde{y}_{k}=\tilde{\Psi}
are as defined in (
4.1
) and (
C.13
) respectively. Let
{r}_{1},K,\delta
satisfy

\displaystyle\delta\leq\min\left\{\frac{{\mathcal{C}}_{r}}{2L_{\beta}},\frac{{\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{2}}{4L_{\xi}^{2}}\right\}

\displaystyle K\delta\leq\min\left\{\frac{1}{64L_{\beta}^{\prime}},\frac{1}{64{L_{\xi}^{\prime}}^{2}},\frac{1}{16\sqrt{L_{R}}L_{\beta}},\frac{1}{256L_{R}L_{\xi}^{2}}\right\}

\displaystyle{r}_{1}\leq{\mathcal{C}}_{r}

(C.19)

where
{\mathcal{C}}_{r}
is defined in (
C.12
).

Let
{A_{k}}
