\displaystyle\quad+{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}\leq\frac{1}{\delta\sqrt{L_{R}}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}}\right\}}{(-2\bm{\left\langle}\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k}),\Exp_{x_{k}}^{-1}(x_{0})\bm{})}

Squaring both sides,

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(x_{k+1},x_{0})}^{4}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{(1+16\delta L_{\beta}^{\prime}+3\delta L_{R}{\left\|\xi_{k}{(x_{k})}\right\|}^{2}+3\delta^{2}L_{R}L_{0}^{2}+2\delta^{2}L_{R}^{2}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{4}+\frac{2}{K})}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}^{4}

\displaystyle\quad+5K\delta^{2}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{4}+64K^{3}\delta^{4}L_{0}^{4}

\displaystyle\quad+\hbox to12.21pt{\vbox to12.21pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 6.10283pt\lower-6.10283pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 8.17 0 C 8.17 4.51 4.51 8.17 0 8.17 C -4.51 8.17 -8.17 4.51 -8.17 0 C -8.17 -4.51 -4.51 -8.17 0 -8.17 C 4.51 -8.17 8.17 -4.51 8.17 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.5pt}{-3.75pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}

(D.6)

where
*
has
0
-mean, and we used a few times Cauchy Schwarz and Young’s Inequality.

Taking expectation wrt
{\mathcal{F}}_{k}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{{\mathcal{F}}_{k}}}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x_{k+1},x_{0})}^{4}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{(1+16\delta L_{\beta}^{\prime}+3\delta L_{R}d+3\delta^{2}L_{R}L_{0}^{2}+2\delta^{2}L_{R}^{2}d^{2}+\frac{2}{K})}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}^{4}+5K\delta^{2}d^{2}+64K^{3}\delta^{4}L_{0}^{4}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{(1+16\delta L_{\beta}^{\prime}+4\delta L_{R}d+3\delta^{2}L_{R}L_{0}^{2}+\frac{2}{K})}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}^{4}+5K\delta^{2}d^{2}+64K^{3}\delta^{4}L_{0}^{4}

Applying the above recursively,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x_{K},x_{0})}^{4}}\right]\leq

\displaystyle\exp{(2+16K\delta L_{\beta}^{\prime}+4K\delta L_{R}d+3K\delta^{2}L_{R}L_{0}^{2})}{(5K^{2}\delta^{2}d^{2}+64K^{4}\delta^{4}L_{0}^{4})}

To prove the second claim, define

\displaystyle r_{0}^{4}:=0

\displaystyle r_{k+1}^{4}:={(1+16\delta L_{\beta}^{\prime}+3\delta L_{R}{\left\|\xi_{k}{(x_{k})}\right\|}^{2}+3\delta^{2}L_{R}L_{0}^{2}+2\delta^{2}L_{R}^{2}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{4}+\frac{2}{K})}r_{k}^{4}

\displaystyle\quad+5K\delta^{2}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{4}+64K^{3}\delta^{4}L_{0}^{4}

\displaystyle\quad+\hbox to12.21pt{\vbox to12.21pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 6.10283pt\lower-6.10283pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 8.17 0 C 8.17 4.51 4.51 8.17 0 8.17 C -4.51 8.17 -8.17 4.51 -8.17 0 C -8.17 -4.51 -4.51 -8.17 0 -8.17 C 4.51 -8.17 8.17 -4.51 8.17 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.5pt}{-3.75pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}

where
*
is the same term as (
D.6
).

We verify that
r_{k}
as defined above is a sub-martingale. Thus by Doob’s martingale inequality,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{k\leq K}r_{k}^{2}\geq s})}\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[{r_{K}^{2}}\right]}{s}

Furthermore, notice that

\displaystyle r_{0}^{2}=

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(x_{0},x_{0})}^{2}=0

\displaystyle r_{k+1}^{2}-\mathrm{d}{(x_{k+1}^{2},x_{0})}^{2}\geq

\displaystyle{(1+16\delta L_{\beta}^{\prime}+3\delta L_{R}{\left\|\xi_{k}{(x_{k})}\right\|}^{2}+3\delta^{2}L_{R}L_{0}^{2}+2\delta^{2}L_{R}^{2}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{4}+\frac{2}{K})}{(r_{k}^{2}-\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}^{2})}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 0

so that
r_{k}\geq\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}
with probability 1, for all
k
.

Thus

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{k\leq K}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}\geq s})}\leq\mathbb{P}{({\max_{k\leq K}r_{k}\geq s})}\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[{r_{K}^{4}}\right]}{s^{4}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{s^{4}}\exp{(2+16K\delta L_{\beta}^{\prime}+4K\delta L_{R}d+3K\delta^{2}L_{R}L_{0}^{2})}{(5K^{2}\delta^{2}d^{2}+64K^{4}\delta^{4}L_{0}^{4})}

The proof for the bound on
r_{K}^{2}
is identical to the proof of the first claim. We conclude our proof of the second claim
∎

D.2.2
Under Dissipativity

Lemma 28

(L2 Bound and Chevyshev under Dissipativity, Discretized SDE)
.

Let
\beta
be a vector field satisfying
2
. Let
x^{*}
be some point with
\beta(x^{*})=0
. Assume that for all
x
such that
\mathrm{d}{(x,x^{*})}\geq\mathcal{R}
, there exists a minimizing geodesic
\gamma:[0,1]\to M
with
\gamma(0)=x,\gamma(1)=x^{*}
, and

\displaystyle\bm{\left\langle}\beta(x),\gamma^{\prime}(0)\bm{}\leq-m\mathrm{d}{(x,x^{*})}^{2}

. Assume in addition that
\delta\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
satisfies
\delta\leq{\frac{m}{128{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}}}

Let
x_{k}
be the following stochastic process:

\displaystyle x_{k+1}=\Exp_{x_{k}}{(\delta\beta(x_{k})+\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k}))}

where
\xi_{k}(x_{k})\sim\mathcal{N}_{x_{k}}{(0,I)}

For any
k
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}}\right]\leq

\displaystyle\exp{(-k\delta m)}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x_{0},x^{*})}^{2}}\right]+\frac{1-{(1-\delta m)}^{k}}{\delta m}{(\frac{2048\delta L_{R}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{4}d^{4}}{m^{5}}+4\delta{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}\mathcal{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp{(-k\delta m)}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x_{0},x^{*})}^{2}}\right]+{\frac{2048L_{R}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{4}d^{2}}{m^{6}}+\frac{4{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}\mathcal{R}^{2}}{m}}

Proof.

In case the minimizing geodesic from
x_{k}
to
x^{*}
is not unique, we let
\Exp_{x_{k}}^{-1}(x^{*})
be any arbitrary (but consistently fixed) choice that satisfies
\bm{\left\langle}\beta(x_{k}),\Exp_{x_{k}}^{-1}(x^{*})\bm{}\leq-m\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}

Let us define
r_{k}:=\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}
. From Lemma
25
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(x_{k+1},x^{*})}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle{(1-\delta m)}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}+{\frac{2048\delta L_{R}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{4}}{m^{5}}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{4}+4\delta{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}\mathcal{R}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}\leq\frac{m}{4\delta\sqrt{L_{R}}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}}\right\}}{(-2\bm{\left\langle}\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k}),\Exp_{x_{k}}^{-1}(x^{*})\bm{})}

Taking expectation,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x_{k+1},x^{*})}^{2}}\right]\leq

\displaystyle{(1-\delta m)}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}}\right]+\frac{2048\delta L_{R}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{4}d^{2}}{m^{5}}+4\delta{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}\mathcal{R}^{2}

Applying the above recursively,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}}\right]\leq

\displaystyle{(1-\delta m)}^{k}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x_{0},x^{*})}^{2}}\right]+{(\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}{(1-\delta m)}^{k-i})}{(\frac{2048\delta L_{R}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{4}d^{2}}{m^{5}}+4\delta{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}\mathcal{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp{(-k\delta m)}+\frac{1-{(1-\delta m)}^{k}}{\delta m}{(\frac{2048\delta L_{R}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{4}d^{2}}{m^{5}}+4\delta{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}\mathcal{R}^{2})}

∎

Lemma 29

(L2 Bound under Dissipativity, Exact SDE)
.

Let
\beta
be a vector field satisfying
2
. Let
x^{*}
be some point with
\beta(x^{*})=0
. Assume that for all
x
such that
\mathrm{d}{(x,x^{*})}\geq\mathcal{R}
, there exists a minimizing geodesic
\gamma:[0,1]\to M
with
\gamma(0)=x,\gamma(1)=x^{*}
, and

\displaystyle\bm{\left\langle}\beta(x),\gamma^{\prime}(0)\bm{}\leq-m\mathrm{d}{(x,x^{*})}^{2}

. Assume in addition that
\delta\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
satisfies
\delta\leq{\frac{m}{128{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}}}

Let
x_{k}
be the following stochastic process:

\displaystyle x_{k+1}=\Phi{(\delta,x_{k},{E^{k}},\beta,\mathbf{B}_{k})}

where for each
k
,
{E^{k}}
is some orthonormal basis at
x_{k}
,
\mathbf{B}_{k}
is a
\mathbb{R}^{d}
Brownian Motion, and
\Phi
is as defined in (
3.4
).
For any
k
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}^{2}}\right]\leq\exp{(-\delta m)}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x_{0},x^{*})}^{2}}\right]+\frac{2^{12}L_{R}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{4}d^{2}}{m^{6}}+\frac{8{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}\mathcal{R}^{2}}{m}

Proof.

Consider fixed
k
. Let us define, for
t\in[0,\delta]
,

\displaystyle x^{i,k}(t):=\overline{\Phi}{(\delta,x_{k},{E^{k}},\beta,\mathbf{B}_{k},i)}

Define
x^{i,k}_{j}:=x^{i,k}(j\delta^{i})
, where
\delta^{i}:=\delta/2^{i}
. Notice that by definition,

x^{i,k}_{j+1}=\Exp_{x^{i,k}_{j+1}}{(\delta^{i}\beta(x^{i,k}_{j})+\sqrt{\delta^{i}}\xi_{j}{(x^{i,k}_{j})})}
, for some
\xi_{j}{(x^{i,k}_{j})}\sim\mathcal{N}_{x^{i,k}_{j}}{(0,I)}
. By Lemma
28
, for all
i
, (note that
2^{i}\delta^{i}=\delta
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x^{i,k}_{2^{i}},x^{*})}^{2}}\right]\leq

\displaystyle\exp{(-2^{i}\delta^{i}m)}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x^{i,k}_{0},x^{*})}^{2}}\right]+\frac{1-{(1-\delta^{i}m)}^{2^{i}}}{\delta^{i}m}{(\frac{2048\delta^{i}L_{R}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{4}d^{4}}{m^{5}}+4\delta^{i}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}\mathcal{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp{(-\delta m)}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x^{i,k}_{0},x^{*})}^{2}}\right]+\frac{2^{i+1}\delta^{i}m}{\delta^{i}m}{(\frac{2048\delta^{i}L_{R}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{4}d^{4}}{m^{5}}+4\delta^{i}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}\mathcal{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp{(-\delta m)}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x^{i,k}_{0},x^{*})}^{2}}\right]+\delta{(\frac{2^{12}L_{R}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{4}d^{4}}{m^{5}}+8{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}\mathcal{R}^{2})}

From Lemma
1
,
x^{i,k}_{2^{i}}=x^{i,k}(\delta)
converges almost surely to
x_{k+1}=\Phi{(\delta,x_{k},{E^{k}},\beta,\mathbf{B}_{k})}
. Thus

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x_{k+1},x^{*})}^{2}}\right]\leq\exp{(-\delta m)}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}}\right]+\delta{(\frac{2^{12}L_{R}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{4}d^{4}}{m^{5}}+8{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}\mathcal{R}^{2})}

The conclusion follows by applying the above recursively up to
k=0
.
∎

Lemma 30

(L2 Bound under Dissipativity, Nongaussian)
.

Let
\beta
be a vector field satisfying
2
. Let
x^{*}
be some point with
\beta(x^{*})=0
. Assume that for all
x
such that
\mathrm{d}{(x,x^{*})}\geq\mathcal{R}
, there exists a minimizing geodesic
\gamma:[0,1]\to M
with
\gamma(0)=x,\gamma(1)=x^{*}
, and

\displaystyle\bm{\left\langle}\beta(x),\gamma^{\prime}(0)\bm{}\leq-m\mathrm{d}{(x,x^{*})}^{2}

. Assume in addition that
\delta\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
satisfies
\delta\leq{\frac{m}{128{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}}}

Let
x_{k}
be the following stochastic process:

\displaystyle x_{k+1}=\Exp_{x_{k}}{(\delta\beta(x_{k})+\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k}))}

where
\xi_{k}
is some random vector field with
{\left\|\xi(x)\right\|}\leq L_{\xi}
almost surely, for all
x\in M
.

For any
k
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}}\right]\leq

\displaystyle\exp{(-k\delta m)}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x_{0},x^{*})}^{2}}\right]+\frac{1-{(1-\delta m)}^{k}}{\delta m}{(\frac{2048\delta L_{R}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{4}L_{\xi}^{4}}{m^{5}}+4\delta{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}\mathcal{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp{(-k\delta m)}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x_{0},x^{*})}^{2}}\right]+{\frac{2048L_{R}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{4}L_{\xi}^{2}}{m^{6}}+\frac{4{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}\mathcal{R}^{2}}{m}}

Proof.

In case the minimizing geodesic from
x_{k}
to
x^{*}
is not unique, we let
\Exp_{x_{k}}^{-1}(x^{*})
be any arbitrary (but consistently fixed) choice that satisfies
\bm{\left\langle}\beta(x_{k}),\Exp_{x_{k}}^{-1}(x^{*})\bm{}\leq-m\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}

Let us define
r_{k}:=\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}
. From Lemma
25
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(x_{k+1},x^{*})}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle{(1-\delta m)}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}+{\frac{2048\delta L_{R}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{4}}{m^{5}}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{4}+4\delta{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}\mathcal{R}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}\leq\frac{m}{4\delta\sqrt{L_{R}}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}}\right\}}{(-2\bm{\left\langle}\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k}),\Exp_{x_{k}}^{-1}(x^{*})\bm{})}

Taking expectation,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x_{k+1},x^{*})}^{2}}\right]\leq

\displaystyle{(1-\delta m)}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}}\right]+\frac{2048\delta L_{R}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{4}L_{\xi}^{2}}{m^{5}}+4\delta{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}\mathcal{R}^{2}

Applying the above recursively,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}}\right]\leq

\displaystyle{(1-\delta m)}^{k}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x_{0},x^{*})}^{2}}\right]+{(\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}{(1-\delta m)}^{k-i})}{(\frac{2048\delta L_{R}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{4}L_{\xi}^{2}}{m^{5}}+4\delta{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}\mathcal{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp{(-k\delta m)}+\frac{1-{(1-\delta m)}^{k}}{\delta m}{(\frac{2048\delta L_{R}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{4}L_{\xi}^{2}}{m^{5}}+4\delta{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}\mathcal{R}^{2})}

∎

D.3
Subgaussian Bounds

D.3.1
Under Lipschitz Continuity

Lemma 31

(Subgaussian Bound under Lipschitz, Nongaussian Noise)
.

Let
\beta
be a vector field satisfying Assumption
2
, and assume that there exists a constant
L_{\beta}
such that
{\left\|\beta(x)\right\|}\leq L_{\beta}
for all
x
. Assume
{\left\|\xi\right\|}\leq L_{\xi}
almost surely. Assume in addition that
K\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}
and
\delta\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
satisfy

\displaystyle K\delta\leq\min\left\{\frac{1}{8\sqrt{L_{R}}L_{\beta}},\frac{1}{64L_{R}L_{\xi}^{2}},\frac{1}{64L_{\xi}^{2}}\right\}

Let
x^{*}
be some point with
\beta(x^{*})=0
. Let
x_{k}
be the following stochastic process:

\displaystyle x_{k+1}=\Exp_{x_{k}}{(\delta\beta(x_{k})+\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k}))}

Then for any
t
, and any
k\leq K
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{k\leq K}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}\geq t})}\leq\exp{(\frac{32K^{2}\delta^{2}{L_{\beta}}^{2}+8K\delta L_{\xi}^{2}-t^{2}}{128K\delta L_{\xi}^{2}})}

Proof of Lemma
31
.

In case the minimizing geodesic from
x_{k}
to
x_{0}
is not unique, we let
\Exp_{x_{k}}^{-1}(x_{0})
be any arbitrary (but consistently fixed) choice that satisfies
\bm{\left\langle}\beta(x_{k}),\Exp_{x_{k}}^{-1}(x_{0})\bm{}\leq-m\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}^{2}
. Using the result from Corollary 8 of
Zhang and Sra 2016
(see Lemma
41
),

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(x_{k+1},x_{0})}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}^{2}{(x_{k},x_{0})}-2\bm{\left\langle}\delta_{k}\beta(x_{k})+\sqrt{\delta_{k}}\xi_{k}(x_{k}),\Exp_{x_{k}}^{-1}(x_{0})\bm{}

\displaystyle\quad+{(1+\sqrt{L_{R}}\mathrm{d}(x_{k},x_{0}))}{\left\|\delta_{k}\beta(x_{k})+\sqrt{\delta_{k}}\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2}

where
\zeta(r):=\frac{r}{\tanh(r)}
.

Expanding and simplifying,

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}^{2}{(x_{k+1},x_{0})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}^{2}{(x_{k},x_{0})}-2\bm{\left\langle}\delta\beta(x_{k})+\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k}),\Exp_{x_{k}}^{-1}(x_{0})\bm{}+{(1+\sqrt{L_{R}}\mathrm{d}(x_{k},x_{0}))}{\left\|\delta\beta(x_{k})+\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}^{2}{(x_{k},x_{0})}-2\bm{\left\langle}\delta\beta(x_{k})+\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k}),\Exp_{x_{k}}^{-1}(x_{0})\bm{}+2\delta^{2}{L_{\beta}}^{2}+2\delta L_{\xi}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+2\sqrt{L_{R}}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}{(\delta^{2}{L_{\beta}}^{2}+\delta L_{\xi}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{(1+2L_{R}{(\delta^{2}L_{\beta}^{2}+\delta L_{\xi}^{2})})}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}^{2}+4\delta^{2}{L_{\beta}}^{2}+4\delta L_{\xi}^{2}+2\delta L_{\beta}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k}.x_{0})}-2\sqrt{\delta}\bm{\left\langle}\xi_{k}(x_{k}),\Exp_{x_{k}}^{-1}(x_{0})\bm{}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{(1+\frac{1}{8K})}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}^{2}+16K\delta^{2}{L_{\beta}}^{2}+4\delta L_{\xi}^{2}\underbrace{-2\sqrt{\delta}\bm{\left\langle}\xi_{k}(x_{k}),\Exp_{x_{k}}^{-1}(x_{0})\bm{}}_{:=\theta_{k}}

(D.7)

Let us now define
s:=\frac{1}{64K\delta L_{\xi}^{2}}
. Using Lemma
38
, we can verify that

\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(s\theta_{k})}}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(2s^{2}\theta_{k}^{2})}}\right]\leq e^{8\delta s^{2}L_{\xi}^{2}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}}\leq e^{\frac{s^{2}}{8K}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}}
. We now apply Lemma
39
, with

\displaystyle q_{k}=s\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}^{2}\qquad\qquad\eta_{k}:=s\theta_{k}

\displaystyle a_{k}=\frac{1}{8K}\qquad\qquad b_{k}=s{(16K\delta^{2}{L_{\beta}}^{2}+4\delta L_{\xi}^{2})}

\displaystyle c_{k}=\frac{s}{8K}\qquad\qquad d_{k}=0

Note also that by definition,
q_{0}=0
. Note also that
\sum a_{k}+c_{k}=\frac{1}{8}
. Lemma
39
then guarantees that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{k\leq K}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}\geq t})}=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{k\leq K}s\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}^{2}\geq st^{2}})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp{(2q_{0}+2K{(b+d)}-\frac{st^{2}}{2})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp{(s{(16K^{2}\delta^{2}{L_{\beta}}^{2}+4K\delta L_{\xi}^{2}-\frac{t^{2}}{2})})}

∎

Lemma 32

(Subgaussian Bound under Lipschitz, Nongaussian Noise, Euclidean Space)
.

Het
{\mathbf{H}}_{k}
be a vector fields satisfying, for all
k
and for all
\mathbf{z}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbf{H}}_{k}(\mathbf{z})}\right]=0\qquad\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbf{H}}_{k}(\mathbf{z}){\mathbf{H}}_{k}(\mathbf{z})^{T}}\right]=I

\displaystyle{\left\|{\mathbf{H}}_{k}(x)\right\|}\leq L_{\mathbf{H}}\qquad\text{almost surely}

Let
\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
be some arbitrary vector. Let
\mathbf{z}_{k}
be the following stochastic process:

\displaystyle\mathbf{z}_{k+1}=\mathbf{z}_{k}+\delta\mathbf{v}+\sqrt{\delta}{\mathbf{H}}_{k}(\mathbf{z}_{k})

Assume
K\delta\leq\frac{1}{64L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}}
, then for any
t
, and any
k\leq K
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{k\leq K}{\left\|\mathbf{z}_{k}\right\|}_{2}\geq t})}\leq\exp{(\frac{{32K^{2}\delta^{2}{\left\|\mathbf{v}\right\|}_{2}^{2}+4K\delta L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}}-\frac{t^{2}}{2}}{64K\delta L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}})}

Proof of Lemma
32
.

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{z}_{k+1}\right\|}_{2}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{z}_{k}\right\|}_{2}^{2}+2\bm{\left\langle}\mathbf{z}_{k},\delta\mathbf{v}+\sqrt{\delta}{\mathbf{H}}_{k}(\mathbf{z}_{k})\bm{}+2\delta^{2}{\left\|\mathbf{v}\right\|}_{2}^{2}+2\delta{\left\|{\mathbf{H}}_{k}(\mathbf{z}_{k})\right\|}_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{(1+\frac{1}{8K})}{\left\|\mathbf{z}_{k}\right\|}_{2}^{2}+16K\delta^{2}{\left\|\mathbf{v}\right\|}_{2}^{2}+2\delta L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}+2\bm{\left\langle}\mathbf{z}_{k},\sqrt{\delta}{\mathbf{H}}_{k}(\mathbf{z}_{k})\bm{}

Let us now define
s:=\frac{1}{64K\delta L_{{\mathbf{H}}}^{2}}
and
\eta_{k}:=2s\bm{\left\langle}\mathbf{z}_{k},\sqrt{\delta}{\mathbf{H}}_{k}(\mathbf{z}_{k})\bm{}
. We verify that
\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(s\eta_{k})}}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(2s^{2}\eta_{k}^{2})}}\right]\leq e^{8\delta s^{2}L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}{\left\|\mathbf{z}_{k}\right\|}_{2}^{2}}\leq e^{\frac{s^{2}}{8K}{\left\|\mathbf{z}_{k}\right\|}_{2}^{2}}
.

We now apply Lemma
39
, with

\displaystyle q_{k}=s{\left\|\mathbf{z}_{k}\right\|}_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle a_{k}=\frac{1}{8K}\qquad\qquad b_{k}=s{(16K\delta^{2}{\left\|\mathbf{v}\right\|}_{2}^{2}+2\delta L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2})}

\displaystyle c_{k}=\frac{s}{8K}\qquad\qquad d_{k}=0

we can thus bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{k\leq K}q_{k}\geq st^{2}})}\leq\exp{(s{(32K^{2}\delta^{2}{\left\|\mathbf{v}\right\|}_{2}^{2}+4K\delta L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2})}-\frac{st^{2}}{2})}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\qquad

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{k\leq K}{\left\|\mathbf{z}_{k}\right\|}_{2}\geq t})}\leq\exp{(\frac{{32K^{2}\delta^{2}{\left\|\mathbf{v}\right\|}_{2}^{2}+4K\delta L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}}-\frac{t^{2}}{2}}{64K\delta L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}})}

∎

D.3.2
Under Dissipativity

Lemma 33

(Subgaussian Bound under Dissipativity, Discretized SDE, adaptive stepsize)
.

Let
\beta
be a vector field satisfying
2
. Let
x^{*}
be some point with
\beta(x^{*})=0
. Assume that for all
x
such that
\mathrm{d}{(x,x^{*})}\geq\mathcal{R}
, there exists a minimizing geodesic
\gamma:[0,1]\to M
with
\gamma(0)=x,\gamma(1)=x^{*}
, and

\displaystyle\bm{\left\langle}\beta(x),\gamma^{\prime}(0)\bm{}\leq-m\mathrm{d}{(x,x^{*})}^{2}

.
Let
x_{k}
be the following stochastic process:

\displaystyle x_{k+1}=\Exp_{x_{k}}{(\delta_{k}\beta(x_{k})+\sqrt{\delta_{k}}\xi_{k}(x_{k}))}

where
\xi_{k}(x_{k})\sim\mathcal{N}_{x_{k}}{(0,I)}
and for each
k
,
\delta_{k}
is a positive stepsize that depends only on
x_{k}
and satisfies

\delta_{k}\leq\min\left\{\frac{m}{16{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}{(1+\sqrt{L_{R}}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})})}},\frac{d}{4m{(1+\sqrt{L_{R}}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})})}}\right\}
. Assume that
\mathrm{d}{(x_{0},x^{*})}\leq 2\mathcal{R}
. Finally, assume that there exists
\delta\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
such that for all
k
,
\delta_{k}\leq\delta
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{i\leq K}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}\geq t})}\leq 8K\delta m\exp{(1+\frac{m\mathcal{R}^{2}}{d}+\frac{2L_{R}d}{m}-\frac{mt^{2}}{32d})}

Proof.

In case the minimizing geodesic from
x_{k}
to
x^{*}
is not unique, we let
\Exp_{x_{k}}^{-1}(x^{*})
be any arbitrary (but consistently fixed) choice that satisfies
\bm{\left\langle}\beta(x_{k}),\Exp_{x_{k}}^{-1}(x^{*})\bm{}\leq-m\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}
. Using the result from Corollary 8 of
Zhang and Sra 2016
(see Lemma
41
),

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(x_{k+1},x^{*})}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}^{2}{(x_{k},x^{*})}-2\bm{\left\langle}\delta_{k}\beta(x_{k})+\sqrt{\delta_{k}}\xi_{k}(x_{k}),\Exp_{x_{k}}^{-1}(x^{*})\bm{}

\displaystyle\quad+{(1+\sqrt{L_{R}}\mathrm{d}(x_{k},x^{*}))}{\left\|\delta_{k}\beta(x_{k})+\sqrt{\delta_{k}}\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2}

By our assumption,
\bm{\left\langle}\beta(x_{k}),\Exp_{x_{k}}^{-1}(x^{*})\bm{}\leq-m\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}+m\mathcal{R}^{2}
. Applying Cauchy Schwarz and simplifying,

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(x_{k+1},x^{*})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{(-2m\delta_{k}+2\delta_{k}^{2}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}+2\delta_{k}^{2}\sqrt{L_{R}}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})})}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}+2\delta_{k}m\mathcal{R}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+2\sqrt{\delta_{k}}\bm{\left\langle}\xi_{k}(x_{k}),\Exp_{x_{k}}^{-1}(x^{*})\bm{}+2\delta_{k}{(1+\sqrt{L_{R}}\mathrm{d}(x_{k},x^{*}))}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\delta_{k}m\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}+2\delta_{k}m\mathcal{R}^{2}+2\sqrt{\delta_{k}}\bm{\left\langle}\xi_{k}(x_{k}),\Exp_{x_{k}}^{-1}(x^{*})\bm{}+2\delta_{k}{(1+\sqrt{L_{R}}\mathrm{d}(x_{k},x^{*}))}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2}

where we used our assumption that
\delta_{k}\leq\frac{m}{16{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}{(1+\sqrt{L_{R}}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})})}}
.

Let
s:=\frac{m}{32d}
We will now apply Lemma
40
with

\displaystyle q_{k}=s\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}\qquad\xi_{k}=s\bm{\left\langle}\xi_{k}(x_{k}),\Exp_{x_{k}}^{-1}(x^{*})\bm{}+2\sqrt{\delta_{k}}s{(1+\sqrt{L_{R}}\mathrm{d}(x_{k},x^{*}))}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2}

\displaystyle\lambda=m\qquad\gamma=2sm\mathcal{R}^{2}\qquad\mu=4sd+\frac{8sL_{R}d^{2}}{m}

Taking expectation conditioned on
\xi_{0}(x_{0})...\xi_{k-1}(x_{k-1})
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(\sqrt{\delta_{k}}\xi_{k})}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(\sqrt{\delta_{k}}s\bm{\left\langle}\xi_{k}(x_{k}),\Exp_{x_{k}}^{-1}(x^{*})\bm{}+2\delta_{k}s{(1+\sqrt{L_{R}}\mathrm{d}(x_{k},x^{*}))}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2})}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(\sqrt{\delta_{k}}2s\bm{\left\langle}\xi_{k}(x_{k}),\Exp_{x_{k}}^{-1}(x^{*})\bm{})}}\right]^{1/2}\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(4\delta_{k}s{(1+\sqrt{L_{R}}\mathrm{d}(x_{k},x^{*}))}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2})}}\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp{(8\delta_{k}s^{2}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}d+4\delta_{k}s{(1+\sqrt{L_{R}}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})})}d)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp{(\frac{\delta_{k}ms}{2}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}+4\delta_{k}sd+\frac{8\delta_{k}sL_{R}d^{2}}{m})}

where the second inequality uses the fact that
\bm{\left\langle}\xi_{k}(x_{k}),\Exp_{x_{k}}^{-1}(x^{*})\bm{}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})})^{2}
and Lemma
38
and Lemma
37
and our assumption that
\delta_{k}\leq\frac{d}{4m{(1+\sqrt{L_{R}}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})})}}
. The third inequality is by Cauchy Schwarz. We thus verify the requirement for Lemma
40
, which bounds

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{i\leq K}q_{i}\geq t^{2}})}\leq

\displaystyle 8K\delta m\exp{(q_{0}+{16s\mathcal{R}^{2}}+\frac{32sd}{m}+\frac{64sL_{R}d^{2}}{m^{2}}-\frac{t^{2}}{2})}

Plugging in the definition of
q_{k}
and
s
, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{i\leq K}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}\geq t})}\leq 32K\delta m\exp{(\frac{m\mathcal{R}^{2}}{d}+\frac{2L_{R}d}{m}-\frac{mt^{2}}{32d})}

∎

Lemma 34

(Subgaussian Bound under Dissipativity, Discretized SDE, fixed stepsize)
.

Let
\beta
be a vector field satisfying
2
. Let
x^{*}
be some point with
\beta(x^{*})=0
. Assume that for all
x
such that
\mathrm{d}{(x,x^{*})}\geq\mathcal{R}
, there exists a minimizing geodesic
\gamma:[0,1]\to M
with
\gamma(0)=x,\gamma(1)=x^{*}
, and

\displaystyle\bm{\left\langle}\beta(x),\gamma^{\prime}(0)\bm{}\leq-m\mathrm{d}{(x,x^{*})}^{2}

.
Let
r\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
denote an arbitrary radius, and assume that
\delta
is a stepsize satisfying

\displaystyle\delta\leq\min\left\{\frac{m}{16{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}{(1+\sqrt{L_{R}}r)}},\frac{d}{4m{(1+\sqrt{L_{R}}r)}}\right\}

.
Let
x_{k}
be the following stochastic process:

\displaystyle x_{k+1}=\Exp_{x_{k}}{(\delta\beta(x_{k})+\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k}))}

where
\xi_{k}(x_{k})\sim\mathcal{N}_{x_{k}}{(0,I)}
. Assume that
\mathrm{d}{(x_{0},x^{*})}\leq 2\mathcal{R}
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{k\leq K}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}\geq r})}\leq 32K\delta m\exp{(\frac{m\mathcal{R}^{2}}{d}+\frac{2L_{R}d}{m}-\frac{mr^{2}}{32d})}

Proof.

Let us define, for analysis purposes, the following process:

\displaystyle\tilde{x}_{k+1}=\Exp_{\tilde{x}_{k}}{(\delta_{k}\beta(\tilde{x}_{k})+\sqrt{\delta_{k}}\xi_{k}(\tilde{x}_{k}))}

initialized at
\tilde{x}_{0}=x_{0}
and where

\displaystyle\delta_{k}:=\min\left\{\delta,\frac{m}{16{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}{(1+\sqrt{L_{R}}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})})}},\frac{d}{4m{(1+\sqrt{L_{R}}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})})}}\right\}

Define the event
A_{k}:=\left\{\max_{i\leq k}\mathrm{d}{(\tilde{x}_{i},x^{*})}\leq r\right\}
. Under the event
A_{k}
,
\delta_{i}=\delta
for all
i\leq k
, and consequently,
\tilde{x}_{i}=x_{i}
for all
i\leq k
. Therefore,
A_{k}=\left\{\max_{i\leq k}\mathrm{d}{({x}_{i},x^{*})}\leq r\right\}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{k\leq K}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}\geq r})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({A_{k}^{c}})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{k\leq K}\mathrm{d}{(\tilde{x}_{k},x^{*})}\geq r})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 32K\delta m\exp{(\frac{m\mathcal{R}^{2}}{d}+\frac{2L_{R}d}{m}-\frac{mr^{2}}{32d})}

where the last inequality follows from Lemma
33
.
∎

Lemma 35

(Subgaussian Bound under Dissipativity, Nongaussian Noise, adaptive stepsize)
.

Let
\beta
be a vector field satisfying
2
. Let
x^{*}
be some point with
\beta(x^{*})=0
. Assume that for all
x
such that
\mathrm{d}{(x,x^{*})}\geq\mathcal{R}
, there exists a minimizing geodesic
\gamma:[0,1]\to M
with
\gamma(0)=x,\gamma(1)=x^{*}
, and

\displaystyle\bm{\left\langle}\beta(x),\gamma^{\prime}(0)\bm{}\leq-m\mathrm{d}{(x,x^{*})}^{2}

.
Let
\xi
be a random vector field satisfying, for all
x\in M
,
\mathbb{E}\left[{\xi(x)}\right]=0
and
{\left\|\xi(x)\right\|}\leq L_{\xi}
almost surely. For
k\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}
, let
\xi_{k}
be iid samples of
\xi
. Let
x_{k}
be the following stochastic process:

\displaystyle x_{k+1}=\Exp_{x_{k}}{(\delta_{k}\beta(x_{k})+\sqrt{\delta_{k}}\xi_{k}(x_{k}))}

where
\delta_{k}
is a positive stepsize that depends only on
x_{k}
and satisfies

\delta_{k}\leq\frac{m}{16{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}{(1+\sqrt{L_{R}}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})})}}
. Assume that
\mathrm{d}{(x_{0},x^{*})}\leq 2\mathcal{R}
. Finally, assume that there exists
\delta\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
such that for all
k
,
\delta_{k}\leq\delta
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{i\leq K}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}\geq t})}\leq 32K\delta m\exp{(\frac{m\mathcal{R}^{2}}{L_{\xi}^{2}}+\frac{2L_{R}L_{\xi}^{2}}{m}-\frac{mt^{2}}{32L_{\xi}^{2}})}

Proof.

In case the minimizing geodesic from
x_{k}
to
x^{*}
is not unique, we let
\Exp_{x_{k}}^{-1}(x^{*})
be any arbitrary (but consistently fixed) choice that satisfies
\bm{\left\langle}\beta(x_{k}),\Exp_{x_{k}}^{-1}(x^{*})\bm{}\leq-m\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}
. Using the result from Corollary 8 of
Zhang and Sra 2016
(see Lemma
41
),

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(x_{k+1},x^{*})}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}^{2}{(x_{k},x^{*})}-2\bm{\left\langle}\delta_{k}\beta(x_{k})+\sqrt{\delta_{k}}\xi_{k}(x_{k}),\Exp_{x_{k}}^{-1}(x^{*})\bm{}

\displaystyle\quad+{(1+\sqrt{L_{R}}\mathrm{d}(x_{k},x^{*}))}{\left\|\delta_{k}\beta(x_{k})+\sqrt{\delta_{k}}\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2}

By our assumption,
\bm{\left\langle}\beta(x_{k}),\Exp_{x_{k}}^{-1}(x^{*})\bm{}\leq-m\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}+m\mathcal{R}^{2}
. Applying Cauchy Schwarz and simplifying,

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(x_{k+1},x^{*})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{(-2m\delta_{k}+2\delta_{k}^{2}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}+2\delta_{k}^{2}\sqrt{L_{R}}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})})}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}+2\delta_{k}m\mathcal{R}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+2\sqrt{\delta_{k}}\bm{\left\langle}\xi_{k}(x_{k}),\Exp_{x_{k}}^{-1}(x^{*})\bm{}+2\delta_{k}{(1+\sqrt{L_{R}}\mathrm{d}(x_{k},x^{*}))}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\delta_{k}m\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}+2\delta_{k}m\mathcal{R}^{2}+2\sqrt{\delta_{k}}\bm{\left\langle}\xi_{k}(x_{k}),\Exp_{x_{k}}^{-1}(x^{*})\bm{}+2\delta_{k}{(1+\sqrt{L_{R}}\mathrm{d}(x_{k},x^{*}))}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2}

where we used our assumption that
\delta_{k}\leq\frac{m}{16{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}{(1+\sqrt{L_{R}}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})})}}
.

Let
s:=\frac{m}{32L_{\xi}^{2}}
We will now apply Lemma
40
with

\displaystyle q_{k}=s\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}\qquad\xi_{k}=s\bm{\left\langle}\xi_{k}(x_{k}),\Exp_{x_{k}}^{-1}(x^{*})\bm{}+2\sqrt{\delta_{k}}s{(1+\sqrt{L_{R}}\mathrm{d}(x_{k},x^{*}))}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2}

\displaystyle\lambda=m\qquad\gamma=2sm\mathcal{R}^{2}\qquad\mu=4sd+\frac{8sL_{R}d^{2}}{m}

Taking expectation conditioned on
\xi_{0}(x_{0})...\xi_{k-1}(x_{k-1})
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(\sqrt{\delta_{k}}\xi_{k})}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(\sqrt{\delta_{k}}s\bm{\left\langle}\xi_{k}(x_{k}),\Exp_{x_{k}}^{-1}(x^{*})\bm{}+2\delta_{k}s{(1+\sqrt{L_{R}}\mathrm{d}(x_{k},x^{*}))}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2})}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(\sqrt{\delta_{k}}2s\bm{\left\langle}\xi_{k}(x_{k}),\Exp_{x_{k}}^{-1}(x^{*})\bm{})}}\right]^{1/2}\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(4\delta_{k}s{(1+\sqrt{L_{R}}\mathrm{d}(x_{k},x^{*}))}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2})}}\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp{(8\delta_{k}s^{2}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}L_{\xi}^{2}+4\delta_{k}s{(1+\sqrt{L_{R}}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})})}L_{\xi}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp{(\frac{\delta_{k}ms}{2}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}+4\delta_{k}sL_{\xi}^{2}+\frac{8\delta_{k}sL_{R}L_{\xi}^{4}}{m})}

where the second inequality uses Lemma
38
. The third inequality is by Cauchy Schwarz. We thus verify the requirement for Lemma
40
, which bounds

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{i\leq K}q_{i}\geq t^{2}})}\leq

\displaystyle 8K\delta m\exp{(q_{0}+{16s\mathcal{R}^{2}}+\frac{32sL_{\xi}^{2}}{m}+\frac{64sL_{R}L_{\xi}^{4}}{m^{2}}-\frac{t^{2}}{2})}

Plugging in the definition of
q_{k}
and
s
, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{i\leq K}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}\geq t})}\leq 32K\delta m\exp{(\frac{m\mathcal{R}^{2}}{L_{\xi}^{2}}+\frac{2L_{R}L_{\xi}^{2}}{m}-\frac{mt^{2}}{32L_{\xi}^{2}})}

∎

Lemma 36

(Subgaussian Bound under Dissipativity, Nongaussian Noise, fixed stepsize)
.

Let
\beta
be a vector field satisfying
2
. Let
x^{*}
be some point with
\beta(x^{*})=0
. Assume that for all
x
such that
\mathrm{d}{(x,x^{*})}\geq\mathcal{R}
, there exists a minimizing geodesic
\gamma:[0,1]\to M
with
\gamma(0)=x,\gamma(1)=x^{*}
, and

\displaystyle\bm{\left\langle}\beta(x),\gamma^{\prime}(0)\bm{}\leq-m\mathrm{d}{(x,x^{*})}^{2}

.
Let
r\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
denote an arbitrary radius, and assume that
\delta
is a stepsize satisfying

\displaystyle\delta\leq\min\left\{\frac{m}{16{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}{(1+\sqrt{L_{R}}r)}}\right\}

.
Let
\xi
be a random vector field satisfying, for all
x\in M
,
\mathbb{E}\left[{\xi(x)}\right]=0
and
{\left\|\xi(x)\right\|}\leq L_{\xi}
almost surely. For
k\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}
, let
\xi_{k}
be iid samples of
\xi
. Let
x_{k}
be the following stochastic process:

\displaystyle x_{k+1}=\Exp_{x_{k}}{(\delta_{k}\beta(x_{k})+\sqrt{\delta_{k}}\xi_{k}(x_{k}))}

Assume that
\mathrm{d}{(x_{0},x^{*})}\leq 2\mathcal{R}
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{k\leq K}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}\geq r})}\leq 32K\delta m\exp{(\frac{m\mathcal{R}^{2}}{L_{\xi}^{2}}+\frac{2L_{R}L_{\xi}^{2}}{m}-\frac{mt^{2}}{32L_{\xi}^{2}})}

Proof.

Let us define, for analysis purposes, the following process:

\displaystyle\tilde{x}_{k+1}=\Exp_{\tilde{x}_{k}}{(\delta_{k}\beta(\tilde{x}_{k})+\sqrt{\delta_{k}}\xi_{k}(\tilde{x}_{k}))}

initialized at
\tilde{x}_{0}=x_{0}
and where

\displaystyle\delta_{k}:=\min\left\{\delta,\frac{m}{16{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}{(1+\sqrt{L_{R}}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})})}}\right\}

Define the event
A_{k}:=\left\{\max_{i\leq k}\mathrm{d}{(\tilde{x}_{i},x^{*})}\leq r\right\}
. Under the event
A_{k}
,
\delta_{i}=\delta
for all
i\leq k
, and consequently,
\tilde{x}_{i}=x_{i}
for all
i\leq k
. Therefore,
A_{k}=\left\{\max_{i\leq k}\mathrm{d}{({x}_{i},x^{*})}\leq r\right\}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{k\leq K}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}\geq r})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({A_{k}^{c}})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{k\leq K}\mathrm{d}{(\tilde{x}_{k},x^{*})}\geq r})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 32K\delta m\exp{(\frac{m\mathcal{R}^{2}}{L_{\xi}^{2}}+\frac{2L_{R}L_{\xi}^{2}}{m}-\frac{mt^{2}}{32L_{\xi}^{2}})}

where the last inequality follows from Lemma
35
.
∎

D.4
Near Subgaussian Bounds

D.5
Misc

Lemma 37
.

For
\lambda\leq\frac{1}{4}
and
\xi\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d\times d})
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(\lambda{\left\|\xi\right\|}_{2}^{2})}}\right]\leq\exp{(\lambda d+2\lambda^{2}d)}\leq\exp{(2\lambda d)}

Proof.

Consequence of
\chi^{2}
distribution being subexponential.
∎

Lemma 38

(Hoeffding’s Lemma)
.

Let
\eta_{k}
be a
0
-mean random variable. Then for all
\lambda
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{\eta}}\left[{\exp{(\lambda\eta)}}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}_{{\eta}}\left[{\exp{(2\lambda^{2}\eta^{2})}}\right]

Proof.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{\eta}}\left[{\exp{(\lambda\eta)}}\right]=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{\eta}}\left[{\exp{(\lambda\eta-\mathbb{E}_{{\eta^{\prime}}}\left[{\lambda\eta^{\prime}}\right])}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{\eta,\eta^{\prime}}}\left[{\exp{(\lambda{(\eta-\eta^{\prime})})}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{\eta,\eta^{\prime},\varepsilon}}\left[{\exp{(\lambda\varepsilon{(\eta-\eta^{\prime})})}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{\eta,\eta^{\prime}}}\left[{\exp{(\lambda^{2}{(\eta-\eta^{\prime})}^{2}/2)}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{\eta,\eta^{\prime}}}\left[{\exp{(\lambda^{2}{(\eta^{2}+{\eta^{\prime}}^{2})})}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{\eta}}\left[{\exp{(2\lambda^{2}\eta^{2})}}\right]

where
\varepsilon
is a Rademacher random variable.
∎

Lemma 39

(Corollary of Doob’s maximal inequality)
.

Let
K
be any positive integer. For any
k\leq K
, let
a_{k},b_{k},c_{k},d_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
be arbitrary positive constants. Assume that for all
k
,
a_{k}\leq\frac{1}{4}
and
{a_{k}+c_{k}}\leq\frac{1}{4}
. Let
q_{k}
be a semi-martingale of the form

\displaystyle q_{k+1}\leq{(1+a_{k})}q_{k}+b_{k}+\eta_{k}

where
\eta_{k}
are random variables. Assume that for all
k
,
\eta_{k}
satisfy

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(\eta_{k})}|\eta_{0}...\eta_{k-1}}\right]\leq\exp{(c_{k}q_{k}+d_{k})}

Assume in addition that
q_{k}\geq 0
almost surely, for all
k
. Finally, assume that
\sum_{k=0}^{K}a_{k}+c_{k}\leq\frac{1}{8}
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{k\leq K}q_{k}\geq t^{2}})}\leq\exp{(q_{0}+\sum_{k=0}^{K}(b_{k}+d_{k})-\frac{t^{2}}{2})}

Proof.

Let us first define

\displaystyle r_{0}:=

\displaystyle q_{0}

\displaystyle r_{k+1}:=

\displaystyle{(1+a_{k})}r_{k}+b_{k}+\eta_{k}

i.e.
r_{k}
is very similar to
q_{k}
, only difference being that we replaced
\leq
by
=
.

We first verify that for all
k\leq K
,
r_{k}\geq q_{k}
. For
k=0
, by definition,
r_{0}=q_{0}
. Now assume that
r_{k}\geq q_{k}
for some
k
. Then for
k+1
,

\displaystyle r_{k+1}:=

\displaystyle{(1+a_{k})}r_{k}+b_{k}+\eta_{k}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle{(1+a_{k})}q_{k}+b_{k}+\eta_{k}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle q_{k+1}

We verify below that
\exp{(r_{k})}
is a sub-martingale: conditioning on
\eta_{0}...\eta_{k-1}
, and taking expectation wrt
\eta_{k}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(r_{k+1})}|\eta_{0}...\eta_{k}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{({(1+a_{k})}r_{k}+b_{k}+\eta_{k})}|\eta_{0}...\eta_{k}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp{((1+a_{k})r_{k}+b_{k})}\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(\eta_{k})}|\eta_{0}...\eta_{k}}\right]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\exp{((1+a_{k})r_{k}+b_{k})}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\exp{(r_{k})}

where the first inequality is by convexity of
\exp
, and
\mathbb{E}\left[{\eta_{k}}\right]=0
, and Jensen’s inequality.

Let us now define
s_{k}:=\prod_{i=0}^{k-1}{(1+a_{i}+c_{i})}^{-1}
. We can upper bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(s_{k+1}r_{k+1})}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(s_{k+1}{((1+a_{k})r_{k}+b_{k}+\eta_{k})})}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(s_{k+1}(1+a_{k})r_{k}+s_{k+1}b_{k})}\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(s_{k+1}\eta_{k})}|\eta_{0}...\eta_{k}}\right]}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(s_{k+1}(1+a_{k})r_{k}+s_{k+1}b_{k})}\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(s_{k+1}\eta_{k})}|\eta_{0}...\eta_{k}}\right]}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(s_{k+1}(1+a_{k})r_{k}+s_{k+1}b_{k})}\cdot{(\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(\eta_{k})}|\eta_{0}...\eta_{k}}\right])}^{s_{k+1}}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(s_{k+1}(1+a_{k})r_{k}+s_{k+1}{(b_{k}+c_{k}q_{k}+d_{k})})}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(s_{k}r_{k})}}\right]\cdot\exp{(s_{k+1}{(b_{k}+d_{k})})}

where the second inequality is by Lemma
38
, the third inequality is by the fact that
s_{k}\leq 1
for all
k
and by Jensen’s inequality, the fourth inequality uses our assumption on
\eta_{k}
in the Lemma statement, as well as the fact that
s_{k}\leq 1
for all
k
. The last equality is by definition of
s_{k}
and because
q_{k}\leq r_{k}
. Applying this recursively gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(s_{K}r_{K})}}\right]\leq\exp{(r_{0})}\cdot\exp{(\sum_{k=0}^{K}s_{k+1}(b_{k}+d_{k}))}\leq\exp{(r_{0}+\sum_{k=0}^{K}(b_{k}+d_{k}))}

By Doob’s maximal inequality (recall that we
e^{r_{k}}
is a sub-martingale),

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{k\leq K}q_{k}\geq t^{2}})}\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{k\leq K}r_{k}\geq t^{2}})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{k\leq K}\exp{(s_{K}r_{k})}\geq\exp{(s_{K}t^{2})}})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(s_{K}r_{K})}}\right]\cdot\exp{(-\frac{t^{2}}{2})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp{(r_{0}+\sum_{k=0}^{K}s_{k+1}(b_{k}+d_{k})-\frac{t^{2}}{2})}

(D.8)

The first equality uses our assumption that
s_{K}=\prod_{k=0}^{K-1}{(1+a_{k}+c_{k})}^{-1}\geq e^{-\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}{4(a_{k}+c_{k})}}\geq e^{-\frac{1}{4}}\geq\frac{1}{2}
and the fact that
r_{K}\geq q_{k}\geq 0
.
∎

Lemma 40

(Uniform Bound)
.

Let
K
be any positive integer. For
k\leq K
, let
\delta_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
, let
\lambda,\gamma,\mu\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
. Let
q_{k}
be a semi-martingale of the form

\displaystyle q_{k+1}\leq{(1-\delta_{k}\lambda)}q_{k}+\delta_{k}\gamma+\sqrt{\delta_{k}}\xi_{k}

where
\xi_{k}
are random variables. Assume that for all
k
,
\xi_{k}
satisfy

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(\sqrt{\delta_{k}}\xi_{k})}|\xi_{0}...\xi_{k-1}}\right]\leq\exp{(\delta_{k}\lambda q_{k}/2+\delta_{k}\mu)}

Assume that there is a constant
\delta
such that for all
k
,
\delta_{k}\leq\delta\leq\frac{1}{8\lambda}
. Assume in addition that
q_{k}\geq 0
almost surely, for all
k
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{i\leq K}q_{i}\geq t^{2}})}\leq

\displaystyle 8K\delta\lambda\exp{(q_{0}+\frac{8(\gamma+\mu)}{\lambda}-\frac{t^{2}}{2})}

Proof.

For any
s\leq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(sq_{k+1})}}\right]\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(s{((1-\delta_{k}\lambda)q_{k}+\delta_{k}\gamma+\sqrt{\delta_{k}}\xi_{k})})}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(s{((1-\delta_{k}\lambda)q_{k}+\delta_{k}\gamma)})}\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(s\sqrt{\delta_{k}}\xi_{k})}}\right]}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(s{((1-\delta_{k}\lambda)q_{k}+\delta_{k}\gamma)})}\cdot{(\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(\sqrt{\delta_{k}}\xi_{k})}}\right])}^{s}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(s{((1-\delta_{k}\lambda/2)q_{k}+\delta_{k}(\gamma+\mu))})}}\right]

Applying the above recursively, for any
k
, we can bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(q_{k})}}\right]\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{((1-\delta_{k}\lambda/2)q_{k-1}+\delta_{k}(\gamma+\mu))}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle...

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(\prod_{i=0}^{k-1}{(1-\delta_{i}\lambda/2)}q_{0}+\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}\prod_{j=i}^{k-1}{(1-\delta_{j}\lambda/2)}{(\delta_{i}(\gamma+\mu))})}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(e^{-\frac{\lambda}{2}\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}\delta_{k}}q_{0}+(\gamma+\mu)\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}e^{-\frac{\lambda}{2}\sum_{j=i}^{k-1}\delta_{j}}\delta_{i})}}\right]

(D.9)

Let us define
t_{k}:=\sum_{i=0}^{k}\delta_{i}
. By our assumption that
\delta_{i}\leq\frac{1}{4\lambda}
, we can verify that
\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}e^{\frac{\lambda}{2}\sum_{j=i}^{k-1}\delta_{j}}\delta_{i}\leq 2\int_{0}^{t_{k}}e^{-\frac{\lambda(t_{k}-t)}{2}}dt\leq\frac{4}{\lambda}
. Therefore, for all
k
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(q_{k})}}\right]\leq\exp{(q_{0}+\frac{4(\gamma+\mu)}{\lambda})}

Let us now define
N:=\left\lceil\frac{1}{4\delta\lambda}\right\rceil\geq 1
(inequality is because
\delta\leq\delta_{k}\leq\frac{1}{8\lambda}
). We verify that
q_{k+1}\leq q_{k-1}+\delta_{k}(\gamma+\mu)+\eta_{k}
. Let us now apply Lemma
39
with
\eta_{k}=\sqrt{\delta_{k}}\xi_{k}
,
a_{k}=0,b_{k}=\delta_{k}\gamma,c_{k}=\lambda/2,d_{k}=\mu
and the fact that
\delta_{k}\lambda\leq 1/4
to bound, for any
k
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{i\leq N}q_{k+i}\geq t^{2}})}\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(q_{k}+(\gamma+\mu)\sum_{i=0}^{N}\delta_{i+N}-\frac{t^{2}}{2})}}\right]\leq\exp{(q_{0}+\frac{8(\gamma+\mu)}{\lambda}-\frac{t^{2}}{2})}

where we use the fact that
N\leq\frac{1}{2\delta\lambda}

Applying union bound over the events
\left\{\max_{i\leq N}q_{k+i}\geq t^{2}\right\}
for
k=0,N,2N...
, we can bound, for any positive integer
M
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{i\leq MN}q_{i}\geq t^{2}})}\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{M-1}\mathbb{P}{({\max_{i\leq N}q_{jN+i}\geq t^{2}})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{M-1}\exp{(q_{0}+\frac{8(\gamma+\mu)}{\lambda}-\frac{t^{2}}{2})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle M\exp{(q_{0}+\frac{8(\gamma+\mu)}{\lambda}-\frac{t^{2}}{2})}

Plugging in
M=\frac{K}{N}\leq 8K\delta\lambda
, it follows that for any
K
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{i\leq K}q_{i}\geq t^{2}})}\leq

\displaystyle 8K\delta\lambda\exp{(q_{0}+\frac{8(\gamma+\mu)}{\lambda}-\frac{t^{2}}{2})}

∎

Lemma 41
.

Let
M
satisfy Assumption
3
. For any 3 points
x,y,z\in M
, let
u,v\in T_{y}M
be such that
z=\Exp_{y}(v)
and
x=\Exp_{y}(u)
. Assume in addition that
{\left\|u\right\|}=\mathrm{d}{(x,y)}
. Then

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(z,x)}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(y,x)}^{2}-2\bm{\left\langle}v,u\bm{}+{\tc{(\sqrt{L_{R}}\mathrm{d}{(y,x)})}}{\left\|v\right\|}^{2}

where
\zeta(r):=\frac{r}{\tanh(r)}
.

The above lemma is a restatement of Corollary 8 from
Zhang and Sra 2016
.

Appendix E
CLT

E.1
Main CLT Result and Proof

Lemma 42
.

Let
{\mathbf{H}}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
be a random function, assume there exist constants
L_{\mathbf{H}},L_{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime},L_{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime\prime}
such that

For all
{\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|}_{2}
:

\displaystyle\qquad{\left\|{\mathbf{H}}(\mathbf{u})\right\|}_{2}\leq L_{\mathbf{H}}

For all
{\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|}_{2}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{L_{R}}}
:

\displaystyle\qquad{\left\|\nabla{\mathbf{H}}(\mathbf{u})\right\|}_{2}\leq L_{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime}\qquad{\left\|\nabla^{2}{\mathbf{H}}(\mathbf{u})\right\|}_{2}\leq L_{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime\prime}

Assume also that for all
\mathbf{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbf{H}}(\mathbf{z})}\right]=0
and
\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbf{H}}(\mathbf{z}){\mathbf{H}}(\mathbf{z})^{T}}\right]=I
. For
k\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}
, let
{\mathbf{H}}_{k}
denote independent samples of
{\mathbf{H}}
, and let
{\bm{\zeta}}_{k}
denote independent samples from
\mathcal{N}(0,I)
. Let
T,\delta,K
satisfy

\displaystyle 1.\ T\leq\min\left\{\frac{1}{16L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}},\frac{1}{16{L_{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime}}^{2}},\frac{1}{32\sqrt{L_{R}}L_{\beta}},\frac{1}{128L_{R}L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}},\frac{1}{256L_{R}L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}\log{(2^{10}L_{R})}}\right\}

\displaystyle 2.\ \delta:=T^{3},K:=T/\delta=T^{-2}

(E.1)

For
k=0...K
, define two discrete stochastic processes

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k+1}=\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}+\delta{\bm{\beta}}+\sqrt{\delta}{\mathbf{H}}_{k}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k})

\displaystyle{\mathbf{z}}_{k+1}={\mathbf{z}}_{k}+\delta{\bm{\beta}}+\sqrt{\delta}{\bm{\zeta}}_{k+1}

(E.2)

initialized at the same point
\mathbf{z}_{0}=\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{0}
.

Then there exists
\lambda_{6}=poly{(L_{\mathbf{H}},L_{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime},L_{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime\prime},L_{R})}
such that

\displaystyle W_{2}{(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{K},\mathbf{z}_{K})}\leq

\displaystyle\lambda_{6}\cdot\log{(\frac{1}{TL_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}})}^{4}\cdot T^{3/2}

Proof of Lemma
42
.

Let
r:=64L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}\log{(\frac{2^{7}}{T^{2}L_{\mathbf{H}}^{4}})}\geq 1
. Let us define
s(r):=\lceil 256r^{2}L_{{\mathbf{H}}}^{2}\rceil
. It will be useful later on to note that
s(r)\leq 512r^{2}L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}
.

For any
k
, let us define

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbf{z}}_{k+1}:=\mathbf{z}_{k}+\delta{\bm{\beta}}+\sqrt{\delta}{\mathbf{H}}_{k}(\mathbf{z}_{k})

A recursive decomposition:

We first write the
W_{2}
distance at step
k+1
in terms of the
W_{2}
distance at step
k
:

\displaystyle W_{2}{(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k+1},\mathbf{z}_{k+1})}\leq

\displaystyle W_{2}{(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k+1},\hat{\mathbf{z}}_{k+1})}+W_{2}{(\hat{\mathbf{z}}_{k+1},{\mathbf{z}}_{k+1})}

Let
A_{k}
denote the event that
{\left\|\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}\right\|}_{2}\leq\frac{1}{4\sqrt{L_{R}}}\text{ AND }{\left\|{\mathbf{z}}_{k}\right\|}_{2}\leq\frac{1}{4\sqrt{L_{R}}}
, i.e.

{\mathbbm{1}\left\{A_{k}\right\}}:={\mathbbm{1}\left\{{\left\|\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}\right\|}_{2}\leq\frac{1}{4\sqrt{L_{R}}}\right\}}\cdot{\mathbbm{1}\left\{{\left\|\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}\right\|}_{2}\leq\frac{1}{4\sqrt{L_{R}}}\right\}}
.

We can verify that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k+1}-\hat{\mathbf{z}}_{k+1}\right\|}_{2}^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}-{\mathbf{z}}_{k}+\sqrt{\delta}{({\mathbf{H}}_{k}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k})-{\mathbf{H}}_{k}(\mathbf{z}_{k}))}\right\|}_{2}^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}-{\mathbf{z}}_{k}\right\|}_{2}^{2}}\right]+\delta\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|{\mathbf{H}}_{k}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k})-{\mathbf{H}}_{k}(\mathbf{z}_{k})\right\|}_{2}^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}-{\mathbf{z}}_{k}\right\|}_{2}^{2}}\right]+\delta\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{A_{k}^{c}\right\}}L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}}\right]+\delta\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{A_{k}\right\}}{L_{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime}}^{2}{\left\|\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}-\mathbf{z}_{k}\right\|}_{2}^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{(1+\delta{L_{{\mathbf{H}}}^{\prime}}^{2})}\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}-{\mathbf{z}}_{k}\right\|}_{2}^{2}}\right]+\delta L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}\mathbb{P}{({A_{k}^{c}})}

the second equality uses the fact that
{\mathbf{H}}_{k}
is zero mean conditioned on
(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k},\mathbf{z}_{k})
.

Taking square roots,

\displaystyle W_{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k+1},\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k+1})\leq{(1+\delta{L_{{\mathbf{H}}}^{\prime}}^{2})}W_{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k},{\mathbf{z}}_{k})+\sqrt{\delta}L_{\mathbf{H}}\sqrt{\mathbb{P}{({A_{k}^{c}})}}

where we use Assumption
C.5
.

\displaystyle W_{2}{(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k+1},\mathbf{z}_{k+1})}\leq

\displaystyle W_{2}{(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k+1},\hat{\mathbf{z}}_{k+1})}+W_{2}{(\hat{\mathbf{z}}_{k+1},{\mathbf{z}}_{k+1})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{(1+\delta{L_{{\mathbf{H}}}^{\prime}}^{2})}W_{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k},{\mathbf{z}}_{k})+\sqrt{\delta}L_{\mathbf{H}}\sqrt{\mathbb{P}{({A_{k}^{c}})}}+W_{2}(\hat{\mathbf{z}}_{k+1},\mathbf{z}_{k+1})

Applying the above recursively for
k=s(r)...K
, we get

\displaystyle W_{2}{(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{K},\mathbf{z}_{K})}\leq

\displaystyle{(1+\delta{L_{{\mathbf{H}}}^{\prime}}^{2})}^{K-s(r)}W_{2}{(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{s(r)},\mathbf{z}_{s(r)})}+\sum_{k=s(r)}^{K}{(1+\delta{L_{{\mathbf{H}}}^{\prime}}^{2})}^{K-k}{(\sqrt{\delta}L_{\mathbf{H}}\sqrt{\mathbb{P}{({A_{k}^{c}})}}+W_{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k},\mathbf{z}_{k}))}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2W_{2}{(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{s(r)},\mathbf{z}_{s(r)})}+2\sum_{k=s(r)}^{K}{(\sqrt{\delta}L_{\mathbf{H}}\sqrt{\mathbb{P}{({A_{k}^{c}})}}+W_{2}(\hat{\mathbf{z}}_{k},\mathbf{z}_{k}))}

where we use the fact that
1+\delta{L_{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime}}^{2}\leq\exp{(\delta{L_{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime}}^{2})}
and that
K\delta\leq\frac{1}{16{L_{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime}}^{2}}
from (
E.1
).

Bounding
W_{2}{(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{s(r)},\mathbf{z}_{s(r)})}

We can verify that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|\mathbf{z}_{k}\right\|}_{2}^{2}}\right]\leq 2{(k\delta)}^{2}{\left\|{\bm{\beta}}\right\|}_{2}^{2}+2k\delta d\leq 2k^{2}\delta^{2}L_{\beta}^{2}+2k\delta d

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{k}\right\|}_{2}^{2}}\right]\leq 2{(k\delta)}^{2}{\left\|{\bm{\beta}}\right\|}_{2}^{2}+2k\delta d\leq 2k^{2}\delta^{2}L_{\beta}^{2}+2k\delta d

so that by simple triangle inequality,

\displaystyle W_{2}{(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{s(r)},\mathbf{z}_{s(r)})}\leq 4s(r)\delta L_{\beta}+2\sqrt{s(r)\delta d}\leq 1024L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}r^{2}\delta L_{\beta}+64L_{\mathbf{H}}r\sqrt{\delta d}

Bounding
\sum_{k=s(r)}^{K}W_{2}(\hat{\mathbf{z}}_{k+1},{\mathbf{z}}_{k+1})
:

We will now bound the term
\sum_{k=s(r)}^{K}W_{2}(\hat{\mathbf{z}}_{k},\mathbf{z}_{k})
. By Lemma
43

\displaystyle W_{2}^{2}{(\mathbf{z}_{k+1},\hat{\mathbf{z}}_{k+1})}\leq

\displaystyle 2^{18}k\delta{({\mathcal{C}}_{k}^{2}(1+r^{8})+\frac{d^{2}}{{k}^{4}})}

\displaystyle\quad+2^{13}L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}k\delta\cdot{(\exp{(-\frac{r^{2}}{32L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}})}+\exp{(-\frac{1}{32k\delta L_{R}L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}})})}

From Lemma
60
,

\displaystyle\sum_{k={s(r)}}^{K}\sqrt{k\delta}{({\mathcal{C}}_{k}+\frac{d}{k^{2}})}\leq\delta^{3/2}K\lambda_{1}+\delta\sqrt{K}\lambda_{2}+\sqrt{\delta}\log(K)\lambda_{3}+\sqrt{\delta}\lambda_{4}+\delta^{2}K\lambda_{5}

for
\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2},\lambda_{3},\lambda_{4},\lambda_{5}
are
poly(d,L_{\mathbf{H}},L_{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime},L_{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime\prime})
defined in (
E.12
).
(While the above looks complicated, the dominating term is really just
\sqrt{\delta}\log(K)\lambda_{3}
.) Thus

\displaystyle\sum_{k=s(r)}^{K}W_{2}(\hat{\mathbf{z}}_{k},\mathbf{z}_{k})\leq

\displaystyle{(1+r^{4})}{(\delta^{3/2}K\lambda_{1}+\delta\sqrt{K}\lambda_{2}+\sqrt{\delta}\log(K)\lambda_{3}+\sqrt{\delta}\lambda_{4}+\delta^{2}K\lambda_{5})}

\displaystyle\quad+2^{7}L_{\mathbf{H}}K^{3/2}\sqrt{\delta}\cdot{(\exp{(-\frac{r^{2}}{32L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}})}+\exp{(-\frac{1}{32K\delta L_{R}L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}})})}

Combining all the terms,
we get

\displaystyle W_{2}{(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{K},\mathbf{z}_{K})}\leq

\displaystyle 1024L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}r^{2}\delta L_{\beta}+64L_{\mathbf{H}}r\sqrt{\delta d}

\displaystyle\quad+{(1+r^{4})}{(\delta^{3/2}K\lambda_{1}+\delta\sqrt{K}\lambda_{2}+\sqrt{\delta}\log(K)\lambda_{3}+\sqrt{\delta}\lambda_{4}+\delta^{2}K\lambda_{5})}

\displaystyle\quad+2^{7}L_{\mathbf{H}}K^{3/2}\sqrt{\delta}\cdot{(\exp{(-\frac{r^{2}}{32L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}})}+\exp{(-\frac{1}{32K\delta L_{R}L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}})})}

For the rest of this proof, it is more convenient to work with
T
instead of
K
and
\delta
. Recall that
\delta=(K\delta)^{3}=T^{3}
and
K=T/\delta=\frac{1}{T^{2}}
. We can then rewrite the above upperbound as

\displaystyle W_{2}{(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{K},\mathbf{z}_{K})}\leq

\displaystyle 1024L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}r^{2}T^{3}L_{\beta}+64L_{\mathbf{H}}rT^{3/2}\sqrt{d}

\displaystyle\quad+{(1+r^{4})}{(T^{9/2}\lambda_{1}+T^{2}\lambda_{2}+T^{3/2}\log(1/T)\lambda_{3}+T^{3/2}\lambda_{4}+T^{6}K\lambda_{5})}

\displaystyle\quad+2^{7}L_{\mathbf{H}}\cdot T^{-3/2}\cdot{(\exp{(-\frac{r^{2}}{32L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}})}+\exp{(-\frac{1}{32TL_{R}L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}})})}

By our definition of
\delta=T^{2}
and Lemma
17
,

\displaystyle 2^{7}L_{\mathbf{H}}T^{-3/2}\cdot\exp{(-\frac{1}{32K\delta L_{R}L_{\mathbf{H}}^{3}})}\leq T^{3/2}L_{\mathbf{H}}^{3}

\displaystyle\Leftarrow\qquad

\displaystyle\frac{1}{64L_{R}TL_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}}\geq\log{(\frac{16}{TL_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}})}

\displaystyle\Leftarrow\qquad

\displaystyle\frac{16}{TL_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}\log{(16/(TL_{\mathbf{H}}^{2})))}}\geq 1024L_{R}

\displaystyle\Leftarrow\qquad

\displaystyle T\leq\frac{1}{256L_{R}L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}\log{(2^{10}L_{R})}}

The last line is satisfied by assumption in our lemma statement.

Next, by definition of
r=64L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}\log{(\frac{2^{7}}{T^{2}L_{\mathbf{H}}^{4}})}
(see start of proof), we verify that

\displaystyle 2^{7}L_{\mathbf{H}}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\exp{(-\frac{r^{2}}{32L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}})}\leq T^{3/2}L_{\mathbf{H}}^{3}

Finally, recall from Lemma
60
that
\lambda_{1}...\lambda_{5}
are
poly{(L_{\mathbf{H}},L_{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime},L_{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime\prime})}
. Combined with the definition of
r
, as well as the fact that
T\leq 1/d\leq 1
(from assumption in Lemma statement), we verify that there exists
\lambda_{6}=poly{(L_{R},L_{\mathbf{H}},L_{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime},L_{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime\prime})}
such that

\displaystyle W_{2}{(\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_{K},\mathbf{z}_{K})}\leq

\displaystyle 1024L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}r^{2}T^{3}L_{\beta}+64L_{\mathbf{H}}rT^{3/2}\sqrt{d}

\displaystyle\quad+{(1+r^{4})}{(T^{9/2}\lambda_{1}+T^{2}\lambda_{2}+T^{3/2}\log(1/T)\lambda_{3}+T^{3/2}\lambda_{4}+T^{6}K\lambda_{5})}

\displaystyle\quad+2^{7}L_{\mathbf{H}}\cdot T^{-3/2}\cdot{(\exp{(-\frac{r^{2}}{32L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}})}+\exp{(-\frac{1}{32TL_{R}L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}})})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lambda_{6}\cdot\log{(\frac{1}{T})}^{4}\cdot T^{3/2}

∎

E.1.1
One-Step-Wasserstein-Bound

The goal of this section is to bound
W_{2}(\mathbf{z}_{k+1},\hat{\mathbf{z}}_{k+1})
.

Lemma 43
.

Consider the same setup as Lemma
42
. Let
r\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
be an arbitrary positive constant, let
k\geq\max\left\{256r^{2}L_{{\mathbf{H}}}^{2},16L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}\right\}
, let

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbf{z}}_{k+1}:=\mathbf{z}_{k}+\sqrt{\delta}{\mathbf{H}}(\mathbf{z}_{k})

Then

\displaystyle W_{2}^{2}{(\mathbf{z}_{k+1},\hat{\mathbf{z}}_{k+1})}\leq

\displaystyle 2^{18}k\delta{({\mathcal{C}}_{k}^{2}(1+r^{8})+\frac{d^{2}}{{k}^{4}})}

\displaystyle\quad+2^{13}L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}k\delta\cdot{(\exp{(-\frac{r^{2}}{32L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}})}+\exp{(-\frac{1}{32k\delta L_{R}L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}})})}

where
{\mathcal{C}}_{k}
is defined in (
E.11
).

Proof.

The main idea of the proof is via a recursion over
k
. Let us consider a fixed
k
, we will bound
W_{2}^{2}{(\mathbf{z}_{k+1},\hat{\mathbf{z}}_{k+1})}
in terms of
W_{2}^{2}{(\mathbf{z}_{k},\hat{\mathbf{z}}_{k})}
.

Step 1: Transform
\mathbf{z}
to
\mathbf{u}
:

To simplify notation, Let us also shift the coordinates, so that
\mathbf{u}_{0}:=\mathbf{z}_{k}-k\delta{\bm{\beta}}=\sqrt{\delta}\sum_{i=0}^{k}{\bm{\zeta}}_{i}

,\mathbf{u}_{\delta}:=\mathbf{z}_{k+1}-(k+1)\delta{\bm{\beta}}=\sum_{i=1}^{k}{\bm{\zeta}}_{i}
,
\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{\delta}:=\hat{\mathbf{z}}_{k+1}-(k+1)\delta{\bm{\beta}}=\sqrt{\delta}\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}{\bm{\zeta}}_{i}+\sqrt{\delta}{\mathbf{H}}(\mathbf{z}_{k})
. To further simplify notation, we let
{\bm{\zeta}}:={\bm{\zeta}}_{k}
. We also let
{\mathbf{H}}(\mathbf{u}):={\mathbf{H}}_{k}(\mathbf{u}+k\delta{\bm{\beta}})={\mathbf{H}}_{k}(\mathbf{z}_{k})
. Given these definitions, we verify that

\displaystyle\mathbf{u}_{0}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,k\delta I)

\displaystyle\mathbf{u}_{\delta}:=\mathbf{u}_{0}+\sqrt{\delta}{\bm{\zeta}},\qquad{\bm{\zeta}}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I)

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{\delta}:=\mathbf{u}_{0}+\sqrt{\delta}{\mathbf{H}}(\mathbf{u}_{0})

(E.3)

Under our assumption that
k\delta\leq\frac{1}{4L_{\beta}\sqrt{L_{R}}}
, we verify that
{\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|}_{2}\leq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{L_{R}}}\Rightarrow{\left\|\mathbf{z}_{k}\right\|}_{2}={\left\|\mathbf{u}+k\delta{\bm{\beta}}\right\|}_{2}\leq{\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|}_{2}+k\delta L_{\beta}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{L_{R}}}
, so that (
C.5
) implies

For all
{\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|}_{2}
:

\displaystyle\qquad{\left\|{\mathbf{H}}(\mathbf{u})\right\|}_{2}\leq L_{\mathbf{H}}

For all
{\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|}_{2}\leq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{L_{R}}}
:

\displaystyle\qquad{\left\|\nabla{\mathbf{H}}(\mathbf{u})\right\|}_{2}\leq L_{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime}\qquad{\left\|\nabla^{2}{\mathbf{H}}(\mathbf{u})\right\|}_{2}\leq L_{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime\prime}

(E.4)

One can also verify that
W_{2}(\mathbf{z}_{k},\hat{\mathbf{z}}_{k})=W_{2}(\mathbf{u}_{\delta},\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{\delta})
. Therefore, for the rest of this section, we aim to bound
W_{2}(\mathbf{u}_{\delta},\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{\delta})
.

Step 2: Talagrand’s inequality with truncated tail

Let
p_{\delta}
denote the density of
\mathbf{u}_{\delta}
and
\hat{p}_{\delta}
denote the density of
\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{\delta}
. Then
\log{(p_{\delta}(\mathbf{u}))}\propto-\frac{1}{2(k+1)\delta}{\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|}_{2}^{2}
. Thus
p_{\delta}
is strongly log-concave, with parameter
\frac{1}{(k+1)\delta}
. Talagrand’s inequality (see e.g.
Otto and Villani 2000
) then implies that

\displaystyle W_{2}^{2}(p_{\delta},\hat{p}_{\delta})\leq(k+1)\delta\mathcal{KL}\left(\hat{p}_{\delta}\|p_{\delta}\right)\leq\chi^{2}(\hat{p}_{\delta}||p_{\delta})

(the second inequality always holds, see
Zhai 2018
).

However, we cannot apply Talagrand’s inequality directly. Under the assumptions of Lemma
42
,
{\mathbf{H}}
does not have Lipschitz derivatives when
{\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|}_{2}
is large (this is due to distortion from curvature being more significant for large
{\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|}_{2}
.
Instead, we consider a "truncated" version of Talagrand’s inequality:

Lemma 44
.

Let
p
be a density such that
\nabla^{2}p(\mathbf{u})\succ mI
. Let
q
be any density. Let
c
be any positive radius, let
B_{c}
be a ball of radius
c
centered at
0
, and let
a:=p(B_{c})
and
b:=q(B_{c})
. Finally, assume that
1-a\leq\frac{1}{16}
and
1-b\leq\frac{1}{16}
.

Then

\displaystyle W_{2}^{2}(p,q)\leq

\displaystyle\frac{3}{m}\int_{B_{c}}{(\frac{q(x)}{p(x)}-1)}^{2}p(x)dx

\displaystyle\quad+2\max\left\{(1-a),(1-b)\right\}\cdot{(\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{{x\sim p(x)}}\left[{{\left\|x\right\|}_{2}^{4}}\right]}+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{{y\sim q(y)}}\left[{{\left\|y\right\|}_{2}^{4}}\right]})}

We defer the proof to Section
E.2.1
. We will apply Lemma
44
with

\displaystyle p:=p_{\delta}\qquad q:=\hat{p}_{\delta}\qquad m:=(k+1)\delta\qquad c:=\min\left\{\frac{1}{4\sqrt{L_{R}}},r\sqrt{k\delta}\right\}

To bound the
2\max\left\{(1-a),(1-b)\right\}\cdot{(\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{{x\sim p(x)}}\left[{{\left\|x\right\|}_{2}^{4}}\right]}+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{{y\sim q(y)}}\left[{{\left\|y\right\|}_{2}^{4}}\right]}+8)}
term, we apply Lemma
44
, with

We now bound
a,b,\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|x\right\|}_{2}^{4}}\right],\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|y\right\|}_{2}^{4}}\right]
: By Lemma
56
, and recalling our definition of
\mathbf{u}_{\delta}
and
\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{\delta}
,

\displaystyle 1-a\leq\mathbb{P}{({{\left\|\mathbf{u}_{\delta}\right\|}_{2}^{2}\geq r^{2}k\delta})}+\mathbb{P}{({{\left\|\mathbf{u}_{\delta}\right\|}_{2}^{2}\geq\frac{1}{4L_{R}}})}

\displaystyle 1-b\leq\mathbb{P}{({{\left\|\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{\delta}\right\|}_{2}^{2}\geq r^{2}k\delta})}+\mathbb{P}{({{\left\|\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{\delta}\right\|}_{2}^{2}\geq\frac{1}{4L_{R}}})}

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({{\left\|\mathbf{u}_{\delta}\right\|}_{2}^{2}\geq r^{2}k\delta})}\leq\exp{(\frac{-r^{2}k\delta+k\delta d}{8k\delta})}\leq 2\exp{(-\frac{r^{2}}{8})}

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({{\left\|\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{\delta}\right\|}_{2}^{2}\geq r^{2}k\delta})}\leq 2\exp{(-\frac{r^{2}k\delta}{8k\delta L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}})}\leq 2\exp{(\frac{-r^{2}}{8L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}})}

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({{\left\|\mathbf{u}_{\delta}\right\|}_{2}^{2}\geq\frac{1}{4\sqrt{L_{R}}}})}\leq 2\exp{(-\frac{1}{32k\delta L_{R}})}

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({{\left\|\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{\delta}\right\|}_{2}^{2}\geq\frac{1}{4\sqrt{L_{R}}}})}\leq 2\exp{(-\frac{1}{32k\delta L_{R}L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}})}

Step 2.1: bounding
4th
moments

To bound the fourth moment terms,

\displaystyle\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|\mathbf{u}_{\delta}\right\|}_{2}^{4}}\right]}\leq

\displaystyle 2^{8}L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}k\delta+\sqrt{\int_{0}^{\infty}s\exp{(-\frac{\sqrt{s}}{16k\delta})}ds}\leq 2^{10}L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}k\delta

where we use Assumption
E.1
:
k\delta\leq\frac{1}{L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}}
.

And

\displaystyle\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{\delta}\right\|}_{2}^{4}}\right]}\leq

\displaystyle 2^{10}L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}k\delta+\sqrt{\int_{0}^{\infty}s\exp{(-\frac{\sqrt{s}}{32k\delta})}ds}\leq 2^{12}L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}k\delta

where we use Assumption
E.1
:
k\delta\leq\frac{1}{L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}}
.

Combining all the results up to this point, we have

\displaystyle W_{2}^{2}{(p_{\delta},\hat{p}_{\delta})}\leq 3k\delta\int_{c}{(\frac{\hat{p}_{\delta}(x)}{p_{\delta}(x)}-1)}^{2}p_{\delta}(x)dx+2^{13}L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}k\delta\cdot{(\exp{(-\frac{r^{2}}{32L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}})}+\exp{(-\frac{1}{32k\delta L_{R}L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}})})}

Step 2.2: Bounding the
\chi^{2}
term

It remains to bound the
{(\frac{\hat{p}_{\delta}(x)}{p_{\delta}(x)}-1)}^{2}
term. This is the most difficult part of this section.

Lemma 45
.

Consider the same setup as Lemma
43
with
\mathbf{u}_{0},\mathbf{u}_{\delta},\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{\delta}
as defined in (
E.3
), with densities
p_{0},p_{\delta},\hat{p}_{\delta}
respectively. Let
{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbf{u}):=\mathbf{u}+\sqrt{\delta}{\mathbf{H}}(\mathbf{u})
. Assume
\delta\leq\frac{1}{16d^{2}{L_{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime}}^{2}}
. For any
r\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
, for any
{\left\|\hat{\mathbf{u}}\right\|}_{2}\leq\min\left\{\frac{1}{4\sqrt{L_{R}}},r\sqrt{k\delta}\right\}
, and for any
k\geq\max\left\{256r^{2}L_{{\mathbf{H}}}^{2},16L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}\right\}
,

\displaystyle{\left|\frac{\hat{p}_{\delta}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})}}{p_{\delta}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})}}-1\right|}^{2}\leq 2^{12}{\mathcal{C}}_{k}^{2}(1+r^{8})+\frac{16d^{2}}{{k}^{4}}

where
{\mathcal{C}}_{k}
is defined in (
E.11
).

We restate this lemma as Lemma
54
, as well as provide its proof, in the next section.

Assuming
k
satisfies the condition of Lemma
45
, then we can plug in the bound from Lemma
45
into our upper bound on
W_{2}^{2}(p_{\delta},\hat{p}_{\delta})
to get

\displaystyle W_{2}^{2}{(p_{\delta},\hat{p}_{\delta})}\leq

\displaystyle 2^{18}k\delta{({\mathcal{C}}_{k}^{2}(1+r^{8})+\frac{d^{2}}{{k}^{4}})}

\displaystyle\quad+2^{13}L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}k\delta\cdot{(\exp{(-\frac{r^{2}}{32L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}})}+\exp{(-\frac{1}{32k\delta L_{R}L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}})})}

(E.5)

The lemma statement follows by using
u_{\delta}:=\mathbf{z}_{k}-k\delta{\bm{\beta}}
.

∎

E.2
\chi^{2}
bound

Following the setup in the proof of Lemma
43
, let us define

\displaystyle p_{\delta}:=\text{ density of $\mathbf{u}_{\delta}$}

\displaystyle\hat{p}_{\delta}:=\text{ density of $\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{\delta}$}

\displaystyle\hat{p}_{0}:=\text{ density of $\mathbf{u}_{0}$}

for
\mathbf{u}_{\delta}
and
\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{\delta}
as defined in (
E.3
).

The goal of this section is to bound the term

\displaystyle{(\frac{\hat{p}_{\delta}(\mathbf{u})}{p_{\delta}(\mathbf{u})}-1)}^{2}

which is needed for the proof of Lemma
43
.

Our main result is Lemma
54
. Most of the work in this section goes into establishing a suitable approximatin of
\hat{p}_{\delta}
, which is given in lemma
52
.

Lemma 46
.

Consider the same setup as Lemma
43
with
\mathbf{u}_{0},\mathbf{u}_{\delta},\hat{\mathbf{u}}_{\delta}
as defined in (
E.3
), with densities
p_{0},p_{\delta},\hat{p}_{\delta}
respectively. Let
{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbf{u}):=\mathbf{u}+\sqrt{\delta}{\mathbf{H}}(\mathbf{u})
, then for all
\bar{\mathbf{u}}
satisfying
{\left\|\bar{\mathbf{u}}\right\|}_{2}\leq\frac{1}{4\sqrt{L_{R}}}
,

\displaystyle{\tilde{p}}_{\delta}(\hat{\mathbf{u}})=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{p_{0}{({\mathcal{G}}^{-1}(\hat{\mathbf{u}}))}{(\det{(\nabla{\mathcal{G}}{({\mathcal{G}}^{-1}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})})})})}^{-1}}\right]

where the expectation is wrt randomness in
{\mathbf{H}}
.

Proof of Lemma
46
.

In this proof, we need to bound the smoothness of
{\mathbf{H}}
for all points on the line
\ell(t)=t(\hat{\mathbf{u}})+(1-t){\mathcal{G}}^{-1}(\hat{\mathbf{u}})
,
t\in[0,1]
. From the assumption in (
C.5
), it suffices to ensure that for all
t\in[0,1]
,
{\left\|\ell(t)\right\|}_{2}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{L_{R}}}
. We already assumed that
{\left\|\bar{\mathbf{u}}\right\|}_{2}\leq\frac{1}{4\sqrt{L_{R}}}
. By Lemma
55
,
{\left\|{\mathcal{G}}^{-1}(\mathbf{u})\right\|}_{2}\leq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{L_{R}}}
, so
\ell(t)
being an interpolation, satisfies
{\left\|\ell(t)\right\|}_{2}\leq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{L_{R}}}
for all
t\in[0,1]
.

From the change of variable formula,

\displaystyle{\tilde{p}}_{\delta}(\hat{\mathbf{u}})=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{p_{0}{({\mathcal{G}}^{-1}(\hat{\mathbf{u}}))}\det{(\nabla{({\mathcal{G}})}^{-1}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})})}}\right]

By the inverse function theorem,

\displaystyle\nabla{\mathcal{G}}^{-1}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})}=

\displaystyle{(\nabla{\mathcal{G}}{({\mathcal{G}}^{-1}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})})})}^{-1}

Plugging in, and using the fact that
\det{(A^{-1})}=\frac{1}{\det{(A)}}
,

\displaystyle{\tilde{p}}_{\delta}(\hat{\mathbf{u}})=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{p_{0}{({\mathcal{G}}^{-1}(\hat{\mathbf{u}}))}{(\det{(\nabla{\mathcal{G}}{({\mathcal{G}}^{-1}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})})})})}^{-1}}\right]

∎

Lemma 47
.

Let
{\mathbf{H}}
satisfy (
E.4
). Let
\delta\leq\min\left\{\frac{1}{16L_{R}L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}},\frac{1}{16{L_{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime}}^{2}}\right\}
. Let
{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbf{u}):=\mathbf{u}+\sqrt{\delta}{\mathbf{H}}(\mathbf{u})
. Assume
\bar{\mathbf{u}}
satisfies
{\left\|\bar{\mathbf{u}}\right\|}_{2}\leq\frac{1}{4\sqrt{L_{R}}}
, and let
\mathbf{u}:={\mathcal{G}}^{-1}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})}
. Then

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{u}-\hat{\mathbf{u}}\right\|}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\delta}L_{{\mathbf{H}}}

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{u}-{(\hat{\mathbf{u}}-\sqrt{\delta}{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})})}\right\|}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\delta L_{{\mathbf{H}}}^{\prime}L_{{\mathbf{H}}}

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{u}-{(\hat{\mathbf{u}}-\sqrt{\delta}{\mathbf{H}}(\hat{\mathbf{u}})+{\delta}\nabla{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})}{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})})}\right\|}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\delta^{3/2}{(L_{{\mathbf{H}}}^{\prime\prime}L_{{\mathbf{H}}}^{2}+{(L_{{\mathbf{H}}}^{\prime})}^{2}L_{{\mathbf{H}}})}

Proof of Lemma
47
.

We define the line
\ell(t)=t(\hat{\mathbf{u}})+(1-t){\mathcal{G}}^{-1}(\hat{\mathbf{u}})
. Using identical steps as the proof of Lemma
46
, we can show that
{\left\|\ell(t)\right\|}_{2}\leq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{L_{R}}}
for all
t\in[0,1]
. Therefore, the Lipschitz derivative bounds for
{\mathbf{H}}
in (
C.5
) hold along
\ell(t)
. Note that
\hat{\mathbf{u}}=\ell(1)
and
\mathbf{u}=\ell(0)
.

By definition of
{\mathcal{G}}
,

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbf{u}}=\mathbf{u}+\sqrt{\delta}{\mathbf{H}}(\mathbf{u})

By Assumption
C.5
,
{\left\|\sqrt{\delta}{\mathbf{H}}{(\mathbf{u})}\right\|}\leq\sqrt{\delta}L_{{\mathbf{H}}}
. Thus

\displaystyle{\left\|\hat{\mathbf{u}}-\mathbf{u}\right\|}_{2}\leq\sqrt{\delta}L_{{\mathbf{H}}}

Next, by Taylor’s Theorem,

\displaystyle{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})}={\mathbf{H}}{(\mathbf{u})}+\int_{0}^{1}\nabla{\mathbf{H}}{(\mathbf{u}+t{(\hat{\mathbf{u}}-\mathbf{u})})}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}}-\mathbf{u})}dt

Therefore,

\displaystyle{\left\|{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})}-{\mathbf{H}}{(\mathbf{u})}\right\|}_{2}\leq

\displaystyle{\left\|\int_{0}^{1}\nabla{\mathbf{H}}{(\mathbf{u}+t{(\hat{\mathbf{u}}-\mathbf{u})})}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}}-\mathbf{u})}dt\right\|}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle L_{{\mathbf{H}}}^{\prime}{\left\|\hat{\mathbf{u}}-\mathbf{u}\right\|}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{\delta}L_{{\mathbf{H}}}^{\prime}L_{{\mathbf{H}}}

Thus

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{u}-\hat{\mathbf{u}}+\sqrt{\delta}{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})}\right\|}_{2}=

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{u}-\hat{\mathbf{u}}+\sqrt{\delta}{\mathbf{H}}{(\mathbf{u})}+\sqrt{\delta}{({\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})}-{\mathbf{H}}{(\mathbf{u})})}\right\|}_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\left\|\sqrt{\delta}{({\mathbf{H}}{(\mathbf{u})}-{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})})}\right\|}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\delta L_{{\mathbf{H}}}^{\prime}L_{{\mathbf{H}}}

Finally,

\displaystyle\nabla{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}}+t{(\mathbf{u}-\hat{\mathbf{u}})})}=\nabla{\mathbf{H}}{(\mathbf{u})}+\int_{0}^{t}\nabla^{2}{\mathbf{H}}{(\mathbf{u}+s{(\hat{\mathbf{u}}-\mathbf{u})})}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}}-\mathbf{u})}ds

Therefore,

\displaystyle{\left\|\nabla{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}}+t{(\mathbf{u}-\hat{\mathbf{u}})})}-\nabla{\mathbf{H}}{(\mathbf{u})}\right\|}_{2}\leq

\displaystyle{\left\|\int_{0}^{t}\nabla^{2}{\mathbf{H}}{(\mathbf{u}+s{(\hat{\mathbf{u}}-\mathbf{u})})}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}}-\mathbf{u})}ds\right\|}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle tL_{{\mathbf{H}}}^{\prime\prime}{\left\|\hat{\mathbf{u}}-\mathbf{u}\right\|}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle t\sqrt{\delta}L_{{\mathbf{H}}}^{\prime\prime}L_{{\mathbf{H}}}

By Taylor’s Theorem,

\displaystyle{\mathbf{H}}(\mathbf{u})=

\displaystyle{\mathbf{H}}(\hat{\mathbf{u}})+\int_{0}^{1}\nabla{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}}+t{(\mathbf{u}-\hat{\mathbf{u}})})}{(\mathbf{u}-\hat{\mathbf{u}})}dt

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathbf{H}}(\hat{\mathbf{u}})+\nabla{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})}{(\mathbf{u}-\hat{\mathbf{u}})}

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{1}{(\nabla{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}}+t{(\mathbf{u}-\hat{\mathbf{u}})})}-\nabla{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})})}{(\mathbf{u}-\hat{\mathbf{u}})}dt

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathbf{H}}(\hat{\mathbf{u}})-\sqrt{\delta}\nabla{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})}{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})}

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{1}{(\nabla{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}}+t{(\mathbf{u}-\hat{\mathbf{u}})})}-\nabla{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})})}{(\mathbf{u}-\hat{\mathbf{u}})}dt

\displaystyle\quad+\nabla{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})}{(\mathbf{u}-\hat{\mathbf{u}}+\sqrt{\delta}{\mathbf{H}}{(\mathbf{u})})}

Therefore,

\displaystyle{\left\|{\mathbf{H}}(\mathbf{u})-{\mathbf{H}}(\hat{\mathbf{u}})+\sqrt{\delta}\nabla{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})}{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})}\right\|}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\left\|\int_{0}^{1}{(\nabla{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}}+t{(\mathbf{u}-\hat{\mathbf{u}})})}-\nabla{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})})}{(\mathbf{u}-\hat{\mathbf{u}})}dt\right\|}_{2}

\displaystyle\quad+{\left\|\nabla{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})}{(\mathbf{u}-\hat{\mathbf{u}}+\sqrt{\delta}{\mathbf{H}}{(\mathbf{u})})}\right\|}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\delta L_{{\mathbf{H}}}^{\prime\prime}L_{{\mathbf{H}}}^{2}+\delta{(L_{{\mathbf{H}}}^{\prime})}^{2}L_{{\mathbf{H}}}

Finally,

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{u}-{(\hat{\mathbf{u}}-\sqrt{\delta}{\mathbf{H}}(\hat{\mathbf{u}})+{\delta}\nabla{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})}{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})})}\right\|}_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{u}-\hat{\mathbf{u}}+\sqrt{\delta}{\mathbf{H}}{(\mathbf{u})}-{(\sqrt{\delta}{\mathbf{H}}{(\mathbf{u})}-\sqrt{\delta}{\mathbf{H}}(\hat{\mathbf{u}})+\delta\nabla{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})}{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})})}\right\|}_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\left\|\sqrt{\delta}{\mathbf{H}}{(\mathbf{u})}-\sqrt{\delta}{\mathbf{H}}(\hat{\mathbf{u}})+\delta\nabla{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})}{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})}\right\|}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\delta^{3/2}L_{{\mathbf{H}}}^{\prime\prime}L_{{\mathbf{H}}}^{2}+\delta^{3/2}{(L_{{\mathbf{H}}}^{\prime})}^{2}L_{{\mathbf{H}}}

∎

Lemma 48
.

Let
{\mathbf{H}}
satisfy (
E.4
). Let
\delta\leq\min\left\{\frac{1}{16L_{R}L_{\mathbf{H}}^{2}},\frac{1}{16{L_{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime}}^{2}}\right\}
. Let
{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbf{u}):=\mathbf{u}+\sqrt{\delta}{\mathbf{H}}(\mathbf{u})
. Assume
\bar{\mathbf{u}}
satisfies
{\left\|\bar{\mathbf{u}}\right\|}_{2}\leq\frac{1}{4\sqrt{L_{R}}}
, and let
\mathbf{u}:={\mathcal{G}}^{-1}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})}
. Then

\displaystyle{\left|{\left\|{\mathcal{G}}^{-1}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})}\right\|}_{2}^{2}-{\left\|\hat{\mathbf{u}}\right\|}_{2}^{2}\right|}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\delta}{(2L_{{\mathbf{H}}}{\left\|\hat{\mathbf{u}}\right\|}_{2}+\sqrt{\delta}L_{{\mathbf{H}}}^{2})}

\displaystyle{\left|{\left\|{\mathcal{G}}^{-1}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})}\right\|}_{2}^{2}-{({\left\|\hat{\mathbf{u}}\right\|}_{2}^{2}-2\sqrt{\delta}\bm{\left\langle}\hat{\mathbf{u}},{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})}\bm{})}\right|}

\displaystyle\leq\delta{(2L_{{\mathbf{H}}}^{\prime}L_{{\mathbf{H}}}{\left\|\hat{\mathbf{u}}\right\|}_{2}+L_{{\mathbf{H}}}^{2})}

and

\displaystyle{\left|{\left\|{\mathcal{G}}^{-1}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})}\right\|}_{2}^{2}-{({\left\|\hat{\mathbf{u}}\right\|}_{2}^{2}-2\sqrt{\delta}\bm{\left\langle}\hat{\mathbf{u}},{\mathbf{H}}(\hat{\mathbf{u}})\bm{}+\delta\bm{\left\langle}\hat{\mathbf{u}},\nabla{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})}{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})}\bm{}+\delta{\left\|{\mathbf{H}}(\hat{\mathbf{u}})\right\|}_{2}^{2})}\right|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\delta^{3/2}{({(L_{{\mathbf{H}}}^{\prime\prime}L_{{\mathbf{H}}}^{2}+{(L_{{\mathbf{H}}}^{\prime})}^{2}L_{{\mathbf{H}}})}{\left\|\hat{\mathbf{u}}\right\|}_{2}+2L_{{\mathbf{H}}}^{\prime}L_{{\mathbf{H}}}^{2}+\sqrt{\delta}{(L_{{\mathbf{H}}}^{\prime}L_{{\mathbf{H}}})}^{2})}

Proof of Lemma
48
.

From Lemma
47
,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{G}}^{-1}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})}=\hat{\mathbf{u}}-\sqrt{\delta}{\mathbf{H}}(\hat{\mathbf{u}})+{\delta}\nabla{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})}{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})}+\gamma_{3}

for some
\gamma_{3}
satisfying

\displaystyle{\left\|\gamma_{3}-\sqrt{\delta}{\mathbf{H}}(\hat{\mathbf{u}})+{\delta}\nabla{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})}{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})}\right\|}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\delta}L_{{\mathbf{H}}}

\displaystyle{\left\|\gamma_{3}+{\delta}\nabla{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})}{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})}\right\|}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\delta L_{{\mathbf{H}}}^{\prime}L_{{\mathbf{H}}}

\displaystyle{\left\|\gamma_{3}\right\|}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\delta^{3/2}{(L_{{\mathbf{H}}}^{\prime\prime}L_{{\mathbf{H}}}^{2}+{(L_{{\mathbf{H}}}^{\prime})}^{2}L_{{\mathbf{H}}})}

Expanding the quadratic, we get

\displaystyle{\left\|{\mathcal{G}}^{-1}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})}\right\|}_{2}^{2}=

\displaystyle{\left\|\hat{\mathbf{u}}-\sqrt{\delta}{\mathbf{H}}(\hat{\mathbf{u}})+{\delta}\nabla{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})}{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})}+\gamma_{3}\right\|}_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\left\|\hat{\mathbf{u}}\right\|}_{2}^{2}+2\bm{\left\langle}\hat{\mathbf{u}},-\sqrt{\delta}{\mathbf{H}}(\hat{\mathbf{u}})+{\delta}\nabla{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})}{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})}+\gamma_{3}\bm{}

\displaystyle\quad+{\left\|-\sqrt{\delta}{\mathbf{H}}(\hat{\mathbf{u}})+{\delta}\nabla{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})}{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})}+\gamma_{3}\right\|}_{2}^{2}

(E.6)

Using the preceding bounds,

\displaystyle{\left|2\bm{\left\langle}\hat{\mathbf{u}},-\sqrt{\delta}{\mathbf{H}}(\hat{\mathbf{u}})+{\delta}\nabla{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})}{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})}+\gamma_{3}\bm{}\right|}\leq

\displaystyle 2\sqrt{\delta}L_{{\mathbf{H}}}{\left\|\hat{\mathbf{u}}\right\|}_{2}

so that

\displaystyle{\left|{\left\|{\mathcal{G}}^{-1}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})}\right\|}_{2}^{2}-{\left\|\hat{\mathbf{u}}\right\|}_{2}^{2}\right|}\leq\sqrt{\delta}{(2L_{{\mathbf{H}}}{\left\|\hat{\mathbf{u}}\right\|}_{2}+\sqrt{\delta}L_{{\mathbf{H}}}^{2})}

This gives our first bound.

Next, using the bound on
{\left\|\gamma_{3}+{\delta}\nabla{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})}{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})}\right\|}_{2}\leq\delta L_{{\mathbf{H}}}^{\prime}L_{{\mathbf{H}}}
, and splitting the inner product term in (
E.6
), we get our second bound

\displaystyle{\left|{\left\|{\mathcal{G}}^{-1}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})}\right\|}_{2}^{2}-{({\left\|\hat{\mathbf{u}}\right\|}_{2}^{2}-2\sqrt{\delta}\bm{\left\langle}\hat{\mathbf{u}},{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})}\bm{})}\right|}\leq\delta{(2L_{{\mathbf{H}}}^{\prime}L_{{\mathbf{H}}}{\left\|\hat{\mathbf{u}}\right\|}_{2}+L_{{\mathbf{H}}}^{2})}

Finally,

\displaystyle 2\bm{\left\langle}\hat{\mathbf{u}},-\sqrt{\delta}{\mathbf{H}}(\hat{\mathbf{u}})+{\delta}\nabla{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})}{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})}+\gamma_{3}\bm{}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\bm{\left\langle}\hat{\mathbf{u}},-\sqrt{\delta}{\mathbf{H}}(\hat{\mathbf{u}})+{\delta}\nabla{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})}{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})}\bm{}+2\bm{\left\langle}\hat{\mathbf{u}},\gamma_{3}\bm{}

where

\displaystyle\bm{\left\langle}\hat{\mathbf{u}},\gamma_{3}\bm{}\leq{\left\|\hat{\mathbf{u}}\right\|}_{2}{\left\|\gamma_{3}\right\|}_{2}=\delta^{3/2}{(L_{{\mathbf{H}}}^{\prime\prime}L_{{\mathbf{H}}}^{2}+{(L_{{\mathbf{H}}}^{\prime})}^{2}L_{{\mathbf{H}}})}{\left\|\hat{\mathbf{u}}\right\|}_{2}

Next, we consider the last term in (
E.6
),

\displaystyle{\left\|-\sqrt{\delta}{\mathbf{H}}(\hat{\mathbf{u}})+{\delta}\nabla{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})}{\mathbf{H}}{(\hat{\mathbf{u}})}+\gamma_{3}\right\|}_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=
