Let us define the variational field

\displaystyle\Lambda(s,t)=\Exp_{z}{(t{(u+s(v-u))})}

We verify that

\displaystyle\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)=

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,0)=

\displaystyle u+s(v-u)

\displaystyle D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)=

\displaystyle v-u

Lemma
61
then immediately gives

\displaystyle{\left\|\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)\right\|}\leq\frac{\sinh{(\sqrt{L_{R}}{({\left\|u\right\|}+{\left\|v\right\|})}t)}}{\sqrt{L_{R}}{({\left\|u\right\|}+{\left\|v\right\|})}}{\left\|v-u\right\|}

∎

Lemma 63
.

Let
x,y\in M
. Let
\gamma(s):[0,1]\to M
be a minimizing geodesic between
x
and
y
with
\gamma(0)=x
and
\gamma(1)=y
. Let
u\in T_{x}M
and
v\in T_{y}M
. Let
u(s)
and
v(s)
be the parallel transport of
u
and
v
along
\gamma
, with
u(0)=u
and
v(1)=v
.

Then

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(\Exp_{x}(u),\Exp_{y}(v))}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle{(1+4{\mathcal{C}}^{2}e^{4{\mathcal{C}}})}\mathrm{d}{(x,y)}^{2}+32e^{{\mathcal{C}}}{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|}^{2}+2\bm{\left\langle}\gamma^{\prime}(0),v(0)-u(0)\bm{}

where
{\mathcal{C}}:=\sqrt{L_{R}}{({\left\|u\right\|}+{\left\|v\right\|})}
.

Proof.

Let us consider the length function
E(\gamma)=\int_{0}^{1}{\left\|\gamma^{\prime}(s)\right\|}^{2}ds
. We define a variation of geodesics
\Lambda(s,t)
:

\displaystyle\Lambda(s,t):=\Exp_{\gamma(s)}{(t{(u(s)+s(v(s)-u(s)))})}

We verify that

\displaystyle\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)=

\displaystyle\gamma^{\prime}(s)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,0)=

\displaystyle u(s)+s(v(s)-u(s))

\displaystyle D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)=

\displaystyle v(s)-u(s)

Consider a fixed
t
, and let
\gamma_{t}(s):=\Lambda(s,t)
(so
\gamma_{t}^{\prime}(s)
is the velocity wrt
s
).

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}E(\gamma_{t})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\int_{0}^{1}{\left\|\gamma_{t}^{\prime}(s)\right\|}^{2}ds

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}2\bm{\left\langle}\gamma_{t}^{\prime}(s),D_{t}\gamma_{t}^{\prime}(s)\bm{}ds

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}2\bm{\left\langle}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t),D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)\bm{}ds

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}2\bm{\left\langle}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0),D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\bm{}ds

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{1}2\bm{\left\langle}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0),\left\{D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)\right\}^{\to\Lambda(s,0)}-D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\bm{}ds

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{1}2\bm{\left\langle}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)-\left\{\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\}^{\to\Lambda(s,t)},D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)\bm{}ds

(F.2)

For any
s
, and for
t=0
,
\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)=\gamma^{\prime}(s)
and
D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)=v(s)-u(s)
. Using the fact that norms and inner products are preserved under parallel transport, the first term can be simplified as

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}2\bm{\left\langle}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0),D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\bm{}ds=2\bm{\left\langle}\gamma^{\prime}(0),v(0)-u(0)\bm{}

To bound the second and third term, we use Lemma
61
:

\displaystyle{\left\|\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}=

\displaystyle{\left\|\gamma^{\prime}(0)\right\|}

and

\displaystyle{\left\|D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)-\Gamma_{\Lambda(s,0)}^{\Lambda(s,t)}{(D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0))}\right\|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{L_{R}{\left\|\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}^{2}}\sinh{(\sqrt{L_{R}{\left\|\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}^{2}})}{\left\|\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}+{(\cosh{(\sqrt{L_{R}{\left\|\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}^{2}})}-1)}{\left\|D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}\sinh{({\mathcal{C}})}{\left\|\gamma^{\prime}(0)\right\|}+{(\cosh{({\mathcal{C}})}-1)}{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|}

where we use the fact that
\sqrt{L_{R}{\left\|\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}^{2}}\leq{\mathcal{C}}
.

We can thus bound the second term of (
F.2
) as

\displaystyle{\left|\int_{0}^{1}2\bm{\left\langle}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0),\left\{D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)\right\}^{\to\Lambda(s,0)}-D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\bm{}ds\right|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2{\left\|\gamma^{\prime}(0)\right\|}\cdot{({\mathcal{C}}\sinh{({\mathcal{C}})}{\left\|\gamma^{\prime}(0)\right\|}+{(\cosh{({\mathcal{C}})}-1)}{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4{\left\|\gamma^{\prime}(0)\right\|}^{2}{({\mathcal{C}}\sinh{({\mathcal{C}})}+{(\cosh{({\mathcal{C}})}-1)}^{2})}+4{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|}^{2}

Finally, to bound the third term of (
F.2
), we again apply Lemma
61
:

\displaystyle{\left\|\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)-\Gamma_{\Lambda(s,0)}^{\Lambda(s,t)}{(\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0))}\right\|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{(\cosh{(\sqrt{L_{R}{\left\|\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}^{2}})}-1)}{\left\|\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}+{(\frac{\sinh{(\sqrt{L_{R}{\left\|\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}^{2}})}}{\sqrt{L_{R}{\left\|\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}^{2}}})}{\left\|D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{(\cosh{(\sqrt{L_{R}}{({\left\|u(s)\right\|}+{\left\|v(s)\right\|})})}-1)}{\left\|\gamma^{\prime}(s)\right\|}+{(\frac{\sinh{(\sqrt{L_{R}}{({\left\|u(s)\right\|}+{\left\|v(s)\right\|})})}}{\sqrt{L_{R}}{({\left\|u(s)\right\|}+{\left\|v(s)\right\|})}})}{\left\|v(s)-u(s)\right\|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{(\cosh{({\mathcal{C}})}-1)}{\left\|\gamma^{\prime}(0)\right\|}+{(\frac{\sinh{({\mathcal{C}})}}{{\mathcal{C}}})}{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|}

and

\displaystyle{\left\|D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)\right\|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{L_{R}{\left\|\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}^{2}}\sinh{(\sqrt{L_{R}{\left\|\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}^{2}})}{\left\|\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}+\cosh{(\sqrt{L_{R}{\left\|\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}^{2}})}{\left\|D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{L_{R}}{({\left\|u\right\|}+{\left\|v\right\|})}\sinh{(\sqrt{L_{R}}{({\left\|u\right\|}+{\left\|v\right\|})})}{\left\|\gamma^{\prime}(0)\right\|}+\cosh{(\sqrt{L_{R}}{({\left\|u\right\|}+{\left\|v\right\|})})}{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}\sinh{({\mathcal{C}})}{\left\|\gamma^{\prime}(0)\right\|}+\cosh{({\mathcal{C}})}{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|}

for
0\leq t\leq 1
, where we usse the fact that
\cosh(r)
and
\frac{\sinh(r)}{r}
are monotonically increasing in
r
.
Put together, the third term of (
F.2
) is bounded as

\displaystyle{\left|\int_{0}^{1}2\bm{\left\langle}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)-\left\{\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\}^{\to\Lambda(s,t)},D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)\bm{}ds\right|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2{({(\cosh{({\mathcal{C}})}-1)}{\left\|\gamma^{\prime}(0)\right\|}+{(\frac{\sinh{({\mathcal{C}})}}{{\mathcal{C}}})}{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|})}\cdot{({\mathcal{C}}\sinh{({\mathcal{C}})}{\left\|\gamma^{\prime}(0)\right\|}+\cosh{({\mathcal{C}})}{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 8{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|}^{2}{(\cosh{({\mathcal{C}})}^{2}+\frac{\sinh{({\mathcal{C}})}^{2}}{{\mathcal{C}}^{2}})}+8{\left\|\gamma^{\prime}(0)\right\|}^{2}{({(\cosh{({\mathcal{C}})}-1)}^{2}+{\mathcal{C}}^{2}\sinh{({\mathcal{C}})}^{2})}

Put together, we get

\displaystyle{\left|\frac{d}{dt}E(\gamma_{t})-2\bm{\left\langle}\gamma^{\prime}(0),v(0)-u(0)\bm{}\right|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 8{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|}^{2}{(\cosh{({\mathcal{C}})}^{2}+\frac{\sinh{({\mathcal{C}})}^{2}}{{\mathcal{C}}^{2}})}+8{\left\|\gamma^{\prime}(0)\right\|}^{2}{({(\cosh{({\mathcal{C}})}-1)}^{2}+{\mathcal{C}}^{2}\sinh{({\mathcal{C}})}^{2})}

\displaystyle\quad+4{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|}^{2}+4{\left\|\gamma^{\prime}(0)\right\|}^{2}{({\mathcal{C}}\sinh{({\mathcal{C}})}+{(\cosh{({\mathcal{C}})}-1)}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 8{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|}^{2}{(\cosh{({\mathcal{C}})}^{2}+\frac{\sinh{({\mathcal{C}})}^{2}}{{\mathcal{C}}^{2}}+1)}

\displaystyle\quad+8{\left\|\gamma^{\prime}(0)\right\|}^{2}{(2{(\cosh{({\mathcal{C}})}-1)}^{2}+{\mathcal{C}}^{2}\sinh{({\mathcal{C}})}^{2}+{\mathcal{C}}\sinh{({\mathcal{C}})})}

Integrating for
t=\in[0,1]
, and noting that
E(\gamma_{0})={\left\|\gamma^{\prime}(0)\right\|}
,

\displaystyle E{(\gamma_{1})}\leq

\displaystyle{(1+8{(2{(\cosh{({\mathcal{C}})}-1)}^{2}+{\mathcal{C}}^{2}\sinh{({\mathcal{C}})}^{2}+{\mathcal{C}}\sinh{({\mathcal{C}})})})}E{(\gamma_{0})}

\displaystyle\qquad+8{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|}^{2}{(\cosh{({\mathcal{C}})}^{2}+\frac{\sinh{({\mathcal{C}})}^{2}}{{\mathcal{C}}^{2}}+1)}

\displaystyle\qquad+2\bm{\left\langle}\gamma^{\prime}(0),v(0)-u(0)\bm{}

From Lemma
14
, we can upper bound

\displaystyle 8{(2{(\cosh{({\mathcal{C}})}-1)}^{2}+{\mathcal{C}}^{2}\sinh{({\mathcal{C}})}^{2}+{\mathcal{C}}\sinh{({\mathcal{C}})})}\leq

\displaystyle 8r^{4}e^{2r}+8r^{4}e^{2r}+r^{2}e^{r}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4r^{2}e^{4r}

\displaystyle\cosh{({\mathcal{C}})}^{2}+\frac{\sinh{({\mathcal{C}})}^{2}}{{\mathcal{C}}^{2}}+1\leq

\displaystyle 4e^{2r}

where we use the fact that
r^{2}\leq e^{2r}/6
for all
r\geq 0
.

The conclusion follows by noting that
\mathrm{d}(x,y)=\sqrt{E{(\gamma_{0})}}
and
\mathrm{d}{(\Exp_{x}(u),\Exp_{y}(v))}\leq\sqrt{E{(\gamma_{1})}}
.

∎

Lemma 64
.

Let
x,y\in M
. Let
\gamma(s):[0,1]\to M
be a minimizing geodesic between
x
and
y
with
\gamma(0)=x
and
\gamma(1)=y
. Let
u\in T_{x}M
and
v\in T_{y}M
. Let
u(s)
and
v(s)
be the parallel transport of
u
and
v
along
\gamma
. Let
u=u_{1}+u_{2}
and
v=v_{1}+v_{2}
be a decomposition such that
v_{2}=\Gamma_{x}^{y}u_{2}
, where the parallel transport is along
\gamma(s)
.

Let us define
u_{1}(s),u_{2}(s),v_{1}(s)
, all mapping from
[0,1]\to T_{\gamma(s)}M
, such that they are the parallel transport of
u_{1},u_{2},v_{1}
along
\gamma(s)
respectively (
u_{1}(0)=u_{1}
,
u_{2}(0)=u_{2}
,
v_{1}(1)=v_{1}
,
u_{2}(1)=v_{2}
)

Then

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(\Exp_{x}(u),\Exp_{y}(v))}^{2}-\mathrm{d}{(x,y)}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\bm{\left\langle}\gamma^{\prime}(0),v(0)-u(0)\bm{}+{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad-2\int_{0}^{1}\bm{\left\langle}R{(\gamma^{\prime}(s),(1-s)u(s)+sv(s))}(1-s)u(s)+sv(s),\gamma^{\prime}(s)\bm{}ds

\displaystyle\quad+{(2{\mathcal{C}}^{2}e^{{\mathcal{C}}}+18{\mathcal{C}}^{4}e^{2{\mathcal{C}}})}{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|}^{2}+{(18{\mathcal{C}}^{4}e^{2{\mathcal{C}}}+4{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}{(x,y)}^{2}+4{\mathcal{C}}^{2}e^{2{\mathcal{C}}}\mathrm{d}{(x,y)}{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|}

where
{\mathcal{C}}:=\sqrt{L_{R}}{({\left\|u\right\|}+{\left\|v\right\|})}
and
{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}:=L_{R}^{\prime}{({\left\|u\right\|}+{\left\|v\right\|})}^{3}
.

Proof.

The proof is similar to Lemma
63
. Let us consider the length function
E(\gamma)=\int_{0}^{1}{\left\|\gamma^{\prime}(s)\right\|}^{2}ds
. We define a variation of geodesics
\Lambda(s,t)
:

\displaystyle\Lambda(s,t):=\Exp_{\gamma(s)}{(t{(u(s)+s(v(s)-u(s)))})}

We verify that

\displaystyle\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)=

\displaystyle\gamma^{\prime}(s)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,0)=

\displaystyle u(s)+s(v(s)-u(s))

\displaystyle D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)=

\displaystyle v(s)-u(s)

Consider a fixed
t
, and let
\gamma_{t}(s):=\Lambda(s,t)
(so
\gamma_{t}^{\prime}(s)
is the velocity wrt
s
).

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}E(\gamma_{t})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\int_{0}^{1}{\left\|\gamma_{t}^{\prime}(s)\right\|}^{2}ds

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}2\bm{\left\langle}\gamma_{t}^{\prime}(s),D_{t}\gamma_{t}^{\prime}(s)\bm{}ds

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}2\bm{\left\langle}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t),D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)\bm{}ds

and

\displaystyle\frac{d^{2}}{dt^{2}}E(\gamma_{t})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}2\bm{\left\langle}D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t),D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)\bm{}ds+\int_{0}^{1}2\bm{\left\langle}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t),D_{t}D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)\bm{}ds

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}2{\left\|D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)\right\|}^{2}ds-\int_{0}^{1}2\bm{\left\langle}R{(\partial_{s}(\Lambda(s,t)),\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,t))}\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,t),\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)\bm{}ds

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}2{\left\|D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)\right\|}^{2}ds-\int_{0}^{1}2\bm{\left\langle}R{(\partial_{s}(\Lambda(s,t)),\Gamma_{\Lambda(s,0)}^{\Lambda(s,t)}\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,0))}\Gamma_{\Lambda(s,0)}^{\Lambda(s,t)}\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,t),\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)\bm{}ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}2{\left\|D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)\right\|}^{2}ds-\int_{0}^{1}2\bm{\left\langle}R{(\partial_{s}(\Lambda(s,0)),\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,0))}\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,t),\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\bm{}ds

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{1}4L_{R}{\left\|\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}^{2}{\left\|\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)\right\|}{\left\|\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)-\Gamma_{\Lambda(s,0)}^{\Lambda(s,t)}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}+4L_{R}^{\prime}{\left\|\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}^{3}{\left\|\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}^{2}ds

where the second equality uses the Jacobi equation.

The Riemannian curvature tensor term can be simplified as

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{1}2\bm{\left\langle}R{(\partial_{s}(\Lambda(s,0)),\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,0))}\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,t),\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\bm{}ds

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\int_{0}^{1}\bm{\left\langle}R{(\gamma^{\prime}(s),(1-s)u(s)+sv(s))}(1-s)u(s)+sv(s),\gamma^{\prime}(s)\bm{}ds

We further bound

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}2{\left\|D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)\right\|}^{2}ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}2{({{\mathcal{C}}}\sinh{({{\mathcal{C}}})}{\left\|\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}+\cosh{({{\mathcal{C}}})}{\left\|D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|})}^{2}ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\cosh{({\mathcal{C}})}^{2}\int_{0}^{1}{\left\|D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}^{2}ds

\displaystyle\quad+2{{\mathcal{C}}}^{2}\sinh{({{\mathcal{C}}})}^{2}\int_{0}^{1}{\left\|\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}^{2}ds+4{\mathcal{C}}\int_{0}^{1}\sinh{({\mathcal{C}})}\cosh{({\mathcal{C}})}{\left\|\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}{\left\|D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}ds

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\cosh{({\mathcal{C}})}^{2}{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+2{{\mathcal{C}}}^{2}\sinh{({{\mathcal{C}}})}^{2}\mathrm{d}{(x,y)}^{2}+4{\mathcal{C}}\int_{0}^{1}\sinh{({\mathcal{C}})}\cosh{({\mathcal{C}})}{\left\|\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}{\left\|D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|}^{2}+2{({\mathcal{C}}^{2}e^{{\mathcal{C}}}+{\mathcal{C}}^{4}e^{2{\mathcal{C}}})}{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+2{\mathcal{C}}^{4}e^{2{\mathcal{C}}}\mathrm{d}{(x,y)}^{2}+4{\mathcal{C}}^{2}e^{2{\mathcal{C}}}\mathrm{d}{(x,y)}{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|}

where we use Lemma
61
and Lemma
14
.

We also bound

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}4L_{R}{\left\|\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}^{2}{\left\|\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)\right\|}{\left\|\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)-\Gamma_{\Lambda(s,0)}^{\Lambda(s,t)}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4{\mathcal{C}}^{2}{(\cosh{({\mathcal{C}})}\mathrm{d}{(x,y)}+\frac{\sinh{({\mathcal{C}})}}{{\mathcal{C}}}{\left\|u(0)-v(0)\right\|})}{({(\cosh{({\mathcal{C}})}-1)}\mathrm{d}{(x,y)}+{(\frac{\sinh{({\mathcal{C}})}}{{\mathcal{C}}}-1)}{\left\|u(0)-v(0)\right\|})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 16{\mathcal{C}}^{4}e^{2{\mathcal{C}}}\mathrm{d}{(x,y)}^{2}+16{\mathcal{C}}^{4}e^{2{\mathcal{C}}}{\left\|u(0)-v(0)\right\|}^{2}

where we use Lemma
61
and Lemma
14
.

We finally bound

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}4L_{R}^{\prime}{\left\|\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}^{3}{\left\|\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}^{2}ds\leq 4{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}\mathrm{d}{(x,y)}^{2}

by definition of
{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}
.

Combining the above bounds,

\displaystyle E{(\gamma_{1})}=\left.\frac{d}{dt}E{(\gamma_{t})}\right|_{t=0}+\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{r}\frac{d^{2}}{dt^{2}}E{(\gamma_{t})}dtdr

\displaystyle\quad\leq\ 2\bm{\left\langle}\gamma^{\prime}(0),v(0)-u(0)\bm{}+{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad-2\int_{0}^{1}\bm{\left\langle}R{(\gamma^{\prime}(s),(1-s)u(s)+sv(s))}(1-s)u(s)+sv(s),\gamma^{\prime}(s)\bm{}ds

\displaystyle\quad+2{({\mathcal{C}}^{2}e^{{\mathcal{C}}}+{\mathcal{C}}^{4}e^{2{\mathcal{C}}})}{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|}^{2}+2{\mathcal{C}}^{4}e^{2{\mathcal{C}}}\mathrm{d}{(x,y)}^{2}+4{\mathcal{C}}^{2}e^{2{\mathcal{C}}}\mathrm{d}{(x,y)}{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|}

\displaystyle\quad+16{\mathcal{C}}^{4}e^{2{\mathcal{C}}}\mathrm{d}{(x,y)}^{2}+16{\mathcal{C}}^{4}e^{2{\mathcal{C}}}{\left\|u(0)-v(0)\right\|}^{2}+4{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}\mathrm{d}{(x,y)}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad=2\bm{\left\langle}\gamma^{\prime}(0),v(0)-u(0)\bm{}+{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad-2\int_{0}^{1}\bm{\left\langle}R{(\gamma^{\prime}(s),(1-s)u(s)+sv(s))}(1-s)u(s)+sv(s),\gamma^{\prime}(s)\bm{}ds

\displaystyle\quad+{(2{\mathcal{C}}^{2}e^{{\mathcal{C}}}+18{\mathcal{C}}^{4}e^{2{\mathcal{C}}})}{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|}^{2}+{(18{\mathcal{C}}^{4}e^{2{\mathcal{C}}}+4{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}{(x,y)}^{2}+4{\mathcal{C}}^{2}e^{2{\mathcal{C}}}\mathrm{d}{(x,y)}{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|}

Our conclusion follows as
\mathrm{d}{(\Exp_{x}(u),\Exp_{y}(v))}^{2}\leq E(\gamma_{1})
.
∎

Lemma 65
.

Let
a_{t},b_{t}:t\to\mathbb{R}^{+}
satisfy

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}a_{t}=b_{t}

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}b_{t}\leq Ca_{t}

with initial conditions
a_{0},b_{0}
, then for all
t
,

\displaystyle a_{t}\leq{a_{0}}\cosh{(\sqrt{C}t)}+\frac{b_{0}}{\sqrt{C}}\sinh{(\sqrt{C}t)}

\displaystyle b_{t}\leq\sqrt{C}{({a_{0}}\sinh{(\sqrt{C}t)}+\frac{b_{0}}{\sqrt{C}}\cosh{(\sqrt{C}t)})}

Proof.

Let
x_{t}:={a_{0}}\cosh{(\sqrt{C}t)}+\frac{b_{0}}{\sqrt{C}}\sinh{(\sqrt{C}t)}
and
y_{t}:=\sqrt{C}{({a_{0}}\sinh{(\sqrt{C}t)}+\frac{b_{0}}{\sqrt{C}}\cosh{(\sqrt{C}t)})}
. We verify that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}x_{t}=\sqrt{C}{({a_{0}}\sinh{(\sqrt{C}t)}+\frac{b_{0}}{\sqrt{C}}\cosh{(\sqrt{C}t)})}=y_{t}

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}y_{t}=C{({a_{0}}\cosh{(\sqrt{C}t)}+\frac{b_{0}}{\sqrt{C}}\sinh{(\sqrt{C}t)})}=Cx_{t}

We further verify the initial conditions. Note that
\sinh(0)=0
and
cosh(0)=1
. Thus

\displaystyle x_{0}=a_{0}

\displaystyle y_{0}=b_{0}

Finally, we verify that
a_{t}\leq x_{t}
and
b_{t}\leq y_{t}
for all
t
:

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}x_{t}-a_{t}=y_{t}-b_{t}

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}y_{t}-b_{t}\geq C{(x_{t}-a_{t})}

∎

Lemma 66
.

Let
a_{t},b_{t}:t\to\mathbb{R}^{+}
satisfy

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}a_{t}=b_{t}

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}b_{t}\leq Ca_{t}+Dt+E

with initial conditions
a_{0}=0,b_{0}=0
, then for all
t
,

\displaystyle a_{t}\leq\frac{E}{C}\cosh(\sqrt{C}t)+\frac{D}{C^{3/2}}\sinh{(\sqrt{C}t)}-\frac{D}{C}t-\frac{E}{C}

\displaystyle b_{t}\leq\frac{E}{\sqrt{C}}\sinh(\sqrt{C}t)+\frac{D}{C}\cosh{(\sqrt{C}t)}-\frac{D}{C}

Proof.

Let
x_{t}:=\frac{E}{C}\cosh(\sqrt{C}t)+\frac{D}{C^{3/2}}\sinh{(\sqrt{C}t)}-\frac{D}{C}t-\frac{E}{C}
and

y_{t}:=\frac{E}{\sqrt{C}}\sinh(\sqrt{C}t)+\frac{D}{C}\cosh{(\sqrt{C}t)}-\frac{D}{C}
.

We verify that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}x_{t}=\frac{E}{\sqrt{C}}\sinh(\sqrt{C}t)+\frac{D}{C}\cosh{(\sqrt{C}t)}-\frac{D}{C}=y_{t}

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}y_{t}=E\cosh(\sqrt{C}t)+\frac{D}{\sqrt{C}}\sinh{(\sqrt{C}t)}=Cx_{t}+Dt+E

we also verify the initial conditions that
x_{0}=0
and
y_{0}=0
.

∎

Lemma 67
.

Let
x,y\in M
, and let
E_{1}...E_{d}
be an orthonormal basis at
T_{x}M
.
Let
v\in T_{x}M
be a random vector with
\mathbb{E}\left[{\bm{\left\langle}E_{i},v\bm{}\bm{\left\langle}E_{j},v\bm{}}\right]={\mathbbm{1}\left\{i=j\right\}}
.
Let
\gamma:[0,1]\to M
be any smooth path between
x
and
y
. Let
v(t)
be the parallel transport of
v
along
\gamma
. Then for any basis
E^{\prime}_{1}...E^{\prime}_{d}
at
T_{y}M
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\bm{\left\langle}v(t),E_{i}^{\prime}\bm{}\bm{\left\langle}v(t),E_{j}^{\prime}\bm{}}\right]={\mathbbm{1}\left\{i=j\right\}}

In other words, if
v
has identity covariance, then the parallel transport of
v
has identity covariance.

Proof.

Let
E_{i}(t)
be an orthonormal frame along
\gamma
with
E_{i}(0)=E_{i}
.
Under parallel transport,
\frac{d}{dt}\bm{\left\langle}v(t),E_{i}(t)\bm{}=0
. Thus for all
t
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\bm{\left\langle}E_{i}(t),v\bm{}\bm{\left\langle}E_{j}(t),v\bm{}}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[{\bm{\left\langle}E_{i}(0),v\bm{}\bm{\left\langle}E_{j}(0),v\bm{}}\right]={\mathbbm{1}\left\{i=j\right\}}

Finally, consider any basis
E_{i}^{\prime}
. Let
E_{i}^{\prime}=\sum_{k}\alpha^{i}_{k}E_{i}(1)
, i.e.
\alpha^{i}_{k}=\bm{\left\langle}E_{i}^{\prime},E_{k}(1)\bm{}
Then

\displaystyle\bm{\left\langle}v,E_{i}^{\prime}\bm{}\bm{\left\langle}v,E_{j}^{\prime}\bm{}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k,l}\alpha^{i}_{j}\alpha^{j}_{l}\bm{\left\langle}v,E_{k}\bm{}\bm{\left\langle}v,E_{l}\bm{}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k,l}\alpha^{i}_{j}\alpha^{j}_{l}{\mathbbm{1}\left\{k=l\right\}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k}\alpha^{i}_{k}\alpha^{j}_{k}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bm{\left\langle}E_{i}^{\prime},E_{j}^{\prime}\bm{}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathbbm{1}\left\{i=j\right\}}

∎

Lemma 68
.

Let
x,y\in M
, and let
E_{1}...E_{d}
be an orthonormal basis at
T_{x}M
.

Let
\alpha
denote a spherically symmetric random variable in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, i.e. for any orthogonal matrix
G\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}

\displaystyle\alpha\overset{d}{=}G\alpha

Then for any
x\in M
, let
E_{1}...E_{d}
and
E_{1}^{\prime}...E_{d}^{\prime}
be two sets of orthonormal bases of
T_{x}M
. then

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{d}\alpha_{i}E_{i}\overset{d}{=}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\alpha_{i}E_{i}^{\prime}

Consequently, let
v\in T_{x}M:=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\alpha_{i}E_{i}
. Let
\gamma:[0,1]\to M
be any differentiable path between
x
and
y
. Let
v(t)
be the parallel transport of
v
along
\gamma
. Then for any orthogonal basis
E^{\prime}_{1}...E^{\prime}_{d}
at
T_{y}M
,

\displaystyle v(1)\overset{d}{=}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\alpha_{i}E_{i}^{\prime}

Proof.

First, we verify that if
\alpha
is spherically symmetric, and
E_{1}..E_{d}
,
E^{\prime}_{1}...E^{\prime}_{d}
are two sets of orthonormal basis at some point
x
, then

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{d}\alpha_{i}E_{i}\overset{d}{=}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\alpha_{i}E^{\prime}_{i}

To see this, notice that there exists an orthogonal matrix
G
, with
G_{i,j}=\bm{\left\langle}E_{i},E^{\prime}_{j}\bm{}
, such that

\displaystyle E_{i}=\sum_{j=1}^{d}G_{j,i}E^{\prime}_{j}

We further verify that
G_{i,j}
is orthogonal. It suffices to verify that
GG^{T}=I
.

\displaystyle{\mathbbm{1}\left\{j=k\right\}}=\bm{\left\langle}E^{\prime}_{i},E^{\prime}_{j}\bm{}=

\displaystyle\bm{\left\langle}\sum_{k=1}^{d}\bm{\left\langle}E^{\prime}_{i},E_{k}\bm{}E_{j},\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\bm{\left\langle}E^{\prime}_{j},E_{\ell}\bm{}E_{\ell}\bm{}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k,\ell}\bm{\left\langle}E^{\prime}_{i},E_{k}\bm{}\bm{\left\langle}E^{\prime}_{j},E_{\ell}\bm{}\bm{\left\langle}E_{j},E_{\ell}\bm{}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k}\bm{\left\langle}E^{\prime}_{i},E_{k}\bm{}\bm{\left\langle}E^{\prime}_{j},E_{k}\bm{}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bm{\left\langle}G_{i,\cdot},G_{j,\cdot}\bm{}

Note that the inner product on the last line is dot product over
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and the inner product on preceding lines are over
T_{x}M
. The above implies that

\displaystyle GG^{T}=I

i.e.
G
is orthogonal.

Now consider any arbitrary function
f:T_{x}M\to\mathbb{R}
, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{f{(\sum_{i}\alpha_{i}E_{i})}}\right]=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{f{(\sum_{i}\sum_{j}\alpha_{i}G_{i,j}E^{\prime}_{j})}}\right]

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{f{(\sum_{j}\beta_{j}E^{\prime}_{j})}}\right]

where we defined
\beta_{j}:=\sum_{i}\alpha_{i}G_{i,j}
. We finally verify that
\beta\overset{d}{=}\alpha
. This follows from the fact that
\beta=G^{T}\alpha
, where
G
is an orthogonal matrix, and the definition of spherical symmetry for
\alpha
.

Consider an arbitrary line
\gamma(t):[0,1]\to M
, with
x:=\gamma(0)
,
y:=\gamma(1)
. Let
E_{i}
be an orthonormal basis at
T_{x}M
, and
E_{i}(t)
be an orthonormal basis at
T_{\gamma(t)}M
obtained from parallel transport of
E_{i}
. This proves the first claim.

To verify the second claim, let
v\in T_{x}M
be a random vector, given by

\displaystyle v=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\alpha_{i}E_{i}

where
\alpha
is some spherically random vector in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Let
v(t)
be the parallel transport of
v
along
\gamma
. Let
\alpha(t):=\bm{\left\langle}v(t),E_{i}(t)\bm{}
. Then by definition of parallel transport, for all
i
,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\bm{\left\langle}v(t),E_{i}(t)\bm{}=0

so that for all
t\in[0,1]
,

\displaystyle\alpha(t):=\alpha

the second claim then follows from the first claim.

∎

Appendix G
First and higher order trivializations

In this section, we consider a "base point"
x\in M
, and show that for any
u,v\in T_{x}M
such that
{\left\|u\right\|}
and
{\left\|v\right\|}
are sufficiently small,
\mu(t):=\Exp_{\Exp_{x}(u)}(tv)
can be described by
\mu(t)=\Exp_{x}(a(t))
, where
a(t)
is a second order ODE in
T_{x}M
. Subsequently, by bounding various moments of
a(t)
, we obtain different orders of approximations of the geodesic
\mu(t)
. The main result of this section is Lemma
69
, which we state in Section
G.2
.

G.1
Setup

Before stating our main result, we will first need to set up some notation, and introduce a few key quantities. The definitions in this subsection will be used throughout the entirety of Section
G
.

Let
x\in M
, let
E=\left\{E_{1}...E_{d}\right\}
denote an orthonormal basis of
T_{x}M
. Throughout this section,
x
and
E
are arbitrary, but fixed.

For any
u\in T_{x}M
, define

\displaystyle\gamma(t;u):=\Exp_{x}(tu)

For
t\in[0,1]
, we define the parallel orthonormal frame along
\gamma(t;u)
as

\displaystyle E(t;u):=\left\{E_{1}(t;u)...E_{d}(t;u)\right\}:=\left\{\Gamma_{x}^{\gamma(t;u)}E_{1}...\Gamma_{x}^{\gamma(t;u)}E_{d}\right\}

(G.1)

where
\Gamma_{x}^{\gamma(t;u)}
is along the geodesic
\gamma(t;u)
.

We define
the coordinates of the Riemannian curvature tensor at
\gamma(t;u)
, wrt
E_{i}(t;u)
:

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}^{i}_{jkl}(t;u):=\bm{\left\langle}R(E_{j}(t;u),E_{k}(t;u))E_{l}(t;u),E_{i}(t;u)\bm{}

(G.2)

Finally, we define

\displaystyle\mathbf{M}_{i,j}(t;u):=-\sum_{k,l}\mathbf{R}^{i}_{jkl}(t;u)\mathbf{u}_{k}\mathbf{u}_{l}

(G.3)

We verify that for all
t\in[0,1]
,
{\left\|\mathbf{M}(t;u)\right\|}_{2}\leq L_{R}{\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|}_{2}^{2}
.

Given any
u,v\in T_{x}M
, let
J(t;u,v)
denote the unique Jacobi field along
\gamma(t;u)
, satisfying

\displaystyle J(0;u,v)=0\qquad D_{t}J(0;u,v)=v

(G.4)

To simplify notation later on, let us also define

\displaystyle K(t;u,v):=D_{t}J(t;u,v)

Next, we define a few quantities whose meaning will become clear later on. First, for
u,v\in T_{x}M
, let
p(t;u,v)
denote the vector field along
\gamma(t;u)
, given by

\displaystyle p(t;u,v):=

\displaystyle-{(\nabla_{J}R)}{(J,\gamma^{\prime})}\gamma^{\prime}-{(\nabla_{\gamma^{\prime}}R)}(J,\gamma^{\prime})J-2R{(J,\gamma^{\prime})}K

(G.5)

where
\gamma:=\gamma(t;u,v),J:=J(t;u,v),K:=K(t;u,v)
. We also let
\mathbf{p}(t;u,v):[0,1]\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
as the coordinates of
p(t;u,v)
with respect to
E(t;u)
, i.e.
\mathbf{p}_{i}(t;u,v):=\bm{\left\langle}p(t;u,v),E_{i}(t;u)\bm{}
.

Let
\emat(t;\mathbf{M})
denote the solution to the matrix ODE, as defined in Lemma
79
. For
u\in T_{x}M
, define

\displaystyle\mathbf{G}(u)=\begin{bmatrix}I_{d\times d}&0\end{bmatrix}\emat{(1;\begin{bmatrix}0&I\\
\mathbf{M}(\cdot;u)&0\end{bmatrix})}\begin{bmatrix}0\\
I_{d\times d}\end{bmatrix}

(G.6)

where
\mathbf{M}(t;u)
is defined in (
G.3
). We also define, for
u\in T_{x}M
,
\mathbf{B}(t;u)
and
\bar{\mathbf{B}}(t;u)
as

\displaystyle\mathbf{B}(t;u):=\begin{bmatrix}I_{d\times d}&0\end{bmatrix}\emat{(t;\begin{bmatrix}0&I_{d\times d}\\
\mathbf{M}(\cdot;u)&0\end{bmatrix})}\begin{bmatrix}0\\
I_{d\times d}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbf{B}}(t;u):=\begin{bmatrix}I_{d\times d}&0\end{bmatrix}\emat{(1-r;\begin{bmatrix}0&I\\
\mathbf{N}_{r}(\cdot;u)&0\end{bmatrix})}\begin{bmatrix}0\\
I_{d\times d}\end{bmatrix}

(G.7)

where
\mathbf{N}_{s}(t;u):=\mathbf{M}(s+t;u)
. We will now define an important quantity
\mathbf{F}(u,v)
: for any
u\in T_{x}M
such that
\mathbf{B}(1;u)
is invertible, and for any
v\in T_{x}M
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{F}(u,v):=-\mathbf{B}(1;u)^{-1}{(\int_{0}^{1}\bar{\mathbf{B}}(r;u){\mathbf{p}(r;u,v)}dr)}

(G.8)

We define, for
u,v\in T_{x}M
,
\mathbf{a}(t;u,v)
as the solution to the following second-order ODE:

\displaystyle\mathbf{a}(0;u,v)=\mathbf{u}\qquad\mathbf{a}^{\prime}(0;u,v)=\mathbf{v}

\displaystyle\frac{d}{ds}\mathbf{a}(s;u,v)=\mathbf{a}^{\prime}(s;u,v)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{ds}\mathbf{a}^{\prime}(s;u,v)=\mathbf{F}(a(s;u,v),a^{\prime}(s;u,v))

(G.9)

where
\mathbf{F}
is as defined in (
G.8
).

Finally, we will be using the constant
{\mathcal{C}}_{r}
defined in (
C.12
) throughout this section. We reproduce it below for ease of reference:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}_{r}:=\frac{1}{16}\min\left\{{L_{R}^{\prime}}^{-1/3},\frac{1}{8\sqrt{L_{R}}}\right\}

G.2
A Tangent Space Curve for the
\Exp^{-1}
of a Geodesic

We now state the main result of Section
G
:

Lemma 69
.

Let
x\in M
, let
u,v\in T_{x}M
. Assume that
{\left\|u\right\|}\leq{\mathcal{C}}_{r}
and
{\left\|u\right\|}\leq{\mathcal{C}}_{r}
. Let
x^{\prime}:=\Exp_{x}(u)
.

Let
\mathbf{a}(s;u,v)
be as defined in (
G.9
) and
\mathbf{G}(u)
be as defined in (
G.6
). Let
a(s;u,v):=\mathbf{a}(s;u,v)\circ E
and
G(v;u)
denote the tensor given by
G(v;u)={(\mathbf{G}(u)\mathbf{v})}\circ E
.

\displaystyle\Exp_{x}{(a(1;u,v))}=\Exp_{x^{\prime}}{(\Gamma_{x}^{x^{\prime}}G(u;v))}

where
\Gamma_{x}^{x^{\prime}}
denotes parallel transport along
\Exp_{x}(tu)
.

Intuitively, Lemma
69
is related to the following question: if
a(s;u,v):s\to T_{x}M
is a curve in the tangent space of
x
, with initial condition
a(0)=u
,
a^{\prime}(0)=v
, how should
a(s;u,v)
curve (i.e. what is
a^{\prime\prime}(s;u,v)
), such that
\mu(s):=\Exp_{x}(a(s;u,v))
is a geodesic? We will see that
a^{\prime\prime}(s;u,v):=\mathbf{F}(a(s;u,v),a^{\prime}(s;u,v))
is exactly the curvature that makes
\mu(s)
a geodesic. We also verify that
\mu^{\prime}(0)
is given by
\Gamma_{x}^{x^{\prime}}G(u;v)
.

Proof.

To simplify notation, we drop the explicit dependence of
a
on
u,v
, i.e. for the rest of this proof, let
a(s):=a(s;u,v)
.

We first verify an uniform bound
{\left\|a(s)\right\|}\leq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{L_{R}}}
for all
s
. This follows from Lemma
75
, which guarantees that under our assumed bounds on
{\left\|u\right\|}
and
{\left\|v\right\|}
, for all
s
,

\displaystyle{\left\|{a}(s)\right\|}_{2}\leq 2{\left\|{a}^{\prime}(0)\right\|}_{2}\leq 4{\mathcal{C}}_{r}\leq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{L_{R}}}.

(G.10)

We can thus apply Lemma
73
pointwise for
s\in[0,1]
, which guarantees the existence of a solutioin to (
G.9
).

In order to verify that
\mu(s):=\Exp_{x}{(a(s;u,v))}
is a geodesic, it suffices to verify that

D_{s}\partial_{s}\Exp_{x}{(a(s;u,v))}=0
for all
s
. Define
\Lambda(s,t):=\Exp_{x}{(t\cdot a(s))}
, so that
\mu(s)=\Lambda(s,1)
. In Lemma
72
, we show that there exists a function
g
(explicitly defined in (
G.12
)) such that
D_{s}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,1)=g(1;a(s),a^{\prime}(s),a^{\prime\prime}(s))
. In Lemma
73
, we verify that
g(1;u,v,\mathbf{F}(u,v)\circ E)=0
for all
u,v
satisfying the assumed norm bounds (which we verify in (
G.10
)). Combining these two lemmas, we see that the definition of
\mathbf{a}(s)
with
\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}(s)=\mathbf{F}(a^{\prime}(s),a^{\prime\prime}(s))
satisfies, for all
s
,

\displaystyle D_{s}\Lambda(s,1)=0

This shows that
\mu(s)=\Exp_{x}(a(s;u,v))
is a geodesic. By definition,
\mu(0)=\Exp_{x}(a(0;u,v))=\Exp_{x}(u)=x^{\prime}
.

We will now verify the direction of the geodesic, given by
\partial_{s}\Lambda(0,1)
, i.e. the Jacobi field at
s=0,t=1
. By Lemma
70
, this Jacobi field is given by
J(1;a(0),a^{\prime}(0))=\mathbf{J}(1;a(0),a^{\prime}(0))\circ E(1;a(0))
, with
\mathbf{J}
defined in (
G.11
). From point 1. of Lemma
79
and from the definition of
\mathbf{J}
and
\mathbf{K}
in (
G.11
),

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{J}(1;u,v)\\
\mathbf{K}(1;u,v)\end{bmatrix}=\emat{(1;\begin{bmatrix}0&I\\
\mathbf{M}(\cdot;u)&0\end{bmatrix})}\begin{bmatrix}0\\
\mathbf{v}\end{bmatrix}

It follows from algebra that
\mathbf{J}(t;u,v)=\begin{bmatrix}I&0\end{bmatrix}\emat{(1;\begin{bmatrix}0&I\\
\mathbf{M}(\cdot;u)&0\end{bmatrix})}\begin{bmatrix}0\\
I\end{bmatrix}\mathbf{v}=:\mathbf{G}(u)\mathbf{v}
. Therefore,
D_{s}\Lambda(0,1)=\mathbf{G}(a(0))\mathbf{a}^{\prime}(0)\circ E(1;a(0))=\Gamma_{x}^{x^{\prime}}{(\mathbf{G}(a(0))\mathbf{a}^{\prime}(0)\circ E)}

The conclusion follows by observing that
a(0)=u,a^{\prime}(0)=v
by definition in (
G.9
).
∎

G.3
The Jacobi Field along
\gamma(t;u)

Let
v\in T_{x}M
and let
\mathbf{v}
be the coordinates of
v
wrt
E
, i.e.
v=\mathbf{v}\circ E
. Let
\mathbf{J}(t;u,v):[0,1]\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\mathbf{K}(t;u,v):[0,1]\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
be the solution to the following second-order ODE:

\displaystyle\mathbf{J}(0;u,v)=0

\displaystyle\mathbf{K}(0;u,v)=\mathbf{v}

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\mathbf{J}(t;u,v)=\mathbf{K}(t;u,v)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\mathbf{K}(t;u,v)=\mathbf{M}(t;u)\mathbf{J}(t;u,v)

(G.11)

For existence and uniqueness of
\mathbf{J}
and
\mathbf{K}
, see Theorem 4.31 of
Lee 2018
. In Lemma
70
below, we see that
\mathbf{J}
and
\mathbf{K}
as defined in (
G.11
) are the coordinates of a Jacobi field deflined along
\gamma(t;u)
.

Lemma 70
.

For any
u,v\in T_{x}M
, let
\gamma(t;u),E(t;u)
be as defined in Section
G.1
. Let
J(t;u,v)
denote the unique Jacobi field along
\gamma(t;u)
with initial condition
J(0;u,v)=u
and let
D_{t}J(0;u,v)=v
. Let
K(t;u,v):=D_{t}J(t;u,v)
. Let
\mathbf{J}(t;u,v)
and
\mathbf{K}(t;u,v)
be as defined in (
G.11
). Then
\mathbf{J}(t;u,v)
and
\mathbf{K}(t;u,v)
are the coordinates, wrt
E(t;u)
, of
J(t;u,v)
and
K(t;u,v)
respectively. I.e. for
i=1...d
,
J(t;u,v)=\mathbf{J}(t;u,v)\circ E(t;u)
and
K(t;u,v)=\mathbf{K}(t;u,v)\circ E(t;u)

The proof is identical to that of Proposition 10.2 of
Lee 2018
, and we omit it. The main idea is to notice that the ODE in (
G.11
) is exactly the Jacobi Equation (written in coordinates wrt
E_{i}(t;u)
).

The following lemma, which bounds the difference between
\mathbf{G}(u)
and
I
, will come in useful later:

Lemma 71
.

Let
\mathbf{G}(u)
be as defined in (
G.6
). Then

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{G}(u)-I\right\|}_{2}\leq\frac{1}{6}L_{R}{\left\|u\right\|}_{2}^{2}e^{\sqrt{L_{R}}{\left\|u\right\|}_{2}}

Proof.

Let

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{A}&\mathbf{B}\\
\mathbf{C}&\mathbf{D}\end{bmatrix}:=\emat{(1;\begin{bmatrix}0&I\\
\mathbf{M}(\cdot;u)&0\end{bmatrix})}

so that
\mathbf{G}(u)=\mathbf{B}
. From, Lemma
80
and Lemma
14

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{B}-I\right\|}_{2}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}}\sinh(\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}})-1\leq\frac{1}{6}L_{\mathbf{M}}e^{\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}}

where
L_{\mathbf{M}}
is an upper bound on
\mathbf{M}(t;u)
, for all
t
. By definition of
\mathbf{M}
in (
G.3
), we can take
L_{\mathbf{M}}=L_{R}{\left\|u\right\|}^{2}
.
∎

G.4
Second Variation ODE

Let
\mathbf{M}(t;u)
be as defined in (
G.3
). Let
p(t;u,v)
be as defined in (
G.5
), and let
\mathbf{p}(t;u,v)
be its coordinates wrt
E(t;u)
.

For
t\in[0,1]
, let
\mathbf{g}(t;u,v,w)
and
\mathbf{h}(t;u,v,w)
denote the solution to the following ODE:

\displaystyle\mathbf{g}(0;u,v,w)=0

\displaystyle\mathbf{h}(0;u,v,w)=\mathbf{w}

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\mathbf{g}(t;u,v,w)=\mathbf{h}(t)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\mathbf{h}(t;u,v,w)=\mathbf{p}(t;u,v)+\mathbf{M}(t;u)\mathbf{g}(t;u,v,w)

(G.12)

where
\mathbf{w}
denotes the coordinates of
w
wrt
E
, i.e.
\mathbf{w}_{i}=\bm{\left\langle}w,E_{i}\bm{}
and
\mathbf{M}(t;u)
is as defined in (
G.3
).

Lemma 72
.

Let
\mathbf{a}(s):[0,1]\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
be any twice differentiable curve. Let
a(s;u,v):=\mathbf{a}(s;u,v)\circ E
. Let
\Lambda(s,t)=\Exp_{x}{(t\cdot a(s))}
. Let
\mathbf{g}
and
\mathbf{h}
be as defined in (
G.12
), let
g(t;{a}(s),{a}^{\prime}(s),{a}^{\prime\prime}(s)):=\mathbf{g}(t;a(s),a^{\prime}(s),a^{\prime\prime}(s))\circ E(t;a(s))
and
h(t;{a}(s),{a}^{\prime}(s),{a}^{\prime\prime}(s)):=\mathbf{h}(t;a(s),a^{\prime}(s),a^{\prime\prime}(s))\circ E(t;a(s))
.

Then

\displaystyle D_{s}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)=g(t;{a}(s),{a}^{\prime}(s),{a}^{\prime\prime}(s))

\displaystyle D_{t}D_{s}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)=h(t;{a}(s),{a}^{\prime}(s),{a}^{\prime\prime}(s))

Proof of Lemma
72
.

In the rest of this proof, we will consider a fixed but arbitrary
s\in[0,1]
. Let
E_{i}(t;a(s))
denote the orthonormal frame along
\Lambda(s,t)=\gamma(t;a(s))
.

To simplify notation, we drop the explicit dependence on
s
,
t
,
\mathbf{a}(s)
and
\mathbf{a}^{\prime}(s)
when unambiguous, and we use
\partial_{s}
to denote
\partial_{s}\Lambda
and
\partial_{t}
to denote
\partial_{t}\Lambda
when unambiguous.

\displaystyle D_{t}D_{t}D_{s}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle D_{t}D_{s}D_{t}\partial_{s}-D_{t}{(R(\partial_{s},\partial_{t})\partial_{s})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle D_{s}D_{t}D_{t}\partial_{s}-R(\partial_{s},\partial_{t}){(D_{t}\partial_{s})}-D_{t}{(R(\partial_{s},\partial_{t})\partial_{s})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-D_{s}{(R{(\partial_{s},\partial_{t})}\partial_{t})}-R(\partial_{s},\partial_{t}){(D_{t}\partial_{s})}-D_{t}{(R(\partial_{s},\partial_{t})\partial_{s})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-{(D_{s}R)}{(\partial_{s},\partial_{t})}\partial_{t}-R{(D_{s}\partial_{s},\partial_{t})}\partial_{t}-R{(\partial_{s},D_{t}\partial_{s})}\partial_{t}-R{(\partial_{s},\partial_{t})}D_{t}\partial_{s}

\displaystyle\quad-R{(\partial_{s},\partial_{t})}D_{t}\partial_{s}

\displaystyle\quad-{(D_{t}R)}(\partial_{s},\partial_{t})\partial_{s}-R(D_{t}\partial_{s},\partial_{t})\partial_{s}-R(\partial_{s},\partial_{t})D_{t}\partial_{s}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-R(D_{s}\partial_{s},\partial_{t})\partial_{t}

\displaystyle-{(D_{s}R)}{(\partial_{s},\partial_{t})}\partial_{t}-{(D_{t}R)}(\partial_{s},\partial_{t})\partial_{s}

\displaystyle-R{(\partial_{s},D_{t}\partial_{s})}\partial_{t}-3R{(\partial_{s},\partial_{t})}D_{t}\partial_{s}-R(D_{t}\partial_{s},\partial_{t})\partial_{s}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-R(D_{s}\partial_{s},\partial_{t})\partial_{t}

\displaystyle-{(D_{s}R)}{(\partial_{s},\partial_{t})}\partial_{t}-{(D_{t}R)}(\partial_{s},\partial_{t})\partial_{s}-2R{(\partial_{s},\partial_{t})}D_{t}\partial_{s}

(G.13)

where we used multiple times the equation
D_{s}D_{t}V-D_{t}D_{s}V=R{(\partial_{s}\Lambda,\partial_{t}\Lambda)}V
(see proposition 7.5 of
Lee 2018
), and the fact that
D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda=D_{s}\partial_{t}\Lambda
. The last line uses the identity
R(a,b)c+R(b,c)a+R(c,a)b=0
for all
a,b,c
.

Recall that
\partial_{t}=\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,t)=\gamma^{\prime}(t;a(s))
and
\partial_{s}=\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)=J(t;a(s),a^{\prime}(s))
, so that the last line,
-{(D_{s}R)}{(\partial_{s},\partial_{t})}\partial_{t}-{(D_{t}R)}(\partial_{s},\partial_{t})\partial_{s}-4R{(\partial_{s},\partial_{t})}D_{t}\partial_{s}
is exactly equal to
p(t;a(s),a^{\prime}(s))
as defined in (
G.5
).

If we let
g(t):=D_{s}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)
and
h(t):=D_{t}g(t)=D_{t}D_{s}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)
, then (
G.13
) can be written as a second-order ODE

\displaystyle g(0)=0

\displaystyle h(0)=a^{\prime\prime}(0)

\displaystyle D_{t}g(t)=h(t)

\displaystyle D_{t}h(t)=-R(g(t),\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,t))\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,t)+p(t;a(s),a^{\prime}(s))

The initial condition
g(0)=0
is because
\Lambda(s,0)=x
for all
s
, and thus
\left.D_{s}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)\right|_{t=0}=0
.The initial condition
h(0)=a^{\prime\prime}(s)
is because,from the definition of
\Lambda
,
\left.\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,t)\right|_{t=0}=a(s)
and
\left.D_{s}D_{s}\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,t)\right|_{t=0}=a^{\prime\prime}(s)
, thus
\left.D_{t}D_{s}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)\right|_{t=0}=a^{\prime\prime}(s)+\left.R(\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t),\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,t))\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)\right|_{t=0}=a^{\prime\prime}(s)
since
\left.\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)\right|_{t=0}=0
.

Letting
\mathbf{g}(t)
and
\mathbf{h}(t)
denote the coordinates of
g(t)
and
h(t)
wrt
E(t;a(s))
, we verify via the definition of
\mathbf{M}
in (
G.3
) and
\mathbf{p}
in (
G.5
), that

\displaystyle\mathbf{g}(0)=0

\displaystyle\mathbf{h}(0)=\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}(0)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\mathbf{g}(t)=\mathbf{h}(t)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\mathbf{h}(t)=\mathbf{p}(t;a(s),a^{\prime}(s))+\mathbf{M}(t;a(s))\mathbf{g}(t)

where we use the fact that
\mathbf{g}_{i}(t)=\bm{\left\langle}g(t),E_{i}(t;a(s))\bm{}
, and
\partial_{t}\mathbf{g}_{i}(t)=\bm{\left\langle}D_{t}g(t),E_{i}(t;a(s))\bm{}
as
D_{t}E_{i}(t;a(s))=0
. We see that the above is exactly (
G.12
) with
u=a(s),v=a^{\prime}(s),w=a^{\prime\prime}(s)
.
∎

Lemma 73
.

Let
\mathbf{F}
be as defined in (
G.8
). Let
\mathbf{g}
be as defined in (
G.12
). For any
u\in T_{x}M
satisfying
L_{R}{\left\|u\right\|}_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{4}
and for any
v\in T_{x}M
,
\mathbf{F}(u,v)
is well defined and

\displaystyle\mathbf{g}(1;u,v,\mathbf{F}(u,v)\circ E)=0

Proof of Lemma
73
.

We apply the second claim from Lemma
79
with
\mathbf{z}(t)=\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{g}(t;u,v,w)\\
\mathbf{h}(t;u,v,w)\end{bmatrix}
and
\mathbf{M}(t):=\begin{bmatrix}0&I\\
\mathbf{M}(t;u)&0\end{bmatrix}
and
\mathbf{v}(t)=\begin{bmatrix}0\\
\mathbf{p}(t;u,v)\end{bmatrix}
, which gives

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{g}(t;u,v,w)\\
\mathbf{h}(t;u,v,w)\end{bmatrix}=\int_{0}^{t}\emat{(t-r;\begin{bmatrix}0&I\\
\mathbf{N}_{r}(\cdot;u)&0\end{bmatrix})}\begin{bmatrix}0\\
\mathbf{p}(r;u,v)\end{bmatrix}dr+\emat{(t;\begin{bmatrix}0&I\\
\mathbf{M}(\cdot;u)&0\end{bmatrix})}\begin{bmatrix}0\\
\mathbf{w}\end{bmatrix}

where
\mathbf{w}
is the coordinates of
w
wrt
E
, and for any
r
,
\mathbf{N}_{r}(t;u):=\mathbf{M}(r+t;u)
.

Define the four blocks

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{A}&\mathbf{B}\\
\mathbf{C}&\mathbf{D}\end{bmatrix}:=\emat{(1;\begin{bmatrix}0&I\\
\mathbf{M}(\cdot;u)&0\end{bmatrix})}

and the four blocks

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}\bar{\mathbf{A}}(r)&\bar{\mathbf{B}}(r)\\
\bar{\mathbf{C}}(r)&\bar{\mathbf{D}}(r)\end{bmatrix}:=\emat{(1-r;\begin{bmatrix}0&I\\
\mathbf{N}_{r}(\cdot;u)&0\end{bmatrix})}

Then by algebra,

\displaystyle\mathbf{g}(1;u,v,w)=\int_{0}^{1}\bar{\mathbf{B}}(r){\mathbf{p}(r;u,v)}dr+\mathbf{B}\mathbf{w}

Recall the definition of
\mathbf{M}(t;u)
from (
G.3
). Under our assumption that
L_{R}{\left\|u\right\|}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{4}
, we verify that for all
t
,
{\left\|\mathbf{M}(t;u)\right\|}_{2}\leq L_{R}{\left\|u\right\|}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{4}
and for all
r,t
,
{\left\|\mathbf{N}_{r}(t;u)\right\|}_{2}\leq L_{R}{\left\|u\right\|}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{4}
. From Lemma
80
and Lemma
14
,
{\left\|\mathbf{B}-I\right\|}_{2}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}}\sinh{(\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}})}-1\leq\frac{1}{6}L_{\mathbf{M}}e^{\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}}\leq\frac{1}{3}L_{\mathbf{M}}\leq\frac{1}{12}
, thus
\mathbf{B}
is invertible, so that
\mathbf{F}
as defined in (
G.8
) is well defined.

Let
\mathbf{F}(u,v)
be as defined in (
G.8
), and notice that
\mathbf{B}
is the same as
\mathbf{B}(1;u)
from (
G.7
), and
\bar{\mathbf{B}}(t)
is the same as
\bar{\mathbf{B}}(t;u)
from (
G.7
), so that

\displaystyle\mathbf{F}(u,v):=-\mathbf{B}^{-1}{(\int_{0}^{1}\bar{\mathbf{B}}(r){\mathbf{p}(r;u,v)}dr)}

Plugging into the expression for
\mathbf{g}(1;u,v,w)
above, we verify that

\displaystyle\mathbf{g}(1;u,v,\mathbf{F}(u,v)\circ E)=0.

This proves the lemma.
∎

Lemma 74
.

Let
\mathbf{F}
be as defined in (
G.8
). For any
u\in T_{x}M
satisfying
L_{R}{\left\|u\right\|}_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{4}
and for any
v\in T_{x}M
,

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{F}(u,v)\right\|}_{2}\leq 16{(L_{R}^{\prime}{\left\|u\right\|}^{2}+L_{R}{\left\|u\right\|})}{\left\|v\right\|}^{2}

Proof.

The rest of the proof will be devoted to bounding the norm of
{\left\|\mathbf{F}(u,v)\right\|}_{2}
. Using the fact that
{\left\|\mathbf{N}_{r}(t;u)\right\|}_{2}\leq L_{R}{\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|}_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{4}
and applying Lemma
80
, we guarantee that for all
r
,
{\left\|\bar{\mathbf{B}}(r)-I\right\|}_{2}\leq\frac{1}{6}L_{\mathbf{M}}e^{\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}}
. We can thus bound
{\left\|\mathbf{B}^{-1}\right\|}_{2}\leq\frac{1}{1-\frac{1}{6}L_{\mathbf{M}}e^{\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}}}
and
{\left\|\bar{\mathbf{B}}\right\|}_{2}\leq 1+\frac{1}{6}L_{\mathbf{M}}e^{\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}}
. Thus

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{F}(u,v)\right\|}_{2}=

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{B}^{-1}{(\int_{0}^{1}\bar{\mathbf{B}}(r){\mathbf{p}(r;u,v)}dr)}\right\|}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1+\frac{1}{6}L_{\mathbf{M}}e^{\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}}}{1-\frac{1}{6}L_{\mathbf{M}}e^{\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}}}\int_{0}^{1}{\left\|\mathbf{p}(t;u,v)\right\|}_{2}dt

Again using
L_{\mathbf{M}}=L_{R}{\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|}_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{4}
, we can bound
\frac{1+\frac{1}{6}L_{\mathbf{M}}e^{\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}}}{1-\frac{1}{6}L_{\mathbf{M}}e^{\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}}}\leq 1+L_{\mathbf{M}}\leq 2
. It remains to bound
{\left\|\mathbf{p}(t;u,v)\right\|}
for all
t
. From the definition of
\mathbf{p}
in (
G.5
),

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{p}(t;u,v)\right\|}_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\left\|p(t;u,v)\right\|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\left\|{(\nabla_{J}R)}{(J,\gamma^{\prime})}\gamma^{\prime}\right\|}+{\left\|{(\nabla_{\gamma^{\prime}}R)}(J,\gamma^{\prime})J\right\|}+{\left\|2R{(J,\gamma^{\prime})}K\right\|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2L_{R}^{\prime}{\left\|J\right\|}^{2}{\left\|\gamma^{\prime}\right\|}^{2}+2L_{R}{\left\|J\right\|}{\left\|\gamma^{\prime}\right\|}{\left\|K\right\|}

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle 2L_{R}^{\prime}{\left\|J(t;u,v)\right\|}^{2}{\left\|\gamma^{\prime}(t;u)\right\|}^{2}+2L_{R}{\left\|J(t;u,v)\right\|}{\left\|\gamma^{\prime}(t;u)\right\|}{\left\|K(t;u,v)\right\|}

By definition,
\gamma^{\prime}(t;u)=u
. By Lemma
61
, we can bound for all
t\in[0,1]
,

\displaystyle{\left\|J(t;u,v)\right\|}\leq

\displaystyle\cosh{({{\mathcal{C}}})}{\left\|J(0;u,v)\right\|}+\frac{\sinh{({{\mathcal{C}}})}}{{{\mathcal{C}}}}{\left\|{K(0;u,v)}\right\|}=\frac{\sinh{({{\mathcal{C}}})}}{{{\mathcal{C}}}}{\left\|v\right\|}

\displaystyle{\left\|K(t;u,v)\right\|}\leq

\displaystyle{{\mathcal{C}}}\sinh{({{\mathcal{C}}})}{\left\|J(0;u,v)\right\|}+\cosh{({{\mathcal{C}}})}{\left\|K(0;u,v)\right\|}=\cosh{({{\mathcal{C}}})}{\left\|v\right\|}

where
{\mathcal{C}}=\sqrt{L_{R}}{\left\|u\right\|}
by our assumption in the lemma statement. We use the fact that
J(0;u,v)=0
and
K(0;u,v)=v
by definition in (
G.11
). By Lemma
14
and our assumption on
u
, we can bound
\cosh({\mathcal{C}})\leq 1+L_{R}{\left\|u\right\|}^{2}e^{\sqrt{L_{R}}{\left\|u\right\|}}\leq 2
and
\sinh({\mathcal{C}})/{\mathcal{C}}\leq e^{\sqrt{L_{R}}{\left\|u\right\|}}\leq 2
, so that

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{p}(t;u,v)\right\|}_{2}\leq 8{(L_{R}^{\prime}{\left\|u\right\|}^{2}+L_{R}{\left\|u\right\|})}{\left\|v\right\|}^{2}

Plugging into the earlier bound on
{\left\|\mathbf{F}(u,v)\right\|}_{2}
, we get

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{F}(u,v)\right\|}_{2}\leq 16{(L_{R}^{\prime}{\left\|u\right\|}^{2}+L_{R}{\left\|u\right\|})}{\left\|v\right\|}^{2}

∎

Lemma 75
.

Let
\mathbf{a}(s;u,v)
be as defined in (
G.9
). Assume that
{\left\|u\right\|}\leq{\mathcal{C}}_{r}
and
{\left\|v\right\|}\leq{\mathcal{C}}_{r}
, where
{\mathcal{C}}_{r}
is the constant defined in (
C.12
). Then for all
s\in[0,1]
,

\displaystyle{\left\|{\mathbf{a}}(s;u,v)\right\|}_{2}\leq{\left\|\mathbf{a}(0;u,v)\right\|}+2{\left\|{\mathbf{a}}^{\prime}(0;u,v)\right\|}_{2}\leq 4{\mathcal{C}}_{r}

\displaystyle{\left\|{\mathbf{a}}^{\prime}(s;u,v)\right\|}_{2}\leq 2s{\left\|{\mathbf{a}}^{\prime}(0;u,v)\right\|}_{2}\leq 2{\mathcal{C}}_{r}

Proof.

To reduce notation we drop the explicit dependence on
u
and
v
, i.e.
a(s):=a(s;u,v)
.

Let us define a "capped" version of (
G.9
):

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbf{a}}(0)=\mathbf{a}(0)=\mathbf{u}\qquad\bar{\mathbf{a}}^{\prime}(0)=\mathbf{a}^{\prime}(0)=\mathbf{v}

\displaystyle\frac{d}{ds}\bar{\mathbf{a}}(s)=\bar{\mathbf{a}}^{\prime}(s)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{ds}\bar{\mathbf{a}}^{\prime}(s)=\mathbf{F}(\Pi_{4{\mathcal{C}}_{r}}{(\bar{\mathbf{a}}(s))},\Pi_{4{\mathcal{C}}_{r}}{(\bar{\mathbf{a}}^{\prime}(s))},0)

where
\Pi_{4{\mathcal{C}}_{r}}(\mathbf{c}):=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathbf{c}&\text{for }{\left\|\mathbf{c}\right\|}_{2}\leq{4{\mathcal{C}}_{r}}\\
\frac{{4{\mathcal{C}}_{r}}}{{\left\|\mathbf{c}\right\|}_{2}}\cdot\mathbf{c}&\text{for }{\left\|\mathbf{c}\right\|}_{2}\geq{4{\mathcal{C}}_{r}}\end{array}\right.
. Thus for any
\mathbf{c}
,
L_{R}{\left\|\Pi_{4{\mathcal{C}}_{r}}(\mathbf{c})\right\|}_{2}^{2}\leq L_{R}{(4{\mathcal{C}}_{r})}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{4}
, so that
L_{R}{\left\|\Pi_{4{\mathcal{C}}_{r}}{(\bar{\mathbf{a}}(s))}\right\|}_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{4}
for all
s
. We can then apply Lemma
74
, which guarantees that for all
s
,

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{F}(\Pi_{4{\mathcal{C}}_{r}}{(\bar{\mathbf{a}}(s))},\Pi_{4{\mathcal{C}}_{r}}{(\bar{\mathbf{a}}^{\prime}(s))})\right\|}_{2}\leq

\displaystyle 16{(L_{R}^{\prime}{\left\|\Pi_{4{\mathcal{C}}_{r}}{(\bar{\mathbf{a}}(s))}\right\|}_{2}^{2}+L_{R}{\left\|\Pi_{4{\mathcal{C}}_{r}}{(\bar{\mathbf{a}}^{\prime}(s))}\right\|}_{2})}{\left\|\Pi_{4{\mathcal{C}}_{r}}{(\bar{\mathbf{a}}^{\prime}(s))}\right\|}_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 16{(L_{R}^{\prime}{\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{3}+L_{R}{\mathcal{C}}_{r}^{2})}{\left\|\Pi_{4{\mathcal{C}}_{r}}{(\bar{\mathbf{a}}^{\prime}(s))}\right\|}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\left\|\Pi_{4{\mathcal{C}}_{r}}{(\bar{\mathbf{a}}^{\prime}(s))}\right\|}_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{4}{\left\|\bar{\mathbf{a}}^{\prime}(s)\right\|}_{2}

where the second inequality follows by definition of
\Pi
, the third inequality follows from (
C.12
).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{ds}{\left\|\bar{\mathbf{a}}^{\prime}(s)\right\|}_{2}\leq\frac{1}{4}{\left\|\bar{\mathbf{a}}^{\prime}(s)\right\|}_{2}

By Gronwall’s Lemma,
{\left\|\bar{\mathbf{a}}^{\prime}(s)\right\|}_{2}\leq e^{1/4}{\left\|\bar{\mathbf{a}}^{\prime}(0)\right\|}_{2}
for all
s\in[0,1]
.

It then follows that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{ds}{\left\|\bar{\mathbf{a}}(s)\right\|}_{2}\leq{\left\|\bar{\mathbf{a}}^{\prime}(s)\right\|}_{2}\leq e^{1/4}{\left\|\bar{\mathbf{a}}^{\prime}(0)\right\|}_{2}

Thus
{\left\|\bar{\mathbf{a}}^{\prime}(s)\right\|}_{2}\leq{\left\|\bar{\mathbf{a}}(0)\right\|}_{2}+2s{\left\|\bar{\mathbf{a}}^{\prime}(0)\right\|}_{2}\leq 4{\mathcal{C}}_{r}
for all
s
.

This implies that for all
s\in[0,1]
,
\Pi_{\alpha}(\bar{\mathbf{a}}(s))=\bar{\mathbf{a}}(s)
and
\Pi_{\alpha}(\bar{\mathbf{a}}^{\prime}(s))=\bar{\mathbf{a}}^{\prime}(s)
and

\mathbf{F}(\Pi_{4{\mathcal{C}}_{r}}{(\bar{\mathbf{a}}(s))},\Pi_{4{\mathcal{C}}_{r}}{(\bar{\mathbf{a}}^{\prime}(s))},0)=\mathbf{F}({\bar{\mathbf{a}}(s)},{\bar{\mathbf{a}}^{\prime}(s)},0)
. Thus
\begin{bmatrix}\bar{\mathbf{a}}(s)\\
\bar{\mathbf{a}}^{\prime}(s)\end{bmatrix}
has identical initialization and dynamics compared to
\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{a}(s)\\
\mathbf{a}^{\prime}(s)\end{bmatrix}
. This in turn implies that
\begin{bmatrix}\bar{\mathbf{a}}(s)\\
\bar{\mathbf{a}}^{\prime}(s)\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{a}(s)\\
\mathbf{a}^{\prime}(s)\end{bmatrix}
for all
s\in[0,1]
.
∎

Appendix H
Approximation Bounds

The following Lemma is taken from Lemma 3 of
Sun et al. 2019
, and is used at multiple places in our proof:

Lemma 76
.

Consider any
x\in M
,
u,v\in T_{x}M
. Define
x^{\prime}:=\Exp_{x}(u)
. Let
\Gamma_{x}^{x^{\prime}}
denote parallel transport along the geodesic
\Exp_{x}(u)
(in case the geodesic is not unique). Then

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(\Exp_{x}(u+v),\Exp_{x^{\prime}}(\Gamma_{x}^{x^{\prime}}v))}\leq

\displaystyle L_{R}{\left\|u\right\|}{\left\|v\right\|}{({\left\|u\right\|}+{\left\|v\right\|})}e^{\sqrt{L_{R}}{({\left\|u\right\|}+{\left\|v\right\|})}}

H.1
First order approximation of
\mathbf{a}(s;u,v)

We first show in Lemma
77
that
\mathbf{a}(1;u,v)
can be well approximated by
\mathbf{a}(0;u,v)+\mathbf{a}^{\prime}(0;u,v)
. Using this bound on
{\left\|\mathbf{a}(1;u,v)-\mathbf{a}(0;u,v)-\mathbf{a}^{\prime}(0;u,v)\right\|}
, we can in turn bound

\mathrm{d}{(\Exp_{x}(u+v),\Exp_{x}(a(1;u,v))}

Lemma 77
.

Let
x\in M
, let
u,v\in T_{x}M
, assume that
{\left\|u\right\|}\leq{\mathcal{C}}_{r}
and
{\left\|u\right\|}\leq{\mathcal{C}}_{r}
(
{\mathcal{C}}_{r}
is defined in (
C.12
)). Let
\mathbf{a}
be as defined in (
G.9
), then

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{a}(1;u,v)-\mathbf{u}-\mathbf{v}\right\|}_{2}\leq 2^{10}{(L_{R}^{\prime}{({\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|}_{2}+{\left\|\mathbf{v}\right\|}_{2})}^{2}+L_{R}{({\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|}_{2}+{\left\|\mathbf{v}\right\|}_{2})})}{\left\|\mathbf{v}\right\|}_{2}^{2}

Proof.

To reduce notation we drop the explicit dependence on
u
and
v
, i.e.
a(s):=a(s;u,v)
.

From Lemma
75
, given our bassumed bounds on
{\left\|u\right\|}
and
{\left\|v\right\|}
, we can uniformly bound
L_{R}{\left\|\mathbf{a}(s)\right\|}_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{4}
for all
s
. We can thus apply Lemma
74
to bound, for all
s
,

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}(s)\right\|}_{2}=

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{F}(\mathbf{a}(s),\mathbf{a}^{\prime}(s))\right\|}_{2}\leq 16{(L_{R}^{\prime}{\left\|\mathbf{a}(s)\right\|}_{2}^{2}+L_{R}{\left\|\mathbf{a}(s)\right\|}_{2})}{\left\|\mathbf{a}^{\prime}(s)\right\|}_{2}^{2}

Applying second order Taylor expansion,

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{a}(1)-\mathbf{u}-\mathbf{v}\right\|}_{2}=

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{a}(1)-\mathbf{a}(0)-\mathbf{a}^{\prime}(0)\right\|}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}{\left\|\mathbf{a}^{\prime}(s)-\mathbf{a}^{\prime}(0)\right\|}_{2}ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}{\left\|\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}(s)\right\|}_{2}ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}16{(L_{R}^{\prime}{\left\|\mathbf{a}(s)\right\|}_{2}^{2}+L_{R}{\left\|\mathbf{a}(s)\right\|}_{2})}{\left\|\mathbf{a}^{\prime}(s)\right\|}_{2}^{2}ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2^{10}{(L_{R}^{\prime}{({\left\|\mathbf{a}(0)\right\|}_{2}+{\left\|\mathbf{a}^{\prime}(0)\right\|}_{2})}^{2}+L_{R}{({\left\|\mathbf{a}(0)\right\|}_{2}+{\left\|\mathbf{a}^{\prime}(0)\right\|}_{2})})}{\left\|\mathbf{a}^{\prime}(0)\right\|}_{2}^{2}

where the fourth inequality is by Lemma
75
.

Finally, it follows by definition in (
G.9
) that
{\left\|\mathbf{a}(0)\right\|}_{2}={\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|}_{2}
and
{\left\|\mathbf{a}^{\prime}(0)\right\|}_{2}={\left\|\mathbf{v}\right\|}_{2}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{a}(1)-\mathbf{u}-\mathbf{v}\right\|}_{2}\leq 2^{10}{(L_{R}^{\prime}{({\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|}_{2}+{\left\|\mathbf{v}\right\|}_{2})}^{2}+L_{R}{({\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|}_{2}+{\left\|\mathbf{v}\right\|}_{2})})}{\left\|\mathbf{v}\right\|}_{2}^{2}

∎

Lemma 78
.

Let
x\in M
, let
u,v\in T_{x}M
. Assume that
{\left\|u\right\|}\leq{\mathcal{C}}_{r}
and
{\left\|u\right\|}\leq{\mathcal{C}}_{r}
. Let
x^{\prime}:=\Exp_{x}(u)
. Let
\mathbf{G}(u)
be as defined in (
G.6
), and let
G(v;u)={(\mathbf{G}(u)\mathbf{v})}\circ E
. Let Let
\Gamma_{x}^{x^{\prime}}
denote parallel transport along the geodesic
\Exp_{x}(u)
. Then

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(\Exp_{x}(u+v),\Exp_{x^{\prime}}(\Gamma_{x}^{x^{\prime}}G(v)))}\leq

\displaystyle 2^{12}{(L_{R}^{\prime}{({\left\|u\right\|}+{\left\|v\right\|})}^{2}+L_{R}{({\left\|u\right\|}+{\left\|v\right\|})})}{\left\|v\right\|}^{2}

and

\displaystyle{\left\|G-Id\right\|}\leq\frac{1}{3}L_{R}{\left\|u\right\|}^{2}

Proof.

Let
\mathbf{a}(s;u,v)
be as defined in (
G.9
). To simplify notation, for the rest of the proof we drop the explicit dependence on
u,v
and let
a(s):=a(s;u,v)
.

Let
\mathbf{G}(u)
be as defined in (
G.6
). Let
G(v;u):={(\mathbf{G}(u)\mathbf{v})}\circ E
. By Lemma
69
,

\displaystyle\Exp_{x}(a(1;u,v))=\Exp_{x^{\prime}}{(\Gamma_{x}^{x^{\prime}}G(v;u))}.

By Lemma
77
,
{\left\|\mathbf{a}(1;u,v)-\mathbf{u}-\mathbf{v}\right\|}\leq 2^{11}{(L_{R}^{\prime}{({\left\|u\right\|}+{\left\|v\right\|})}^{2}+L_{R}{({\left\|u\right\|}+{\left\|v\right\|})})}{\left\|v\right\|}^{2}
. Together with Lemma
62
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(\Exp_{x}(u+v),\Exp_{x}(a(1;u,v)))}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\sinh{(\sqrt{L_{R}}{({\left\|a(1;u,v)\right\|}+{\left\|u+v\right\|})})}}{\sqrt{L_{R}}{({\left\|a(1;u,v)\right\|}+{\left\|u+v\right\|})}}{\left\|a(1;u,v)-u-v\right\|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle e^{\sqrt{L_{R}}{({\left\|a(1;u,v)\right\|}+{\left\|u+v\right\|})}}{\left\|a(1;u,v)-u-v\right\|}

where the second inequality uses Lemma
14
. By our assumption that
{\left\|u\right\|}\leq{\mathcal{C}}_{r}
,
{\left\|v\right\|}\leq{\mathcal{C}}_{r}
, we can use Lemma
75
to bound
{\left\|a(1;u,v)\right\|}\leq 4{\mathcal{C}}_{r}
, so that altogether,
e^{\sqrt{L_{R}}{({\left\|a(1;u,v)\right\|}+{\left\|u+v\right\|})}}\leq 2
. Combining all ove the above,

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(\Exp_{x}(u+v),\Exp_{x^{\prime}}{(\Gamma_{x}^{x^{\prime}}G(v;u))})}=

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(\Exp_{x}(u+v),\Exp_{x}(a(1;u,v)))}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2^{12}{(L_{R}^{\prime}{({\left\|u\right\|}+{\left\|v\right\|})}^{2}+L_{R}{({\left\|u\right\|}+{\left\|v\right\|})})}{\left\|v\right\|}^{2}

Finally, by Lemma
71
,
{\left\|\mathbf{G}(u)-I\right\|}_{2}\leq\frac{1}{6}L_{R}{\left\|u\right\|}^{2}e^{\sqrt{L_{R}}{\left\|u\right\|}_{2}}\leq\frac{1}{3}L_{R}{\left\|u\right\|}^{2}
. This concludes the proof.

∎

Appendix I
Matrix ODE

In this section we provide Gronwall-style inequality for matrix ODE. This section does not make explicit use of Riemannian geometry but it provides a natural way to study ODEs on manifold.

Lemma 79

(Formal Matrix Exponent)
.

Given
\mathbf{M}(t):\mathbb{R}^{+}\to\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
, define
\emat{(t;\mathbf{M})}:\mathbb{R}^{+}\to\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
as the solution to the matrix ODE

\displaystyle\emat{(0;\mathbf{M})}=

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\emat{(t;\mathbf{M})}=

\displaystyle\mathbf{M}(t)\emat{(t;\mathbf{M})}

Then

1.

Let
\mathbf{x}(t)
be the solution to the ODE
\frac{d}{dt}\mathbf{x}(t)=\mathbf{M}(t)\mathbf{x}(t)
, for some
\mathbf{M}
, then

\displaystyle\mathbf{x}(t)=\emat{(t;\mathbf{M})}\mathbf{x}(0)

2.

Let
\mathbf{z}(t)
be the solution to
\frac{d}{dt}\mathbf{z}(t)=\mathbf{M}(t)\mathbf{z}(t)+\mathbf{v}(t)
, for some
\mathbf{M}
,
\mathbf{v}
, then

\displaystyle\mathbf{z}(T)=\int_{0}^{T}\emat{(T-s;\mathbf{N}_{s})}\mathbf{v}(s)ds+\emat{(T;\mathbf{M})}\mathbf{z}(0)

where for any
s,t
,
\mathbf{N}_{s}(t):=\mathbf{M}(s+t)
.

Proof of Lemma
79
.

Let
\mathbf{y}_{t}:=\emat{(t;\mathbf{M})}\mathbf{x}(0)
. We verify that

\displaystyle\mathbf{y}(0)=

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\mathbf{y}(t)=

\displaystyle{(\frac{d}{dt}\emat{(t;\mathbf{M})})}\mathbf{x}(0)=\mathbf{M}(t)\mathbf{y}(t)

Given the same dynamics and initial conditions, we conclude that
\mathbf{x}(t)=\mathbf{y}(t)
for all
t
.

To verify the second claim, note that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\int_{0}^{t}\emat{(t-s;\mathbf{N}_{s})}\mathbf{v}(s)ds

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\emat{(0;\mathbf{N}_{t})}\mathbf{v}(s)+\int_{0}^{t}{(\frac{d}{dt}\emat{(t-s;\mathbf{N}_{s})})}\mathbf{v}(s)ds

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{v}(s)+\int_{0}^{t}\mathbf{N}_{s}(t-s)\emat{(t-s;\mathbf{N}_{s})}\mathbf{v}(s)dt

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{v}(s)+\mathbf{M}(t)\int_{0}^{t}\emat{(t-s;\mathbf{N}_{s})}\mathbf{v}(s)ds

Additionally,
\frac{d}{dt}\emat{(t;\mathbf{M})}\mathbf{z}(0)=\mathbf{M}(t)\emat{(t;\mathbf{M})}\mathbf{z}(0)
, summing,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\mathbf{z}(t)=

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\int_{0}^{t}\emat{(t-s;\mathbf{N}_{s})}\mathbf{v}(s)ds+\emat{(t;\mathbf{M})}\mathbf{z}(0)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{v}(s)+\mathbf{M}(t)\int_{0}^{t}\emat{(t-s;\mathbf{N}_{s})}\mathbf{v}(s)ds+\mathbf{M}(t)\emat{(t;\mathbf{M})}\mathbf{z}(0)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{v}(s)+\mathbf{M}(t)\mathbf{z}(t)

∎

Lemma 80
.

Let
\emat
be as defined in Lemma
79
. Let

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{A}(t)&\mathbf{B}(t)\\
\mathbf{C}(t)&\mathbf{D}(t)\end{bmatrix}:=\emat{(t;\begin{bmatrix}0&I\\
\mathbf{M}(t)&0\end{bmatrix})}

for some
\mathbf{M}(t)
. Assume
{\left\|\mathbf{M}(t)\right\|}_{2}\leq L_{\mathbf{M}}
for all
t
. Then for all
t
,

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{A}(t)\right\|}_{2}\leq\cosh{(\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}t)}

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{B}(t)\right\|}_{2}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}}\sinh{(\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}t)}

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{C}(t)\right\|}_{2}\leq\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}\sinh{(\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}t)}

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{D}(t)\right\|}_{2}\leq\cosh{(\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}t)}

and

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{A}(t)-I\right\|}_{2}\leq\cosh(\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}t)-1

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{B}(t)-tI\right\|}_{2}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}}\sinh{(\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}t)}-t

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{D}(t)-I\right\|}_{2}\leq\cosh{(\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}t)}-1

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{A}(t)-I\right\|}_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}L_{\mathbf{M}}e^{L_{\mathbf{M}}}

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{B}(t)-tI\right\|}_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{6}L_{\mathbf{M}}e^{L{\mathbf{M}}}

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{D}(t)-I\right\|}_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}L_{\mathbf{M}}e^{L_{\mathbf{M}}}

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{C}(t)\right\|}_{2}\leq

\displaystyle L_{\mathbf{M}}e^{L_{\mathbf{M}}}

Proof of Lemma
80
.

We first verify the first part of the lemma. Consider the ODE given by

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{x}\\
\mathbf{y}\end{bmatrix}(t)=\begin{bmatrix}0&I\\
\mathbf{M}(t)&0\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{x}(t)\\
\mathbf{y}(t)\end{bmatrix}

By Lemma
79
,
\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{A}(t)&\mathbf{B}(t)\\
\mathbf{C}(t)&\mathbf{D}(t)\end{bmatrix}
satisfies

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{x}(t)\\
\mathbf{y}(t)\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{A}(t)&\mathbf{B}(t)\\
\mathbf{C}(t)&\mathbf{D}(t)\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{x}(0)\\
\mathbf{y}(0)\end{bmatrix}

By Cauchy Schwarz,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}{\left\|\mathbf{x}(t)\right\|}_{2}\leq{\left\|\mathbf{y}(t)\right\|}_{2}

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}{\left\|\mathbf{y}(t)\right\|}_{2}\leq L_{\mathbf{M}}{\left\|\mathbf{x}(t)\right\|}_{2}

We apply Lemma
65
, with
a_{t}:={\left\|\mathbf{x}(t)\right\|}_{2}
and
b_{t}:={\left\|\mathbf{y}(t)\right\|}_{2}
,
C:=L_{\mathbf{M}}
. Then

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{x}_{t}\right\|}_{2}\leq{{\left\|\mathbf{x}_{0}\right\|}_{2}}\cosh{(\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}t)}+\frac{{\left\|\mathbf{y}_{0}\right\|}_{2}}{\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}}\sinh{(\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}t)}

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{y}_{t}\right\|}_{2}\leq\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}{({{\left\|\mathbf{x}_{0}\right\|}_{2}}\sinh{(\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}t)}+\frac{{\left\|\mathbf{y}_{0}\right\|}_{2}}{\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}}\cosh{(\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}t)})}

This immediately implies that

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{A}(t)\right\|}_{2}\leq\cosh{(\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}t)}

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{B}(t)\right\|}_{2}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}}\sinh{(\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}t)}

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{C}(t)\right\|}_{2}\leq\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}\sinh{(\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}t)}

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{D}(t)\right\|}_{2}\leq\cosh{(\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}t)}

This proves the first claim of the Lemma.

We now prove the second claim. We verify that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{x}(t)-\mathbf{x}(0)-t\mathbf{y}(0)\\
\mathbf{y}(t)-\mathbf{y}(0)\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{y}(t)-\mathbf{y}(0)\\
\mathbf{M}(t)\mathbf{x}(t)\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{x}(t)-\mathbf{x}(0)-t\mathbf{y}(0)\\
\mathbf{y}(t)-\mathbf{y}(0)\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{y}(t)-\mathbf{y}(0)\\
\mathbf{M}(t)\mathbf{x}(t)\end{bmatrix}=

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{y}(t)-\mathbf{y}(0)\\
\mathbf{M}(t)\mathbf{x}(t)\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{y}(t)-\mathbf{y}(0)\\
\mathbf{M}(t){(\mathbf{x}(t)-\mathbf{x}(0)-t\mathbf{y}(0))}\end{bmatrix}+\begin{bmatrix}0\\
\mathbf{M}(t){(\mathbf{x}(0)+t\mathbf{y}(0))}\end{bmatrix}

Thus

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}{\left\|\mathbf{x}(t)-\mathbf{x}(0)-t\mathbf{y}(0)\right\|}_{2}\leq

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{y}(t)-\mathbf{y}(0)\right\|}_{2}

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}{\left\|\mathbf{y}(t)-\mathbf{y}(0)\right\|}_{2}\leq

\displaystyle L_{\mathbf{M}}{\left\|\mathbf{x}(t)-\mathbf{x}(0)-t\mathbf{y}(0)\right\|}_{2}+L_{\mathbf{M}}{({\left\|\mathbf{x}(0)\right\|}_{2}+t{\left\|\mathbf{y}(0)\right\|}_{2})}

We verify that

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{y}(t)-\mathbf{y}(0)\right\|}_{2}\leq L_{\mathbf{M}}\int_{0}^{t}{\left\|\mathbf{x}(s)-s\mathbf{y}(0)\right\|}_{2}ds+\frac{t^{2}}{2}L_{\mathbf{M}}

Let us apply Lemma
66
with
a_{t}={\left\|\mathbf{x}_{t}-\mathbf{x}(0)-t\mathbf{y}(0)\right\|}_{2}
,
b_{t}={\left\|\mathbf{y}(t)-\mathbf{y}(0)\right\|}_{2}
,
C=L_{\mathbf{M}}
,
D=L_{\mathbf{M}}{\left\|\mathbf{y}(0)\right\|}_{2}
and
E=L_{\mathbf{M}}{\left\|\mathbf{x}(0)\right\|}_{2}

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{x}_{t}-\mathbf{x}(0)-t\mathbf{y}(0)\right\|}_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{L_{\mathbf{M}}{\left\|\mathbf{x}(0)\right\|}_{2}}{L_{\mathbf{M}}}\cosh(\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}t)+\frac{L_{\mathbf{M}}{\left\|\mathbf{y}(0)\right\|}_{2}}{L_{\mathbf{M}}^{3/2}}\sinh{(\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}t)}

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{L_{\mathbf{M}}{\left\|\mathbf{y}(0)\right\|}_{2}}{L_{\mathbf{M}}}t-\frac{L_{\mathbf{M}}{\left\|\mathbf{x}(0)\right\|}_{2}}{L_{\mathbf{M}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{x}(0)\right\|}_{2}{(\cosh(\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}t)-1)}+{\left\|\mathbf{y}(0)\right\|}_{2}{(\frac{1}{\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}}\sinh{(\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}t)}-t)}

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{y}(t)-\mathbf{y}(0)\right\|}_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{L_{\mathbf{M}}{\left\|\mathbf{x}(0)\right\|}_{2}}{\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}}\sinh(\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}t)+\frac{L_{\mathbf{M}}{\left\|\mathbf{y}(0)\right\|}_{2}}{L_{\mathbf{M}}}\cosh{(\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}t)}-\frac{L_{\mathbf{M}}{\left\|\mathbf{y}(0)\right\|}_{2}}{L_{\mathbf{M}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{x}(0)\right\|}_{2}\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}\sinh(\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}t)+{\left\|\mathbf{y}(0)\right\|}_{2}{(\cosh{(\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}t)}-1)}

(I.1)

Again from Lemma
79
, we know that

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{x}(t)\\
\mathbf{y}(t)\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{A}(t)&\mathbf{B}(t)\\
\mathbf{C}(t)&\mathbf{D}(t)\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{x}(0)\\
\mathbf{y}(0)\end{bmatrix}

thus

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{x}(t)-\mathbf{x}(0)-t\mathbf{y}(0)\\
\mathbf{y}(t)-\mathbf{y}(0)\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{A}(t)-I&\mathbf{B}(t)-tI\\
\mathbf{C}(t)&\mathbf{D}(t)-I\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{x}(0)\\
\mathbf{y}(0)\end{bmatrix}

combined with (
I.1
), and using the fact that the above hold for all
\mathbf{y}(0)
and
\mathbf{x}(0)
, we can bound

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{A}(t)-I\right\|}_{2}\leq\cosh(\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}t)-1

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{B}(t)-tI\right\|}_{2}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}}\sinh{(\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}t)}-t

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{D}(t)-I\right\|}_{2}\leq\cosh{(\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}t)}-1

Finally, to prove the third claim,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{x}(t)-t\mathbf{y}(0)-\frac{t^{2}}{2}\mathbf{M}(0)\mathbf{x}(0)\\
\mathbf{y}(t)-\mathbf{y}(0)-t\mathbf{M}(0)\mathbf{x}(0)\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}0&I\\
\mathbf{M}(t)&0\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{x}(t)\\
\mathbf{y}(t)\end{bmatrix}-\begin{bmatrix}0&I\\
\mathbf{M}(0)&0\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{y}(0)\\
\mathbf{v}\end{bmatrix}

∎

Lemma 81
.

Let
\emat
be as defined in Lemma
79
. Let

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{A}(t)&\mathbf{B}(t)\\
\mathbf{C}(t)&\mathbf{D}(t)\end{bmatrix}:=\emat{(t;\begin{bmatrix}0&I\\
\mathbf{M}(t)&0\end{bmatrix})}

for some
\mathbf{M}(t)
. Assume
{\left\|\mathbf{M}(t)\right\|}_{2}\leq L_{\mathbf{M}}
for all
t
. Then for all
t
,

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{C}(t)-t\mathbf{M}(0)\right\|}_{2}\leq\frac{{(L_{\mathbf{M}}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{2}L_{\mathbf{M}}^{2})}}{\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}}\sinh(\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}t)

Proof.

The proof is similar to Lemma
80
. Consider the ODE given by

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{x}\\
\mathbf{y}\end{bmatrix}(t)=\begin{bmatrix}0&I\\
\mathbf{M}(t)&0\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{x}(t)\\
\mathbf{y}(t)\end{bmatrix}

with initial condition
\mathbf{y}(0)=0
.

By Lemma
79
,
\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{A}(t)&\mathbf{B}(t)\\
\mathbf{C}(t)&\mathbf{D}(t)\end{bmatrix}
satisfies

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{x}(t)\\
\mathbf{y}(t)\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{A}(t)&\mathbf{B}(t)\\
\mathbf{C}(t)&\mathbf{D}(t)\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{x}(0)\\
\mathbf{y}(0)\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{x}(t)-\mathbf{x}(0)-\frac{t^{2}}{2}\mathbf{M}(0)\mathbf{x}(0)\\
\mathbf{y}(t)-t\mathbf{M}(0)\mathbf{x}(0)\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}y(t)-t\mathbf{M}(0)\mathbf{x}(0)\\
\mathbf{M}(t)\mathbf{x}(t)-\mathbf{M}(0)\mathbf{x}(0)\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}y(t)-t\mathbf{M}(0)\mathbf{x}(0)\\
\mathbf{M}(t)\mathbf{x}(t)-\mathbf{M}(0)\mathbf{x}(0)\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{y}(t)-t\mathbf{M}(0)\mathbf{x}(0)\\
\mathbf{M}(t){(\mathbf{x}(t)-\mathbf{x}(0)-\frac{t^{2}}{2}\mathbf{M}(0))}\end{bmatrix}+\begin{bmatrix}0\\
{(\mathbf{M}(t)-\mathbf{M}(0))}\mathbf{x}(0)\end{bmatrix}+\begin{bmatrix}0\\
\frac{t^{2}}{2}\mathbf{M}(t)\mathbf{M}(0)\mathbf{x}(0)\end{bmatrix}

By Cauchy Schwarz, for all
t\leq 1
,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}{\left\|\mathbf{x}(t)-\mathbf{x}(0)-\frac{t^{2}}{2}\mathbf{M}(0)\mathbf{x}(0)\right\|}_{2}\leq{\left\|\mathbf{y}(t)-t\mathbf{M}(0)\mathbf{x}(0)\right\|}_{2}

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}{\left\|\mathbf{y}(t)-t\mathbf{M}(0)\mathbf{x}(0)\right\|}_{2}\leq L_{\mathbf{M}}{\left\|\mathbf{x}(t)-\mathbf{x}(0)-\frac{t^{2}}{2}\mathbf{M}(0)\mathbf{x}(0)\right\|}_{2}+{(L_{\mathbf{M}}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{2}L_{\mathbf{M}}^{2})}{\left\|\mathbf{x}(0)\right\|}_{2}

Apply Lemma
66
with
a_{t}={\left\|\mathbf{x}(t)-\mathbf{x}(0)-\frac{t^{2}}{2}\mathbf{M}(0)\mathbf{x}(0)\right\|}_{2}
,
b_{t}={\left\|\mathbf{y}(t)-t\mathbf{M}(0)\mathbf{x}(0)\right\|}_{2}
,
C=L_{\mathbf{M}}
,
D=0
and
E={(L_{\mathbf{M}}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{2}L_{\mathbf{M}}^{2})}{\left\|\mathbf{x}(0)\right\|}_{2}
to get

\displaystyle a_{t}\leq\frac{{(L_{\mathbf{M}}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{2}L_{\mathbf{M}}^{2})}{\left\|\mathbf{x}(0)\right\|}_{2}}{L_{\mathbf{M}}}{(\cosh(\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}t)-1)}

\displaystyle b_{t}\leq\frac{{(L_{\mathbf{M}}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{2}L_{\mathbf{M}}^{2})}{\left\|\mathbf{x}(0)\right\|}_{2}}{\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}}\sinh(\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}t)

Finally, recall that

\displaystyle\mathbf{y}(t)-t\mathbf{M}(0)\mathbf{x}(0)={(\mathbf{C}(t)-t\mathbf{M}(0))}\mathbf{x}(0)

Since we have shown that
{\left\|\mathbf{y}(t)-t\mathbf{M}(0)\mathbf{x}(0)\right\|}_{2}\leq\frac{{(L_{\mathbf{M}}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{2}L_{\mathbf{M}}^{2})}{\left\|\mathbf{x}(0)\right\|}_{2}}{\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}}\sinh(\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}t)
for all
\mathbf{x}(0)
, it follows that

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{C}(t)-t\mathbf{M}(0)\right\|}_{2}\leq\frac{{(L_{\mathbf{M}}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{2}L_{\mathbf{M}}^{2})}}{\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}}\sinh(\sqrt{L_{\mathbf{M}}}t)

∎

Appendix J
Main Theorem and Proofs for SGD Generalization

J.1
Additional Notation and Assumptions

In this section, let us
A:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{S}^{d\times d}
be a matrix-valued function, and let
g(x):=A^{-1}(x)
. Let us consider the Riemannian manifold
{(\mathbb{R}^{d},g)}
, and let
\mathrm{d}{(x,y)}
be the Riemannian distance for this manifold.

Assumption 8
.

Exist strictly positive constants
\lambda_{A},L_{A}
, such that for all
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\lambda_{A}I\prec A(x)\prec L_{A}I

\displaystyle\text{tr}{(A(x)-A(y))}^{2}\leq{L_{A}^{\prime}}^{2}{\left\|x-y\right\|}_{2}^{2}

Assumption 9
.

Exist strictly positive constant
L_{A}^{\prime}
, such that for all
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\text{tr}{(A(x)-A(y))}^{2}\leq{L_{A}^{\prime}}^{2}{\left\|x-y\right\|}_{2}^{2}

Note that under Assumption
9
,
\lambda_{A}{\left\|x-y\right\|}_{2}\leq\mathrm{d}{(x,y)}\leq L_{A}{\left\|x-y\right\|}_{2}
. It is useful to note that combining Assumption
9
with Lemma 1 from
Eldan et al. 2020
gives the following bound:

\displaystyle\text{tr}{(A^{1/2}(x)-A^{1/2}(y))}^{2}\leq\frac{{L_{A}^{\prime}}^{2}}{\lambda_{A}}{\left\|x-y\right\|}_{2}^{2}

We use
\bm{\left\langle}\cdot,\cdot\bm{}
to denote the inner product wrt
g
, i.e. given
u,v\in T_{x}M
,
\bm{\left\langle}u,v\bm{}=\sum_{i,j=1}^{d}u_{i}v_{j}g_{i,j}(x)
. We use
\bm{\left\langle}u,v\bm{}_{2}
to denote the
Euclidean dot product
, i.e.
\bm{\left\langle}u,v\bm{}_{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{d}u_{i}v_{i}
. It follows that
\bm{\left\langle}u,v\bm{}=\bm{\left\langle}u,g(x)v\bm{}_{2}
. We will use
\nabla
to denote the Levi Civita connection. We use the bold version
{\bm{\nabla}}
to denote the
Euclidean derivative
of a function
h:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
, i.e.

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}f(x+tv)=\bm{\left\langle}{\bm{\nabla}}f(x),v\bm{}_{2}

We will use
e_{i}
to denote the basis vectors, e.g.
e_{1}=[0,1,0,0...0]
. We will also use
\partial_{i}f(x):=\bm{\left\langle}{\bm{\nabla}}f(x),e_{i}\bm{}
. Similarly, let
\nabla^{2}f
denote the Hessian tensor wrt the Riemannian metric, and
{\bm{\nabla}}^{2}f
denote the Hessian wrt Euclidean metric.

We let
\Gamma
denote the Christoffel symbols of
g
. Specifically,for
i,j,k\in\left\{1...d\right\}
,

\displaystyle\Gamma^{k}_{ij}(x):=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{l=1}^{d}{({({(g(x))}^{-1})}_{kl}{(\partial_{i}g_{jl}(x)+\partial_{j}g_{il}(x)-\partial_{l}g_{ij}(x))})}

(J.2)

It will also be convenient to define
\Gamma^{k}(x)
as the matrix whose
(i,j)^{th}
entry is
\Gamma^{k}_{i,j}
. With slight overloading of notation, we define
\phi(x):\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\phi(x,v):\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
as follows:

\displaystyle\phi_{k}(x):=\text{tr}{({(g(x))}^{-1}\Gamma^{k}(x))}

\displaystyle\phi_{k}(x,v):=v^{T}\Gamma^{k}(x)v

(J.3)

Assumption 10
.

Exist
L_{\phi}
,
L_{\phi}^{\prime}
, such that for all
x,y,v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle{\left\|\phi(x,v)\right\|}_{2}\leq L_{\phi}{\left\|v\right\|}_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle{\left\|\phi(x,v)-\phi(y,v)\right\|}_{2}\leq L_{\phi}^{\prime}{\left\|x-y\right\|}_{2}{\left\|v\right\|}_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle{\left\|\phi(x,v)-\phi(x,w)\right\|}_{2}\leq L_{\phi}{\left\|v-w\right\|}_{2}{({\left\|v\right\|}+{\left\|w\right\|})}

Remark 5
.

The form of assumption
10
really arises from its definition in (
J.3
).
L_{\phi}
and
L_{\phi}^{\prime}
can be bounded, for example, if
\Gamma^{i}_{jk}
is coordinate-wise bounded and smooth.

We note a useful characterization of geodesics in terms of coordinates below (see, e.g. Eqn 4.16 of
Lee 2018
). A curve
x(t):\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
is a geodesic if and only if, for all
k\in\left\{1...d\right\}
,

\displaystyle x^{\prime\prime}_{k}(t)=-\sum_{i,j=1}^{d}{(x^{\prime}_{i}(t)x^{\prime}_{j}(t)\Gamma^{k}_{ij}{(x(t))})}=-\phi(x(t),x^{\prime}(t))

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\qquad

\displaystyle x^{\prime\prime}(t)=\phi(x(t),x^{\prime}(t))

(J.4)

Assumption 11
.

We assume that the random vector field satisfies, for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\xi(x)}\right]=0\qquad\mathbb{E}\left[{\xi(x)\xi(x)^{T}}\right]=A(x)

Assumption 12
.

Assume that for all
x,y
, with probability
1
,

\displaystyle{\left\|\beta(x)-\beta(y)\right\|}_{2}\leq L_{\beta,2}^{\prime}{\left\|x-y\right\|}_{2}

\displaystyle{\left\|\xi(x)-\xi(y)\right\|}_{2}\leq L_{\xi,2}^{\prime}{\left\|x-y\right\|}_{2}

\displaystyle{\left\|\xi(x)\right\|}_{2}\leq L_{\xi,2}

J.2
Euclidean Walk to Manifold Walk

Lemma 82

(One Step Error On Euclidean Space)
.

Let
\beta
and
\xi
satisfy Assumption
12
and Assumption
11
. Assume
10
,
8
and
9
. Let
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
be arbitrary, and define

\displaystyle x^{\prime}=\Exp_{x}{(\delta\beta(x)+\sqrt{\delta}\xi(x)+\frac{1}{2}\phi(x))}

\displaystyle\tilde{x}^{\prime}=x+\delta\beta(x)+\sqrt{\delta}\xi(x)

Then

\displaystyle{\left\|x^{\prime}-\tilde{x}^{\prime}\right\|}_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq O{(\delta^{6}{\left\|\beta(x)\right\|}_{2}^{6}+\delta^{2})}

\displaystyle{\left\|x^{\prime}-\tilde{x}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{2}\phi(x,\sqrt{\delta}\xi(x))-\frac{\delta}{2}\phi(x)\right\|}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq O{(\delta^{3}{\left\|\beta(x)\right\|}_{2}^{3}+\delta^{3/2})}

where
O{()}
hides polynomial dependence on
L_{A},\frac{1}{\lambda_{A}},L_{\phi},L_{\phi}^{\prime},d,L_{\xi}
.

Proof of Lemma
82
.

Let us define the geodesic
y(t)=\Exp_{x}{(t{(\delta\beta(x)+\delta\phi(x)+\sqrt{\delta}\xi(x))})}
for
t\in[0,1]
. From (
J.4
), we verify that
y(t)
satisfies the following second order ODE:

\displaystyle y(0)=x

\displaystyle v(0)=\delta\beta(x)+\sqrt{\delta}\xi(x)+\frac{\delta}{2}\phi(x)

\displaystyle dy(t)=v(t)dt

\displaystyle dv(t)=-\phi(y(t),v(t))

Thus
y(1)=x^{\prime}
. We will now bound the distance between
\tilde{x}^{\prime}
and
y(1)
.

\displaystyle{\left\|y(1)-\tilde{x}^{\prime}\right\|}_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\left\|\int_{0}^{1}v(t)-\delta\beta(x)-\sqrt{\delta}\xi(x)dt\right\|}_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\left\|\int_{0}^{1}v(t)-v(0)+\frac{\delta}{2}\phi{(x)}dt\right\|}_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\left\|\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{t}-\phi{(y(s),v(s))}+\delta\phi{(x)}dsdt\right\|}_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{t}{\left\|-\phi{(y(s),v(s))}+\delta\phi{(x)}\right\|}_{2}^{2}dsdt

(J.5)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 8\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{t}{\left\|\phi{(y(s),v(s))}-\phi{(y(0),v(s))}\right\|}_{2}^{2}+{\left\|\phi{(y(0),v(s))}-\phi{(y(0),v(0))}\right\|}_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+{\left\|\phi{(y(0),v(0))}-\delta\phi{(x)}\right\|}_{2}^{2}dsdt

We bound each of the terms above. It will first be convenient to establish a uniform bound on
{\left\|v(t)\right\|}
,
{\left\|v(t)-v(0)\right\|}
and
{\left\|y(s)-y(0)\right\|}
for all
t
. Notice by definition of the geodesic that
{\left\|v(t)\right\|}={\left\|v(0)\right\|}
(note: this is
g
-norm) for all
t
, equivalently,
v(t)^{T}g(y(t))v(t)=g(0)^{T}g(y(0))v(0)
for all
t
. Let us denote, by
L_{v}:=\sup_{t\in[0,1]}{\left\|v(t)\right\|}_{2}

\displaystyle L_{v}\leq\frac{L_{A}}{\lambda_{A}}{\left\|v(0)\right\|}_{2}\leq\frac{L_{A}}{\lambda_{A}}\cdot{({\left\|\delta\beta(x)\right\|}_{2}+\sqrt{\delta}L_{\xi})}

\displaystyle{\left\|v(t)-v(0)\right\|}_{2}\leq L_{\phi}L_{v}^{2}

\displaystyle{\left\|y(s)-y(0)\right\|}_{2}\leq L_{v}

By Assumption
10
,

\displaystyle{\left\|\phi{(y(s),v(s))}-\phi{(y(0),v(s))}\right\|}_{2}\leq L_{\phi}^{\prime}{\left\|y(s)-y(0)\right\|}_{2}{\left\|v(s)\right\|}_{2}^{2}\leq L_{\phi}^{\prime}L_{v}^{3}

\displaystyle{\left\|\phi{(y(0),v(s))}-\phi{(y(0),v(0))}\right\|}_{2}\leq L_{\phi}^{2}{({\left\|v(s)\right\|}_{2}+{\left\|v(0)\right\|}_{2})}{\left\|v(s)-v(0)\right\|}_{2}\leq 2L_{\phi}L_{v}^{3}

\displaystyle{\left\|\phi{(y(0),v(0))}-\delta\phi{(x)}\right\|}_{2}\leq L_{\phi}{(\delta dL_{A}+\delta^{2}{\left\|\beta(x)\right\|}^{2}+\delta L_{\xi}^{2})}

Plugging and simplifying,

\displaystyle{\left\|y(1)-\tilde{x}^{\prime}\right\|}_{2}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle O{(\delta^{6}{\left\|\beta(x)\right\|}_{2}^{6}+\delta^{4}{\left\|\beta(x)\right\|}_{2}^{4}+\delta^{2})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O{(\delta^{6}{\left\|\beta(x)\right\|}_{2}^{6}+\delta^{2})}

where
O{()}
hides polynomial dependence on
L_{A},\frac{1}{\lambda_{A}},L_{\phi},L_{\phi}^{\prime},d,L_{\xi}
.

We can also bound

\displaystyle{\left\|\phi{(y(0),v(0))}-\phi{(x,\sqrt{\delta}\xi(x))}\right\|}_{2}\leq L_{\phi}{(L_{v}+\sqrt{\delta}{\left\|\xi(x)\right\|}_{2})}{({\left\|\delta\beta(x)\right\|}_{2}+\delta L_{\phi})}

Next, following the same steps leading up to (
J.5
),

\displaystyle{\left\|y(1)-\tilde{x}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{2}\phi(y(0),\sqrt{\delta}\xi(x))-\frac{\delta}{2}\phi(x)\right\|}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{t}{\left\|-\phi{(y(s),v(s))}+\delta\phi{(x)}+\phi(y(0),\sqrt{\delta}\xi(x))-\delta\phi(x)\right\|}_{2}dsdt

\displaystyle\leq
