\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{t}{\left\|\phi{(y(s),v(s))}-\phi{(y(0),v(s))}\right\|}_{2}+{\left\|\phi{(y(0),v(s))}-\phi{(y(0),v(0))}\right\|}_{2}

\displaystyle\quad+{\left\|\phi{(y(0),v(0))}-\phi{(y(0),\sqrt{\delta}\xi(x))}\right\|}_{2}dsdt

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O{(\delta^{3}{\left\|\beta(x)\right\|}_{2}^{3}+\delta^{2}{\left\|\beta(x)\right\|}_{2}^{2}+\delta^{3/2}+\delta^{3/2}{\left\|\beta(x)\right\|}_{2}+\delta^{3/2})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O{(\delta^{3}{\left\|\beta(x)\right\|}_{2}^{3}+\delta^{3/2})}

where
O{()}
hides polynomial dependence on
L_{A},\frac{1}{\lambda_{A}},L_{\phi},L_{\phi}^{\prime},d,L_{\xi}
.

∎

Lemma 83
.

Assume
\beta
and
\xi
satisfy Assumption
12
and Assumption
11
. Assume
10
,
8
and
9
.

\displaystyle x_{k+1}=\Exp_{x_{k}}{(\delta\beta(x_{k})+\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k})+\frac{\delta}{2}\phi(x_{k}))}

\displaystyle z_{k+1}=x_{k}+\delta\beta(z_{k})+\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(z_{k})

with
x_{0}=z_{0}
. Assume that
K\delta\leq\min\left\{\frac{1}{L_{\beta,2}^{\prime}},\frac{1}{L_{\xi,2}^{\prime}}\right\}
, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|x_{K}-z_{K}\right\|}_{2}^{2}}\right]\leq

\displaystyle O{(K\delta^{6}{(\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|\beta(x_{k})\right\|}_{2}^{6}}\right])}+K\delta^{2})}

where
O{()}
hides polynomial dependence on
L_{A},\frac{1}{\lambda_{A}},L_{\phi},L_{\phi}^{\prime},d,L_{\xi,2}
. and where expectation is wrt the randomness in
\xi_{0}...\xi_{K-1}
.

Proof.

Consider a fixed
k
. Let us define the geodesic
y(t)=\Exp_{x_{k}}{(t{(\delta\beta(x_{k})+\delta\phi(x_{k})+\sqrt{\delta}\xi(x_{k}))})}
for
t\in[0,1]
. From (
J.4
), we verify that
y(t)
satisfies the following second order ODE:

\displaystyle y_{k}(0)=x_{k}

\displaystyle v_{k}(0)=\delta\beta(x_{k})+\sqrt{\delta}\xi(x_{k})+\frac{\delta}{2}\phi(x_{k})

\displaystyle dy_{k}(t)=v_{k}(t)dt

\displaystyle dv_{k}(t)=-\phi(y_{k}(t),v_{k}(t))

Thus
y_{k}(1)=x_{k+1}
. Next, we define
\bar{x}_{k+1}:=x_{k}+\delta\beta(x_{k})+\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k})
. We can apply Lemma
82
to bound

\displaystyle{\left\|x_{k+1}-\bar{x}_{k+1}\right\|}_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq O{(\delta^{6}{\left\|\beta(x_{k})\right\|}_{2}^{6}+\delta^{2})}

\displaystyle{\left\|x_{k+1}-\bar{x}_{k+1}+\frac{1}{2}\phi(x_{k},\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k}))-\frac{\delta}{2}\phi(x_{k})\right\|}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq O{(\delta^{3}{\left\|\beta(x_{k})\right\|}_{2}^{3}+\delta^{3/2})}

(J.6)

where
O{()}
hides polynomial dependence on
L_{A},\frac{1}{\lambda_{A}},L_{\phi},L_{\phi}^{\prime},d,L_{\xi,2}
.

Consider some fixed
k
. From now on, unless otherwise stated, all expectations are wrt
\xi_{k}
, conditioned on
\xi_{0}...\xi_{k-1}
.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|x_{k+1}-z_{k+1}\right\|}_{2}^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\left\|x_{k}-z_{k}\right\|}_{2}^{2}+\bm{\left\langle}x_{k}-z_{k},\mathbb{E}\left[{x_{k+1}-x_{k}-(z_{k+1}-z_{k})}\right]\bm{}_{2}+\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|x_{k+1}-x_{k}-(z_{k+1}-z_{k})\right\|}_{2}^{2}}\right]

We now bound the
\bm{\left\langle}x_{k}-z_{k},\mathbb{E}\left[{x_{k+1}-x_{k}-(z_{k+1}-z_{k})}\right]\bm{}_{2}
term. Notice that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{x_{k+1}-x_{k}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\bar{x}_{k+1}-x_{k}+x_{k+1}-\bar{x}_{k+1}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\delta\beta(x_{k})+x_{k+1}-\bar{x}_{k+1}+\frac{1}{2}\phi(x_{k},\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k}))-\frac{\delta}{2}\phi(x_{k})+\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k})}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\delta\beta(x_{k})+x_{k+1}-\bar{x}_{k+1}+\frac{1}{2}\phi(x_{k},\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k}))-\frac{\delta}{2}\phi(x_{k})}\right]

where the second equality is because
\mathbb{E}\left[{\phi(x_{k},\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k}))-\delta\phi(x_{k})}\right]=0
, and the third inequality is because
\mathbb{E}\left[{\xi_{k}(x_{k})}\right]=0
. We can also verify that
\mathbb{E}\left[{z_{k+1}-z_{k}}\right]=\delta\beta(z_{k})
. Plugging these into
\bm{\left\langle}x_{k}-z_{k},\mathbb{E}\left[{x_{k+1}-x_{k}-(z_{k+1}-z_{k})}\right]\bm{}_{2}
, we get

\displaystyle\bm{\left\langle}x_{k}-z_{k},\mathbb{E}\left[{x_{k+1}-x_{k}-(z_{k+1}-z_{k})}\right]\bm{}_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bm{\left\langle}x_{k}-z_{k},\delta\beta(x_{k})-\delta\beta(z_{k})\bm{}_{2}+\bm{\left\langle}x_{k}-z_{k},\mathbb{E}\left[{x_{k+1}-\bar{x}_{k+1}+\frac{1}{2}\phi(x_{k},\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k}))-\frac{\delta}{2}\phi(x_{k})}\right]\bm{}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\delta{L_{\beta,2}^{\prime}}{\left\|x_{k}-z_{k}\right\|}_{2}^{2}+\frac{1}{K}{\left\|x_{k}-z_{k}\right\|}_{2}^{2}+O{(K\delta^{6}{\left\|\beta(x_{k})\right\|}_{2}^{6}+K\delta^{3})}

where we use Assumption
12
and Young’s inequality from (
J.6
).

Finally, we can bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|x_{k+1}-x_{k}-(z_{k+1}-z_{k})\right\|}_{2}^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|x_{k+1}-\bar{x}_{k+1}+\bar{x}_{k+1}-x_{k}-(z_{k+1}-z_{k})\right\|}_{2}^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|x_{k+1}-\bar{x}_{k+1}\right\|}_{2}^{2}}\right]+2\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|\bar{x}_{k+1}-x_{k}-(z_{k+1}-z_{k})\right\|}_{2}^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O{(\delta^{6}{\left\|\beta(x_{k})\right\|}_{2}^{6}+\delta^{2})}+2\delta^{2}{L_{\beta,2}^{\prime}}^{2}{\left\|x_{k}-z_{k}\right\|}_{2}^{2}+2\delta^{2}{L_{\beta,2}^{\prime}}^{2}{\left\|x_{k}-z_{k}\right\|}_{2}^{2}+2\delta{L_{\xi,2}^{\prime}}^{2}{\left\|x_{k}-z_{k}\right\|}_{2}^{2}

where we use (
J.6
) and Assumption
12
.

Putting everything together, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|x_{k+1}-z_{k+1}\right\|}_{2}^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{(1+2\delta L_{\beta,2}^{\prime}+2\delta{L_{\xi,2}^{\prime}}^{2}+\frac{1}{K})}{\left\|x_{k}-z_{k}\right\|}_{2}^{2}+O{(K\delta^{6}{\left\|\beta(x_{k})\right\|}_{2}^{6}+K\delta^{3}+\delta^{2})}

Applying the above recursively for
k=0...K-1
, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|x_{K}-z_{K}\right\|}_{2}^{2}}\right]\leq

\displaystyle\exp{(1+2K\delta L_{\beta,2}^{\prime}+2K\delta{L_{\xi,2}^{\prime}}^{2})}+O{(K\delta^{6}{(\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|\beta(x_{k})\right\|}_{2}^{6}}\right])}+K^{2}\delta^{3}+K\delta^{2})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O{(K\delta^{6}{(\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|\beta(x_{k})\right\|}_{2}^{6}}\right])}+K^{2}\delta^{3}+K\delta^{2})}

∎

J.3
Manifold SDE to Euclidean SDE

Lemma 84
.

Consider the Riemannian manifold
(\mathbb{R}^{d},g)
. Let
\beta
be a vector field satisfying Assumption
2
. Given any initial point
x_{0}
, orthonormal basis
E
of
T_{x_{0}}M
, standard Brownian motion
\mathbf{B}(t)
and any
T\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
, let
x(t):=\Phi(t;x_{0},E,\beta,\mathbf{B})
where
\Phi
is as defined in (
3.4
),
E
is an arbitrary orthonormal basis of
x(0)
,
\mathbf{B}
is a standard Brownian motion over
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Let us also define the Euclidean SDE

\displaystyle dz(t)=\beta(z(t))dt-\frac{1}{2}\phi(z(t))dt+g(z(t))^{-1/2}d\mathbf{W}(t)

with
z(0)=x(0)
, and where
\mathbf{W}(t)
is another standard Brownian motion and
\phi
is as defined in (
J.3
).

Then
x
and
z
have the same distribution.

Proof.

We will verify that
x(t)
and
z(t)
have the same generator. The conclusion follows from Theorem 1.3.6 of
Hsu 2002
which states that diffusion measusure with the given generator and initial distribution is unique.

We have already observed in Lemma
2
that
x(t)
is the diffusion process generated by
Lf=\bm{\left\langle}\nabla f,\beta\bm{}+\frac{1}{2}\Delta(f)
, where
\Delta
denotes the Laplace Beltrami operator. We verify that
z(t)
has the same generator.

It is important to recall our definition of
{\bm{\nabla}}^{2},{\bm{\nabla}},\bm{\left\langle}\bm{}_{2},\nabla^{2},\nabla,\bm{\left\langle}\bm{}
in Section
J.1
.

By Ito’s Lemma, for any twice continuously differentiable
f
,

\displaystyle df(z(t))=

\displaystyle\bm{\left\langle}{\bm{\nabla}}f(z(t)),\beta(z(t))\bm{}_{2}dt-\frac{1}{2}\bm{\left\langle}{\bm{\nabla}}f(z(t)),\phi(z(t))\bm{}_{2}+\frac{1}{2}\text{tr}{(g(z(t))^{-1}{\bm{\nabla}}^{2}f(z(t)))}dt

\displaystyle\quad+\bm{\left\langle}{\bm{\nabla}}f(z(t)),g(z(t))^{-1}d\mathbf{W}(t)\bm{}_{2}

By definition of
\bm{\left\langle}\bm{}_{2},{\bm{\nabla}},\bm{\left\langle},\bm{}\nabla
,
\bm{\left\langle}{\bm{\nabla}}f(z(t)),\beta(z(t))\bm{}_{2}=\bm{\left\langle}\nabla f(z(t)),\beta(z(t))\bm{}
.

Next, we verify that
-\frac{1}{2}\bm{\left\langle}{\bm{\nabla}}f(z(t)),\phi(z(t))\bm{}_{2}+\frac{1}{2}\text{tr}{(g(z(t))^{-1}{\bm{\nabla}}^{2}f(z(t)))}dt=\frac{1}{2}\Delta f(z(t))
. This is because

\displaystyle\Delta f=

\displaystyle\sum_{j,k=1}^{d}{(g^{-1})}_{j,k}{({\bm{\nabla}}^{2}f)}_{k,j}-\sum_{j,k,\ell}{(g^{-1})}_{jk}\Gamma^{\ell}_{jk}{(\nabla f)}_{\ell}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\text{tr}{(g^{-1}{\bm{\nabla}}^{2}f)}+\bm{\left\langle}\nabla f,\phi\bm{}_{2}

where we use the definition of
\phi
in (
J.3
).

We have thus verified that

\displaystyle df(z(t))-\bm{\left\langle}\nabla f(z(t)),\beta(z(t))\bm{}-\frac{1}{2}\Delta f(z(t))=\bm{\left\langle}{\bm{\nabla}}f(z(t)),g(z(t))^{-1}d\mathbf{W}(t)\bm{}_{2}

is a martingale.
∎

J.4
A CLT for Euclidean SDE

Lemma 85
.

Assume
M={(\mathbb{R}^{d},g)}
satisfies Assumptions
3
,
4
and
5
. Let
A=g^{-1}
satisfy Assumption
8
and
9
. Let
\beta
and
\xi
satisfy Assumptions
12
and
11
(equivalent to Assumption
7
). Let
\phi
satisfy Assumption
10
. Let
\beta^{g}:=\beta+\phi
satisfying Assumption
2
. Assume that
\beta^{g}(x^{*})=0
at some
x^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Let
\xi
be a vector field and assume there exists constants
L_{\xi},L_{\xi}^{\prime},L_{\xi}^{\prime\prime}
so that Assumption
6
holds with probability 1.

Let
\delta\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
and
K\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}
, Let
z_{0}
be a point, let
{s}:=\max\left\{1,\mathrm{d}{(z_{0},x^{*})}\right\}
. Define

\displaystyle z_{k+1}=z_{k}+\delta\beta(z_{k})+\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(z_{k})

and SDE

\displaystyle dy(t)=\beta(y(t))dt+A(y(t))^{1/2}d\mathbf{W}(t)

where
\mathbf{W}(t)
is a Brownian motion and where
y(0)=z_{0}
.
There exists constant
{\mathcal{C}}_{1}
, which depend polynomially on
L_{\beta},L_{\beta}^{\prime},L_{\xi},L_{\xi}^{\prime},L_{\xi}^{\prime\prime},L_{R},L_{R}^{\prime},d,\mathcal{R},L_{\xi,2}^{\prime},L_{\beta,2}^{\prime}
, such that for any positive
T\leq\frac{1}{{\mathcal{C}}_{1}}
and
\delta:={T}^{3}
and
K:=T/\delta
, there exists a coupling between
y
and
\hat{y}
, such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|z_{K}-y(K\delta)\right\|}_{2}}\right]\leq{O}{({(K\delta)}^{3/2}{(1+{\left\|z_{0}-x^{*}\right\|}_{2}^{3})})}

where
\tilde{O}
hides polynomial dependency on
L_{\beta},L_{\beta}^{\prime},L_{\xi},L_{\xi}^{\prime},L_{\xi}^{\prime\prime},L_{R},L_{R}^{\prime},d,\mathcal{R},L_{A},\frac{1}{\lambda_{A}},L_{\phi},L_{\phi}^{\prime},d,L_{\xi,2}^{\prime},L_{\beta,2}^{\prime}
.

Remark 6
.

Note that the requirement that
\delta=(K\delta)^{3}
means that for
K\delta\varepsilon
error, we need
K\delta=\varepsilon^{2}
and
\delta=\varepsilon^{6}
.

Proof of Lemma
85
.

Let us define

\displaystyle x_{k+1}=\Exp_{x_{k}}{(\delta\beta(x_{k})+\frac{\delta}{2}\phi(x_{k})+\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k}))}

By Lemma
83
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|x_{K}-z_{K}\right\|}_{2}^{2}}\right]\leq

\displaystyle O{(K\delta^{6}{(\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|\beta(z_{k})\right\|}_{2}^{6}}\right])}+K\delta^{2})}

By Lemma
90
and Assumption
12
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|\beta(z_{k})\right\|}_{2}^{6}}\right]\leq

\displaystyle 8{L_{\beta,2}^{\prime}}^{6}\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|z_{k}-z_{0}\right\|}_{2}^{6}}\right]+8\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|\beta(z_{0})\right\|}_{2}^{6}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2^{11}{L_{\beta,2}^{\prime}}^{6}{({K^{3}\delta^{6}{\left\|\beta(z_{0})\right\|}_{2}^{6}}+K^{3}\delta^{3}L_{\xi,2}^{6})}+8\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|\beta(z_{0})\right\|}_{2}^{6}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 9\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|\beta(z_{0})\right\|}_{2}^{6}}\right]+2^{11}{L_{\beta,2}^{\prime}}^{6}K^{3}\delta^{3}L_{\xi,2}^{6}

where we assume that
K\delta\leq 1/(32L_{\beta,2}^{\prime})
. Plugging into the earlier bound from Lemma
83
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|x_{K}-z_{K}\right\|}_{2}^{2}}\right]\leq

\displaystyle O{(K\delta^{6}{\left\|\beta(z_{0})\right\|}_{2}^{6}+K\delta^{2})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O{({(K\delta)}^{15}{\left\|\beta(z_{0})\right\|}_{2}^{6}+{(K\delta)}^{4})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O{({(K\delta)}^{15}{\left\|z_{0}-x^{*}\right\|}_{2}^{6}+{(K\delta)}^{4})}

using the fact that
\delta=(K\delta)^{3}
by definition. The last line is because
{\left\|\beta(z_{0})\right\|}_{2}\leq L_{\beta,2}^{\prime}{\left\|z_{0}-x^{*}\right\|}_{2}+{\left\|\beta(x^{*})\right\|}_{2}\leq L_{\beta,2}^{\prime}{\left\|z_{0}-x^{*}\right\|}_{2}+L_{\phi}
.

By Lemma
82
, we verify that
y(t)
has the same distribution as
\hat{y}(t)=\Phi(K\delta;y_{0},E,\beta,\mathbf{B})
where
E
is some orthonormal basis of
T_{y_{0}}M
and
\mathbf{B}(t)
is a standard Brownian motion. By Theorem
2
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(\hat{y}_{K},{x}_{K})}}\right]\leq{O}{({(K\delta)}^{3/2}{(1+\mathrm{d}{(z_{0},x^{*})}^{3})})}={O}{({(K\delta)}^{3/2}{(1+{\left\|z_{0}-x^{*}\right\|}_{2}^{3})})}

Combining the bounds on
\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(\hat{y}_{K},{x}_{K})}}\right]
and
\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|x_{K}-z_{K}\right\|}_{2}^{2}}\right]
(which is entirely dominated in the big-O sense), and using Assumption
8
, and recalling that
\hat{y}_{K}\overset{d}{=}y(K\delta)
, we conclude that there is a coupling between
z
and
y
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|z_{K}-y(K\delta)\right\|}_{2}}\right]\leq{O}{({(K\delta)}^{3/2}{(1+{\left\|z_{0}-x^{*}\right\|}_{2}^{3})})}

∎

J.5
Notation and Assumptions for SGD

Let
\mathcal{S}
denote a set of samples, let
p_{\mathcal{S}}
denote the population distribution over
\mathcal{S}
.
\ell(x,s):\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathcal{S}
denote a loss function, where
x
can be viewed as parameterizing a model. We assume that that
\ell
is bounded in the following sense

Assumption 13
.

Exists
L_{\ell}
such that for all
s,s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}
,

\displaystyle{\left\|{\bm{\nabla}}\ell(x,s)-{\bm{\nabla}}\ell(x,s^{\prime})\right\|}_{2}\leq L_{\ell}

Let
\mathcal{S}_{n}=\left\{s_{1},s_{2},...s_{n}\right\}
, where
s_{i}
are sampled i.i.d from
p_{\mathcal{S}}
. Let
\tilde{s}_{1}\sim p_{\mathcal{S}}
be sampled independently of
s_{1}...s_{n}
. Let
\tilde{\mathcal{S}}_{n}={(\tilde{s}_{1},s_{2}...s_{n})}
, i.e.
\tilde{\mathcal{S}}_{n}
is
\mathcal{S}_{n}
with the first sample replaced.

Let

\displaystyle\ell_{n}(x):=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell(x,s_{i})

\displaystyle\tilde{\ell}_{n}=\frac{1}{n}{(\ell(x,s_{1})+\sum_{i=2}^{n}\ell(x,s_{i}))}

(J.7)

Let us define the matrix
A_{n}(x)
to be the noise covariance of gradient under the empirical distribution over
\mathcal{S}_{n}
, i.e.

\displaystyle A_{n}(x):=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{({\bm{\nabla}}\ell(x,s_{i})-{\bm{\nabla}}\ell(x))}{({\bm{\nabla}}\ell_{n}(x,s_{i})-{\bm{\nabla}}\ell_{n}(x))}^{T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\bm{\nabla}}\ell(x,s_{i}){\bm{\nabla}}\ell(x,s_{i})^{T}-{\bm{\nabla}}\ell_{n}(x){\bm{\nabla}}\ell_{n}(x)^{T}

(J.8)

Let
\tilde{A}_{n}
denote the noise covariance of gradient under under the empirical distribution over
\tilde{\mathcal{S}}_{n}

\displaystyle\tilde{A}_{n}(x):=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=2}^{n}{({\bm{\nabla}}\ell(x,s_{i})-{\bm{\nabla}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(x))}{({\bm{\nabla}}\ell(x,s_{i})-{\bm{\nabla}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(x))}^{T}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{n}{({\bm{\nabla}}\ell(x,\tilde{s}_{1})-{\bm{\nabla}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(x))}{({\bm{\nabla}}\ell(x,\tilde{s}_{1})-{\bm{\nabla}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(x))}^{T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}{({\bm{\nabla}}\ell(x,s_{1}){\bm{\nabla}}\ell(x,s_{1})^{T}+\sum_{i=2}^{n}{\bm{\nabla}}\ell(x,s_{i}){\bm{\nabla}}\ell(x,s_{i})^{T})}-{\bm{\nabla}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(x){\bm{\nabla}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(x)^{T}

(J.9)

Lemma 86
.

Let
\ell_{n},\tilde{\ell}_{n},A_{n},\tilde{A}_{n}
be as defined in (
J.7
), (
J.8
) and (
J.9
) respectively. Under Assumption
13
, for all
x
,

\displaystyle\text{tr}{(A_{n}(x)-\tilde{A}_{n}(x))}^{2}\leq\frac{128dL_{\ell}^{4}}{n^{2}}

\displaystyle\text{tr}{(A_{n}^{1/2}(x)-\tilde{A}_{n}^{1/2}(x))}^{2}\leq\frac{128dL_{\ell}^{4}}{\lambda_{A}n^{2}}

\displaystyle{\left\|\tilde{\ell}_{n}(x)-{\ell}_{n}(x)\right\|}_{2}\leq\frac{2L_{\ell}}{n}

We verify that

\displaystyle A_{n}(x)-\tilde{A}_{n}(x)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\underbrace{{({\bm{\nabla}}\ell(x,\tilde{s}_{1})-{\bm{\nabla}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(x))}{({\bm{\nabla}}\ell(x,\tilde{s}_{1})-{\bm{\nabla}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(x))}^{T}}_{\hbox to9.22pt{\vbox to9.22pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.60942pt\lower-4.60942pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=2}^{n}\underbrace{{({\bm{\nabla}}\ell_{n}(x)-{\bm{\nabla}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(x))}{({\bm{\nabla}}\ell(x,s_{i})-{\bm{\nabla}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(x))}^{T}}_{\hbox to9.22pt{\vbox to9.22pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.60942pt\lower-4.60942pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

, we can bound

}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\right\|}_{2}+{\left\|\hbox to11.37pt{\vbox to11.37pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 5.68657pt\lower-5.68657pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 7.59 0 C 7.59 4.19 4.19 7.59 0 7.59 C -4.19 7.59 -7.59 4.19 -7.59 0 C -7.59 -4.19 -4.19 -7.59 0 -7.59 C 4.19 -7.59 7.59 -4.19 7.59 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.5pt}{-3.22221pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\right\|}_{2}\leq 8L_{\ell}^{2}
. Furthermore, we verify that
{\left\|\tilde{\ell}_{n}(x)-{\ell}_{n}(x)\right\|}_{2}\leq\frac{2L_{\ell}}{n}
, so that
{\left\|\hbox to11.37pt{\vbox to11.37pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 5.68657pt\lower-5.68657pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 7.59 0 C 7.59 4.19 4.19 7.59 0 7.59 C -4.19 7.59 -7.59 4.19 -7.59 0 C -7.59 -4.19 -4.19 -7.59 0 -7.59 C 4.19 -7.59 7.59 -4.19 7.59 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.5pt}{-3.22221pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\right\|}_{2}+{\left\|\hbox to11.37pt{\vbox to11.37pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 5.68657pt\lower-5.68657pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 7.59 0 C 7.59 4.19 4.19 7.59 0 7.59 C -4.19 7.59 -7.59 4.19 -7.59 0 C -7.59 -4.19 -4.19 -7.59 0 -7.59 C 4.19 -7.59 7.59 -4.19 7.59 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.5pt}{-3.22221pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\right\|}_{2}\leq\frac{8L_{\ell}^{2}}{n}
. Put together, we can bound
{\left\|A_{n}(x)-\tilde{A}_{n}(x)\right\|}_{2}\leq\frac{16L_{\ell}^{2}}{n}
and thus

\displaystyle\text{tr}{(A_{n}(x)-\tilde{A}_{n}(x))}^{2}\leq\frac{128dL_{\ell}^{4}}{n^{2}}

\displaystyle{\left\|\tilde{\ell}_{n}(x)-{\ell}_{n}(x)\right\|}_{2}\leq\frac{2L_{\ell}}{n}

The bound on
\text{tr}{(A_{n}^{1/2}(x)-\tilde{A}_{n}^{1/2}(x))}^{2}
then follows from Lemma 1 of
Eldan et al. 2020
.
∎

Lemma 87
.

Let
u(x)
be a vector field and
F(x)
be a matrix valued function. Assume that for all
x
,
{\left\|\tilde{u}(x)-u(x)\right\|}_{2}\leq\varepsilon_{u}
and
{\left\|\tilde{F}(x)-F(x)\right\|}_{F}\leq\varepsilon_{F}
. Assume further that
{\left\|u(x)-u(y)\right\|}_{2}\leq L_{u}^{\prime}{\left\|x-y\right\|}_{2}
and
\text{tr}{(F(x)-F(y))}^{2}\leq{L_{F}^{\prime}}^{2}{\left\|x-y\right\|}_{2}^{2}
. Let

\displaystyle dx(t)=u(x(t))dt+F(x(t))dB_{t}

\displaystyle dy(t)=\tilde{u}(y(t))dt+\tilde{F}(y(t))dB_{t}

initialized at some arbitrary points
x_{0}
and
y_{0}
respectively.

Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|x(T)-y(T)\right\|}_{2}^{2}}\right]\leq e^{T{(2L_{u}^{\prime}+2L_{F}^{\prime}+1)}}{({\left\|x(0)-y(0)\right\|}_{2}^{2}+8T{(\varepsilon_{u}^{2}+\varepsilon_{F}^{2})})}

Proof.

Let us denote by
v(x):=u(x)-\tilde{u}(x)
and
H(x):=F(x)-\tilde{F}(x)
. By Ito’s Lemma,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|x(t)-y(t)\right\|}_{2}^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{2\bm{\left\langle}x(t)-y(t),{(u(x(t))-u(y(t)))}dt+{(F(x(t))-F(y(t)))},dB_{t}\bm{}_{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\left[{2\bm{\left\langle}x(t)-y(t),v(y(t))dt+H(y(t))dB_{t}\bm{}_{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\left[{2\text{tr}{(F(x(t))-F(y(t)))}^{2}+2\text{tr}{(H(y(t)))}^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{(2L_{u}^{\prime}+2L_{F}^{\prime})}\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|x(t)-y(t)\right\|}_{2}^{2}}\right]+2\varepsilon_{u}\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|x(t)-y(t)\right\|}_{2}}\right]+2\varepsilon_{H}^{2}

For any
T
, we can apply Young’s inequality to get

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|x(t)-y(t)\right\|}_{2}^{2}}\right]\leq{(2L_{u}^{\prime}+2L_{F}^{\prime}+1)}\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|x(t)-y(t)\right\|}_{2}^{2}}\right]+\varepsilon_{u}^{2}+2\varepsilon_{H}^{2}

Integrating over
[0,T]
, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|x(T)-y(T)\right\|}_{2}^{2}}\right]\leq e^{T{(2L_{u}^{\prime}+2L_{F}^{\prime}+1)}}{({\left\|x(0)-y(0)\right\|}_{2}^{2}+8T{(\varepsilon_{u}^{2}+\varepsilon_{F}^{2})})}

∎

J.6
Main SGD Result

Lemma 88
.

Assume the same assumptions as Theorem
4
.

Let
\delta\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
, define the sequences

\displaystyle x_{k+1}=x_{k}+\delta\ell_{n}(x_{k})+\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x)

\displaystyle\tilde{x}_{k+1}=\tilde{x}_{k}+\delta\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\tilde{x}_{k})+\sqrt{\delta}\tilde{\xi}_{k}(\tilde{x}_{k})

Let
f,\alpha
be as defined in Theorem
1
. There exists constant
{\mathcal{C}}_{1}
, which depend polynomially on
L_{\beta},L_{\beta}^{\prime},L_{\xi},L_{\xi}^{\prime},L_{\xi}^{\prime\prime},L_{R},L_{R}^{\prime},\frac{1}{m},d,\mathcal{R},L_{\xi,2}^{\prime},L_{\beta,2}^{\prime}
, such that for any positive

T\leq\frac{1}{{\mathcal{C}}_{1}}\min\left\{1,\frac{1}{{\left\|x_{0}-x^{*}\right\|}_{2}^{2}+{\left\|\tilde{x}_{0}-\tilde{x}^{*}\right\|}_{2}^{2}}\right\}
and
\delta:={T}^{3}
and
K:=T/\delta
, there exists a coupling between
x
and
\tilde{x}
such that for
j=\lceil\frac{1}{32T{(1+2L_{A}^{\prime}/\sqrt{\lambda_{A}}+L_{\beta}^{\prime})}}\rceil
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{f{(\mathrm{d}{(x_{jK},\tilde{x}_{jK})})}}\right]\leq

\displaystyle\exp{(-\alpha jK\delta)}f{(\mathrm{d}{(x_{0},\tilde{x}_{0})})}

\displaystyle\quad+{O}{(j{(K\delta)}^{3/2}{(1+\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|x_{0}-x^{*}\right\|}_{2}^{3}+{\left\|\tilde{x}_{0}-\tilde{x}^{*}\right\|}_{2}^{3}}\right])}+\frac{{(jK\delta)}}{n})}

where
\tilde{O}
hides polynomial dependency on
L_{\beta},L_{\beta}^{\prime},L_{\xi},L_{\xi}^{\prime},L_{\xi}^{\prime\prime},L_{R},L_{R}^{\prime},d,\mathcal{R},L_{A},\frac{1}{\lambda_{A}},L_{\phi},L_{\phi}^{\prime},d,L_{\xi,2}^{\prime},L_{\beta,2}^{\prime},L_{\ell}
.

Proof of Lemma
88
.

Under existing assumption on
T
, we assume without loss of further generality that
K\delta=T\leq\frac{1}{32{(1+2L_{A}^{\prime}/\sqrt{\lambda_{A}}+L_{\beta}^{\prime})}}
. Thus
\frac{1}{32T{(1+2L_{A}^{\prime}/\sqrt{\lambda_{A}}+L_{\beta}^{\prime})}}\geq 1
, thus
jK\delta:=K\delta\lceil\frac{1}{32K\delta{(1+2L_{A}^{\prime}/\sqrt{\lambda_{A}}+L_{\beta}^{\prime})}}\rceil\leq 2
. We verify from definition of
j
that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{32{(1+2L_{A}^{\prime}/\sqrt{\lambda_{A}}+L_{\beta}^{\prime})}}\leq jK\delta\leq\frac{1}{16{(1+2L_{A}^{\prime}/\sqrt{\lambda_{A}}+L_{\beta}^{\prime})}}

(J.10)

This will be useful at several places later in this proof.

Let us define, for
t\in[0,jK\delta]
,

\displaystyle d{y}(t)=\beta({y}(t))dt+A({y}(t))^{1/2}d\mathbf{W}(t)

\displaystyle d\tilde{y}(t)=\tilde{\beta}(\tilde{y}(t))dt+\tilde{A}(\tilde{y}(t))^{1/2}d\tilde{\mathbf{W}}(t)

\displaystyle d\bar{y}(t)={\beta}(\bar{y}(t))dt+{A}(\bar{y}(t))^{1/2}d\bar{\mathbf{W}}(t)

where
\mathbf{W},\tilde{\mathbf{W}},\bar{\mathbf{W}}
are Brownian motions and where
y(0)=x_{0}
,
\tilde{y}(0)=\bar{y}(0)=\tilde{x}_{0}
. We can bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{f{(\mathrm{d}{(x_{jK},\tilde{x}_{jK})})}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{f{(\mathrm{d}{(y({jK}\delta),\bar{y}({jK}\delta))})}}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(y({jK}\delta),x_{jK})}}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(\bar{y}({jK}\delta),\tilde{x}_{jK})}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{f{(\mathrm{d}{(y({jK}\delta),\bar{y}({jK}\delta))})}}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(y({jK}\delta),x_{jK})}}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(\tilde{y}({jK}\delta),\tilde{x}_{jK})}}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(\bar{y}({jK}\delta),\tilde{y}({jK}\delta))}}\right]

(J.11)

By Lemma
84
,
y(t)\overset{d}{=}\Phi(K\delta;y(0),E,\beta^{g},{\mathbf{B}})
and
\bar{y}(t)\overset{d}{=}\Phi(K\delta;\bar{y}(0),\tilde{E},{\beta}^{g},\bar{\mathbf{B}})
. Therefore, by Lemma
3
, there exists a coupling between
y
and
\bar{y}
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{f{(\mathrm{d}{(y(jK\delta),\bar{y}(jK\delta))})}}\right]\leq

\displaystyle\exp{(-\alpha jK\delta)}f{(\mathrm{d}{(x_{0},\tilde{x}_{0})})}

Now consider a fixed
i\leq j
. Define
\hat{x}_{(i+1)K}:=\Phi(K\delta;x_{iK},E^{iK},\beta^{g},\hat{\mathbf{B}}^{iK})
where
E^{iK}
is an orthonormal basis at
T_{x_{iK}}M
and
\hat{\mathbf{B}}^{iK}
is a Brownian motion. From Lemma
84
and Lemma
85
,
\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|x_{(i+1)K}-\hat{x}_{(i+1)K}\right\|}_{2}}\right]\leq{O}{({(K\delta)}^{3/2}{(1+{\left\|x_{iK}-x^{*}\right\|}_{2}^{3})})}
. By Lemma
84
and Lemma
87
with
\varepsilon_{u}=\varepsilon_{F}=0
and
L_{u}^{\prime}=L_{\beta}^{\prime}
and
L_{F}^{\prime}=L_{A}^{\prime}/\sqrt{\lambda_{A}}
, we can bound

\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|\hat{x}_{(i+1)K}-y((i+1)K\delta)\right\|}_{2}}\right]\leq\exp{(K\delta{(1+2L_{A}^{\prime}/\sqrt{\lambda_{A}}+L_{\beta}^{\prime})})}\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|x_{iK}-y(iK\delta)\right\|}_{2}}\right]
. Therefore, by triangle inequality,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|x_{(i+1)K}-y((i+1)K\delta)\right\|}_{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp{(K\delta{(1+2L_{A}^{\prime}/\sqrt{\lambda_{A}}+L_{\beta}^{\prime})})}\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|x_{iK}-y(iK\delta)\right\|}_{2}}\right]+{O}{({(K\delta)}^{3/2}{(1+{\left\|x_{iK}-x^{*}\right\|}_{2}^{3})})}

Applying the above recursively for
i=0...j-1
, and noting that from (
J.10
),
jK\delta{(1+2L_{A}^{\prime}/\sqrt{\lambda_{A}}+L_{\beta}^{\prime})}\leq 1/16
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|x_{jK}-y(jK\delta)\right\|}_{2}}\right]\leq

\displaystyle 8\sum_{i=0}^{j-1}{O}{({(K\delta)}^{3/2}{(1+\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|x_{iK}-x^{*}\right\|}_{2}^{3}}\right])})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{O}{(j{(K\delta)}^{3/2}{(1+\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|x_{0}-x^{*}\right\|}_{2}^{3}}\right])})}

where the last inequality is because
\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|x_{iK}-x^{*}\right\|}_{2}^{3}}\right]\leq O{({\left\|x_{0}-x^{*}\right\|}_{2}^{3})}
by Lemma
90
, and our assumption that
jK\delta\leq\frac{1}{32L_{\beta}^{\prime}}
from (
J.10
).

By an identical sequence of steps, we can also show that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|\tilde{x}_{jK}-\tilde{y}(jK\delta)\right\|}_{2}}\right]\leq{O}{(j{(K\delta)}^{3/2}{(1+\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|\tilde{x}_{0}-\tilde{x}^{*}\right\|}_{2}^{3}}\right])})}

Finally, by Lemma
86
, we can apply Lemma
87
with
\varepsilon_{u}=\frac{2L_{\ell}}{n}
,
\varepsilon_{F}=\frac{16\sqrt{d}L_{\ell}^{2}}{\sqrt{\lambda_{A}}n}
and
L_{u}^{\prime}=L_{\beta}^{\prime}
and
L_{F}^{\prime}=L_{A}^{\prime}/\sqrt{\lambda_{A}}
, so that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|\bar{y}(jK\delta)-\tilde{y}(jK\delta)\right\|}_{2}^{2}}\right]\leq

\displaystyle e^{jK\delta{(2L_{u}^{\prime}+2L_{F}^{\prime}+1)}}{({\left\|x(0)-y(0)\right\|}_{2}^{2}+8jK\delta{(\varepsilon_{u}^{2}+\varepsilon_{F}^{2})})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O{(\frac{jK\delta}{n^{2}})}=O{(\frac{{(jK\delta)}^{2}}{n^{2}})}

The last line is because by (
J.10
),
\frac{1}{jK\delta}\leq 32{(1+2L_{A}^{\prime}/\sqrt{\lambda_{A}}+L_{\beta}^{\prime})}
. Note: we picked
j
to ensure that, while
K\delta
is small,
jK\delta
is large –
O(1)
in terms of various parameters.

Plugging everything into (
J.11
), (applying Jensen’s inequality to
\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|\bar{y}(jK\delta)-\tilde{y}(jK\delta)\right\|}_{2}^{2}}\right]
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{f{(\mathrm{d}{(x_{jK},\tilde{x}_{jK})})}}\right]\leq

\displaystyle\exp{(-\alpha TjK\delta)}f{(\mathrm{d}{(x_{0},\tilde{x}_{0})})}

\displaystyle\quad+{O}{(j{(K\delta)}^{3/2}{(1+\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|x_{0}-x^{*}\right\|}_{2}^{3}+{\left\|\tilde{x}_{0}-\tilde{x}^{*}\right\|}_{2}^{3}}\right])})}+O{(\frac{{(jK\delta)}}{n})}

This concludes the proof.
∎

Theorem 4
.

Let
\ell(x,s):\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathcal{S}\to\mathbb{R}
denote a loss function. Let
\ell_{n},\tilde{\ell}_{n},A_{n},\tilde{A}_{n}
be as defined in (
J.7
), (
J.8
), (
J.9
). Assume
\ell
satisfies Assumption
13
. Assume exists
L_{\ell}^{\prime}
such that for all
x,y,s
,
{\left\|{\bm{\nabla}}\ell(x,s)-{\bm{\nabla}}\ell(y,s)\right\|}_{2}\leq L_{\ell}^{\prime}{\left\|x-y\right\|}_{2}
. Let
\xi
be a random vector field satisfying Assumptions
12
and
11
wrt
A
and
\tilde{\xi}
be a random vector field satisfying Assumptions
12
and
11
wrt
\tilde{A}
. Let
M={(\mathbb{R}^{d},g)}
and
\tilde{M}={(\mathbb{R}^{d},\tilde{g})}
, and assume both
M
and
\tilde{M}
satisfy Assumptions
3
,
4
and
5
. Let
\phi
be as defined in (
J.3
) wrt
g
and analogously
\tilde{\phi}
be defined wrt
\tilde{g}
. Assume
\phi
and
\tilde{\phi}
satisfy Assumption
10
. Let
\beta:=\ell_{n}
and
\tilde{\beta}:=\tilde{\ell}_{n}
. Assume
\beta,\tilde{\beta}
satisfy Assumption
14
. Let
\beta^{g}:=\beta+\frac{1}{2}\phi
and
\tilde{\beta}^{g}:=\tilde{\beta}+\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\phi}
, and assume
{\beta}^{g},\tilde{\beta}^{g}
satisfy Assumptions
1
and
2
. Finally, assume there exist points
x^{*}
and
\tilde{x}^{*}
such that
\ell_{n}(x^{*})=0
and
\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\tilde{x}^{*})=0
. Let
x_{0}
be some point satisfying
{\left\|x_{0}-x^{*}\right\|}_{2}\leq\mathcal{R}_{2}
and let
\tilde{x}_{0}
be some point satisfying
{\left\|\tilde{x}_{0}-\tilde{x}^{*}\right\|}_{2}\leq\mathcal{R}_{2}
.

Let
\delta>0
be a stepsize, let
x_{0}=\tilde{x}_{0}
, and define the processes

\displaystyle x_{k+1}=x_{k}+\delta\ell_{n}(x_{k})+\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x)

\displaystyle\tilde{x}_{k+1}=\tilde{x}_{k}+\delta\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\tilde{x}_{k})+\sqrt{\delta}\tilde{\xi}_{k}(\tilde{x}_{k})

Let
f,\alpha
be as defined in Theorem
1
. There exists constant
{\mathcal{C}}_{3}
, which depends polynomially on

L_{\beta},L_{\beta}^{\prime},L_{\xi},L_{\xi}^{\prime},L_{\xi}^{\prime\prime},L_{R},L_{R}^{\prime},\frac{1}{m},d,\mathcal{R},L_{\xi,2}^{\prime},L_{\beta,2}^{\prime}
, such that for any positive

T\leq\frac{1}{{\mathcal{C}}_{3}}
and
\delta:={T}^{3}
and
K:=T/\delta
, there exists a coupling between
x
and
\tilde{x}
such that for
j=\lceil\frac{1}{32T{(1+2L_{A}^{\prime}/\sqrt{\lambda_{A}}+L_{\beta}^{\prime})}}\rceil
and for any
i
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|x_{ijK}-\tilde{x}_{ijK}\right\|}_{2}^{2}}\right]\leq\min\left\{ijK\delta,\frac{1}{\alpha}\right\}\cdot O{({(K\delta)}^{1/2}+\frac{1}{n})}

where
{O}
hides polynomial dependency on
L_{\beta},L_{\beta}^{\prime},L_{\xi},L_{\xi}^{\prime},L_{\xi}^{\prime\prime},L_{R},L_{R}^{\prime},d,\mathcal{R},L_{A},\frac{1}{\lambda_{A}},L_{\phi},L_{\phi}^{\prime},d,L_{\xi,2}^{\prime},L_{\beta,2}^{\prime},L_{\ell}
.

Proof.

Under Assumption
13
and our assumption on
\lambda_{A}
, we verify that both
A(x)
and
\tilde{A}(x)
satisfy Assumption
8
with
L_{A}=4L_{\ell}^{2}
. Our assumption on
L_{\ell^{\prime}}
implies Assumption
9
holds with
L_{A}^{\prime}\leq 4L_{\ell}^{2}{L_{\ell}^{\prime}}^{2}
. Note that
\beta
and
\tilde{\beta}
satisfy Assumption
12
with
L_{\beta,2}^{\prime}\leq L_{\ell}^{\prime}
, and Assumption
2
with
L_{\beta}^{\prime}\leq L_{A}L_{\ell}^{\prime}
.

Let
{s}
denote a radius and let
V_{k}
denote the event that
\sup_{i\leq k}\max\left\{{\left\|x_{k}-x_{2}^{*}\right\|}_{2},{\left\|\tilde{x}_{k}-\tilde{x}_{2}^{*}\right\|}_{2}\right\}\geq{s}
. Recall that
x_{2}^{*}
is by definition a point with
\ell_{n}(x_{2}^{*})=0
and similarly
\tilde{x}_{2}^{*}
is a point with
\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\tilde{x}_{2}^{*})=0
. From Lemma
91
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{i\leq k}{\left\|x_{K}-x^{*}_{2}\right\|}_{2}^{2}\geq{s}^{2}})}\leq

\displaystyle\exp{(\frac{m_{2}}{64L_{\xi,2}^{2}}{(2\mathcal{R}_{2}^{2}+\frac{L_{\beta,2}^{\prime}\mathcal{R}_{2}^{2}}{m_{2}}-{s}^{2})})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp{(\frac{m_{2}}{64L_{\xi,2}^{2}}{(\frac{4L_{\beta,2}^{\prime}\mathcal{R}_{2}^{2}}{m_{2}}-{s}^{2})})}

We get the same bound for
\mathbb{P}{({\max_{i\leq k}{\left\|\tilde{x}_{K}-\tilde{x}^{*}_{2}\right\|}_{2}^{2}\geq{s}^{2}})}
. Together with the second-moment bound from lemma
91
and triangle inequality, we verify that for all
k
,
\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{V_{k}^{c}\right\}}{({\left\|x_{k}-\tilde{x}_{k}\right\|}_{2})}}\right]\leq 2\exp{(\frac{m_{2}}{128L_{\xi,2}^{2}}{(\frac{4L_{\beta,2}^{\prime}\mathcal{R}_{2}^{2}}{m_{2}}-{s}^{2})})}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{L_{\beta,2}^{\prime}\mathcal{R}_{2}^{2}}{m}+\frac{2{L_{\xi,2}}^{2}}{m}}
. We verify that there is a constant
{\mathcal{C}}_{2}=poly(L_{\beta,2}^{\prime},\mathcal{R}_{2},\frac{1}{{m_{2}}},L_{\xi,2})
, such that for any
T
,
{s}={\mathcal{C}}_{2}\log{(1/T)}
gives
\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{V_{k}^{c}\right\}}{({\left\|x_{k}-\tilde{x}_{k}\right\|}_{2})}}\right]\leq{(T)}^{3/2}
.

Consider an arbitrary step
k
, and condition on the event
V_{k}
. Let
{\mathcal{C}}_{1}
be the constant from Lemma
88
. We verify using Lemma
17
that any
T\leq\frac{1}{32{\mathcal{C}}_{1}{\mathcal{C}}_{2}^{2}\log{(1+{\mathcal{C}}_{1}{\mathcal{C}}_{2}^{2})}}
implies that
T\leq\frac{1}{{\mathcal{C}}_{1}{\mathcal{C}}_{2}^{2}\log{(T)}^{2}}=\frac{1}{{\mathcal{C}}_{1}{s}^{2}}
. Therefore, there exists a
{\mathcal{C}}_{3}
such that, under the event
V_{k}
,
T=\frac{1}{{\mathcal{C}}_{3}}
satisfies the requirement of Lemma
88
, which then implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{V_{k+jK}\right\}}f{(\mathrm{d}{(x_{k+jK},\tilde{x}_{k+jK})})}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{V_{k}\right\}}f{(\mathrm{d}{(x_{k+jK},\tilde{x}_{k+jK})})}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\mathbbm{1}\left\{V_{k}\right\}}\exp{(-\alpha jK\delta)}f{(\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},\tilde{x}_{k})})}

\displaystyle\quad+{O}{(j{(K\delta)}^{3/2}{(1+\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{V_{k}\right\}}{({\left\|x_{k}-x^{*}\right\|}_{2}^{3}+{\left\|\tilde{x}_{k}-\tilde{x}^{*}\right\|}_{2}^{3})}}\right])}+\frac{{(jK\delta)}}{n})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathbbm{1}\left\{V_{k}\right\}}\exp{(-\alpha jK\delta)}f{(\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},\tilde{x}_{k})})}+{O}{(j{(K\delta)}^{3/2}+\frac{{(jK\delta)}}{n})}

(J.12)

where the expectation is conditional on randomness up to step
k
, and where
j:=\lceil\frac{1}{32T{(1+2L_{A}^{\prime}/\sqrt{\lambda_{A}}+L_{\beta}^{\prime})}}\rceil
is as defined in Lemma
88
.

The last line follows from the definition of
V_{k}
, as well as the fact that
{\left\|x_{2}^{*}-x^{*}\right\|}_{2}+{\left\|\tilde{x}_{2}^{*}-\tilde{x}^{*}\right\|}_{2}=O(1)
, which we verify presently: Assumption
10
implies
{\left\|\beta(x^{*})\right\|}_{2}\leq L_{\phi}
. If
{\left\|x^{*}-x_{2}^{*}\right\|}_{2}\geq\mathcal{R}_{2}
, then we are done. So suppose otherwise. Assumption
14
implies that
L_{\phi}{\left\|x^{*}-x_{2}^{*}\right\|}_{2}\geq\bm{\left\langle}\frac{1}{2}\phi(x^{*}),x^{*}-x_{2}^{*}\bm{}_{2}\geq m_{2}{\left\|x^{*}-x_{2}^{*}\right\|}_{2}^{2}
, so that
{\left\|x^{*}-x_{2}^{*}\right\|}_{2}\leq\frac{L_{\phi}}{m_{2}}
. By identical argument,
V_{k}
also implies
{\left\|\tilde{x}_{2}^{*}-\tilde{x}^{*}\right\|}_{2}\leq\mathcal{R}_{2}+\frac{L_{\phi}}{m_{2}}
.

Applying (
J.12
) recursively over
k
being a integral multiple of
jK
, we get that for any
i
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{V_{ijK}\right\}}f{(\mathrm{d}{(x_{ijK},\tilde{x}_{ijK})})}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp{(-\alpha ijK\delta)}\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{V_{0}\right\}}f{(\mathrm{d}{(x_{0},\tilde{x}_{0})})}}\right]+{O}{(\sum_{r=0}^{i-1}\exp{(-\alpha rjK\delta)}{(j{(K\delta)}^{3/2}+\frac{{(jK\delta)}}{n})})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O{(\frac{1-\exp{(-\alpha rjK\delta)}}{\alpha}\cdot{({(K\delta)}^{1/2}+\frac{1}{n})})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min\left\{ijK\delta,\frac{1}{\alpha}\right\}\cdot O{({(K\delta)}^{1/2}+\frac{1}{n})}

Finally, recall our earlier bound that
\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{V_{k}^{c}\right\}}{({\left\|x_{k}-\tilde{x}_{k}\right\|}_{2})}}\right]\leq{(K\delta)}^{3/2}=\min\left\{ijK\delta,\frac{1}{\alpha}\right\}\cdot O{({(K\delta)}^{1/2}+\frac{1}{n})}
.

Summing these two bounds, and using the fact that
r\leq O{(\frac{1}{\alpha})}f(r)
gives our conclusion.

∎

J.7
Stability and generalization

Lemma 89
.

For a given geodesically
L-
lipschitz function
\ell
, any n samples
s_{1},s_{2},...,s_{n}
(distribution free), and two distributions of parameters
x_{0}\sim\pi_{0},x_{1}\sim\pi_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{x_{0}\sim\pi_{0},x_{1}\sim\pi_{1}}\left[\tfrac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell(s_{i},x_{0})-\ell(s_{i},x_{1})\right]\leq LW_{1}{(x_{0},x_{1})}.

Proof.

Let
\pi
be a coupling between
\pi_{0}
and
\pi_{1}
. In other word
\int\pi(x,y)dy=\pi_{0}(x),\int\pi(x,y)dx=\pi_{1}(y)
. Then for any such coupling we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{x_{0}\sim\pi_{0},x_{1}\sim\pi_{1}}\left[\tfrac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{(\ell(s_{i},x_{0})-\ell(s_{i},x_{1}))}\right]

\displaystyle=\inf_{\pi}\mathbb{E}_{(x_{0},x_{1})\sim\pi}\left[\tfrac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{(\ell(s_{i},x_{0})-\ell(s_{i},x_{1}))}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\inf_{\pi}\mathbb{E}_{(x_{0},x_{1})\sim\pi}\left[\tfrac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}L\mathrm{d}(x_{0},x_{1})\right]

\displaystyle=LW_{1}(x_{0},x_{1})

∎

Proof for Lemma
6

Proof.

Denote
\mathcal{A}(S)
as the algorithm output based on dataset
S
. Denote
S^{\prime}=\{s_{1}^{\prime},...,s_{n}^{\prime}\}
,
S^{i}=\{s_{1},...,s_{i-1},s_{i}^{\prime},s_{i+1},s_{n}\}
, where
s_{i},s_{i}^{\prime}
are independently sampled from the population distribution. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{S,S^{\prime}\mathcal{A}}\left[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell(s_{i}^{\prime},\mathcal{A}(S))-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell(s_{i},\mathcal{A}(S))\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{S,\mathcal{A}}\left[\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}}\left[\ell(s^{\prime},\mathcal{A}(S))\right]-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell(s_{i},\mathcal{A}(S))\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{S,s_{i}^{\prime},\mathcal{A}}\left[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell(s_{i}^{\prime},\mathcal{A}(S))-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell(s_{i},\mathcal{A}(S))\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{S,s_{i}^{\prime},\mathcal{A}}\left[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{(\ell(s_{i}^{\prime},\mathcal{A}(S))-\ell(s_{i},\mathcal{A}(S)))}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{S,s_{i}^{\prime},\mathcal{A}}\left[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{(\ell(s_{i},\mathcal{A}(S^{i}))-\ell(s_{i},\mathcal{A}(S)))}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{S,s_{i}^{\prime},\mathcal{A}}\left[LW_{1}(\mathcal{A}(S^{i}),\mathcal{A}(S))\right]

The first line uses linearity of expectation. The third line is by the fact that
\mathcal{A}(S)
is exchangeable in
x_{i}
and hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{S,s_{i}^{\prime},\mathcal{A}}\left[\ell(s_{i},\mathcal{A}(S^{i}))\right]=\mathbb{E}_{S,s_{i}^{\prime},\mathcal{A}}\left[\ell(s_{i}^{\prime},\mathcal{A}(S))\right].

The fourth line follows by Lemma
89
. Then the statement follows by upper-bounding
W_{1}(\mathcal{A}(S^{i}),\mathcal{A}(S))
by Theorem
4
.
∎

J.8
Euclidean Tail Bounds

Assumption 14

(
L_{2}
one-point dissipativity)
.

There exists a
x^{*}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that
\beta(x^{*}_{2})=0
. There exist constants
m_{2},\mathcal{R}_{2}
and for all
x:{\left\|x-x^{*}_{2}\right\|}_{2}\geq\mathcal{R}_{2}
,

\displaystyle\bm{\left\langle}\beta(x),x-x_{2}^{*}\bm{}_{2}\leq-m_{2}{\left\|x-x_{2}^{*}\right\|}_{2}^{2}

Lemma 90
.

Let
\beta
satisfy Assumption
12
. Let

\displaystyle x_{k+1}=x_{k}+\beta(x_{k})+\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k})

with arbitrary
x_{0}
. Assume and
K\delta\leq 1/(32L_{\beta,2}^{\prime})
. Then for all
k
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|x_{k}-x_{0}\right\|}_{2}^{8}}\right]\leq{2^{10}K^{4}\delta^{8}{\left\|\beta(x_{0})\right\|}_{2}^{8}}+2^{10}K^{4}\delta^{4}L_{\xi,2}^{8}

Proof.

\displaystyle{\left\|x_{k+1}-x_{0}\right\|}_{2}^{2}=

\displaystyle{\left\|x_{k}-x_{0}\right\|}_{2}^{2}+2\bm{\left\langle}x_{k}-x_{0},\beta(x_{k})+\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k})\bm{}_{2}+{\left\|x_{k+1}-x_{0}\right\|}_{2}^{2}

Notice that

\displaystyle\bm{\left\langle}x_{k}-x_{0},\beta(x_{k})\bm{}_{2}\leq

\displaystyle 2\delta L_{\beta,2}^{\prime}{\left\|x_{k}-x_{0}\right\|}_{2}^{2}+\frac{\delta}{L_{\beta,2}^{\prime}}{\left\|\beta(x_{0})\right\|}_{2}^{2}

and
{\left\|x_{k+1}-x_{0}\right\|}_{2}^{2}\leq 4\delta^{2}{L_{\beta,2}^{\prime}}^{2}{\left\|x_{k+1}-x_{0}\right\|}_{2}^{2}+4\delta^{2}{\left\|\beta(x_{0})\right\|}_{2}^{2}+4\delta L_{\xi,2}^{2}\leq\delta L_{\beta,2}^{\prime}{\left\|x_{k+1}-x_{0}\right\|}_{2}^{2}+4\delta^{2}{\left\|\beta(x_{0})\right\|}_{2}^{2}+4\delta L_{\xi,2}^{2}\leq
by assumption that
\delta\leq 1/(16L_{\beta,2}^{\prime})
. Put together,

\displaystyle{\left\|x_{k+1}-x_{0}\right\|}_{2}^{2}\leq{(1+2\delta L_{\beta,2}^{\prime})}{\left\|x_{k}-x_{0}\right\|}_{2}^{2}+\delta^{2}{\left\|\beta(x_{0})\right\|}_{2}^{2}+2\delta L_{\xi,2}^{2}+2\bm{\left\langle}x_{k}-x_{0},\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k})\bm{}_{2}

squaring both sides, applying Young’s inequality, then squaring both sides again and applying Young’s inequality again,

\displaystyle{\left\|x_{k+1}-x_{0}\right\|}_{2}^{8}\leq

\displaystyle{(1+16\delta L_{\beta,2}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{K})}{\left\|x_{k}-x_{0}\right\|}_{2}^{8}+{2^{8}K^{3}\delta^{8}{\left\|\beta(x_{0})\right\|}_{2}^{8}}+2^{8}K^{3}\delta^{4}L_{\xi,2}^{8}+\hbox to12.21pt{\vbox to12.21pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 6.10283pt\lower-6.10283pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 8.17 0 C 8.17 4.51 4.51 8.17 0 8.17 C -4.51 8.17 -8.17 4.51 -8.17 0 C -8.17 -4.51 -4.51 -8.17 0 -8.17 C 4.51 -8.17 8.17 -4.51 8.17 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.5pt}{-3.75pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}

where
*
has 0-mean conditioned on
x_{k}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|x_{k+1}-x_{0}\right\|}_{2}^{8}}\right]\leq{(1+16\delta L_{\beta,2}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{K})}\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|x_{k}-x_{0}\right\|}_{2}^{8}}\right]+{2^{8}K^{3}\delta^{8}{\left\|\beta(x_{0})\right\|}_{2}^{8}}+2^{8}K^{3}\delta^{4}L_{\xi,2}^{8}

Applying the above recursively, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|x_{k}-x_{0}\right\|}_{2}^{8}}\right]\leq

\displaystyle{2^{10}K^{4}\delta^{8}{\left\|\beta(x_{0})\right\|}_{2}^{8}}+2^{10}K^{4}\delta^{4}L_{\xi,2}^{8}

∎

Lemma 91
.

Let

\displaystyle x_{k+1}=x_{k}+\beta(x_{k})+\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k})

Assume Assumption
14
, Assumption
12
, and
\delta\leq m_{2}/(16{L_{\beta,2}^{\prime}}^{2})
. Then for all
K
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|x_{K}-x^{*}_{2}\right\|}_{2}^{2}}\right]\leq\frac{L_{\beta,2}^{\prime}\mathcal{R}_{2}^{2}}{m}+\frac{2{L_{\xi,2}}^{2}}{m}

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{k\leq K}{\left\|x_{K}-x^{*}_{2}\right\|}_{2}^{2}\geq t^{2}})}\leq\exp{(\frac{m_{2}}{64L_{\xi,2}^{2}}{(2{\left\|x_{0}-x^{*}_{2}\right\|}_{2}^{2}+\frac{L_{\beta,2}^{\prime}\mathcal{R}_{2}^{2}}{m_{2}}-t^{2})})}

Proof.

\displaystyle{\left\|x_{k+1}-x^{*}_{2}\right\|}_{2}^{2}=

\displaystyle{\left\|x_{k}-x^{*}_{2}\right\|}_{2}^{2}+2\bm{\left\langle}x_{k}-x^{*}_{2},\beta(x_{k})+\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k})\bm{}_{2}+{\left\|x_{k+1}-x^{*}_{2}\right\|}_{2}^{2}

Notice that

\displaystyle\bm{\left\langle}x_{k}-x^{*}_{2},\beta(x_{k})\bm{}_{2}\leq

\displaystyle{\mathbbm{1}\left\{{\left\|x_{k}-x^{*}_{2}\right\|}_{2}\geq\mathcal{R}_{2}\right\}}{(-\delta m_{2})}{\left\|x_{k}-x^{*}_{2}\right\|}_{2}+{\mathbbm{1}\left\{{\left\|x_{k}-x^{*}_{2}\right\|}_{2}\leq\mathcal{R}_{2}\right\}}{(\delta L_{\beta,2}^{\prime})}{\left\|x_{k}-x^{*}_{2}\right\|}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\delta m_{2}{\left\|x_{k}-x^{*}_{2}\right\|}_{2}+2\delta L_{\beta,2}^{\prime}\mathcal{R}_{2}^{2}

and
{\left\|x_{k+1}-x^{*}_{2}\right\|}_{2}^{2}\leq 2\delta^{2}{L_{\beta,2}^{\prime}}^{2}{\left\|x_{k+1}-x^{*}_{2}\right\|}_{2}^{2}+2\delta L_{\xi,2}^{2}\leq\delta m_{2}/2{\left\|x_{k+1}-x^{*}_{2}\right\|}_{2}^{2}+2\delta L_{\xi,2}^{2}\leq
by assumption that
\delta\leq m_{2}/(16{L_{\beta,2}^{\prime}}^{2})
. Put together,

\displaystyle{\left\|x_{k+1}-x^{*}_{2}\right\|}_{2}^{2}\leq{(1-\delta m_{2}/2)}{\left\|x_{k}-x^{*}_{2}\right\|}_{2}^{2}+\delta L_{\beta,2}^{\prime}\mathcal{R}_{2}^{2}+2\delta L_{\xi,2}^{2}+2\bm{\left\langle}x_{k}-x^{*}_{2},\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k})\bm{}_{2}

Applying the above recursively gives us our first result.

We now apply Lemma
39
with

\displaystyle q_{k}=\frac{m_{2}}{32L_{\xi,2}^{2}}{\left\|x_{k+1}-x^{*}_{2}\right\|}_{2}^{2}\qquad\eta_{k}=\frac{m_{2}}{32L_{\xi,2}^{2}}\cdot 2\bm{\left\langle}x_{k}-x^{*}_{2},\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k})\bm{}_{2}

\displaystyle a=-\delta m_{2}/2\qquad b=\frac{m_{2}}{32L_{\xi,2}^{2}}{(\delta L_{\beta,2}^{\prime}\mathcal{R}_{2}^{2}+2\delta L_{\xi,2}^{2})}\qquad c=\frac{\delta m_{2}}{4}\qquad d=0

We verify that
a+c<0
, so that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{k\leq K}q_{k}\geq t^{2}})}\leq\exp{(\exp{(K(a+c))}q_{0}+\frac{b+d}{{\left|a+c\right|}}-\frac{t^{2}}{2})}

Plugging in the terms,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{k\leq K}\frac{m_{2}}{32L_{\xi,2}^{2}}{\left\|x_{K}-x^{*}_{2}\right\|}_{2}^{2}\geq t^{2}})}\leq\exp{(\frac{m_{2}}{32L_{\xi,2}^{2}}{\left\|x_{0}-x^{*}_{2}\right\|}_{2}^{2}+\frac{L_{\beta,2}^{\prime}\mathcal{R}_{2}^{2}}{32{L_{\xi,2}^{\prime}}^{2}}-t^{2}/2)}

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\qquad

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{k\leq K}{\left\|x_{K}-x^{*}_{2}\right\|}_{2}^{2}\geq t^{2}})}\leq\exp{(\frac{m_{2}}{64L_{\xi,2}^{2}}{(2{\left\|x_{0}-x^{*}_{2}\right\|}_{2}^{2}+\frac{L_{\beta,2}^{\prime}\mathcal{R}_{2}^{2}}{m_{2}}-t^{2})})}

∎
